| **Bilingual UI (Hebrew RTL default / English LTR)** | **all** | In progress | **Cross-cutting — constrains every row below, and cannot be cut.** Static screens use `.he-text`/`.en-text` duplication + a CSS-only toggle; becomes a React resource file + Context (D10). Every new screen ships bilingual and is checked in both directions. See [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) |
| Language preference saved to the account | 2 | Planned | `users.language`; `localStorage` (`screens/lang.ts`) stays the pre-login default only |
| **Landing** | 1–2 | In progress | Approved screen **#1**, and the first **public, signed-out** screen the project has built that is not an auth form. Static HTML/CSS on `feature/landing-page` (`screens/landing.html` + `screens/landing.css`, `d110e6f`) on a filename nobody had reserved — `landing.html` was chosen over `index.html` to keep the one-file-per-approved-screen naming the other screens use, and `index.html` is preserved as the alternative. **It carries its own `.site-nav`, not the `.app-nav`.** A signed-out visitor has no notifications and no account, so the bell and the profile chip are **absent, not emptied** — the same reasoning the 404 screen used for dropping the app navbar, and the reason this screen is **not** an eighth copy of it. Five items instead of eight also puts it clear of the 620–860px band [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) records the app navbar as overflowing in; its links take their own row from **760px** down. **The hero sits beside the dependency visual**, which is the screen's main communication and is deliberately given more weight than the three value blocks below it. **The dependency visual is an explicit placeholder**, because approved screen **#13** (project dashboard) does not exist and there is no real screenshot to show: it is a four-row working-day chart built from **CSS grid rather than one SVG**, so grid columns follow the inline direction and the whole chart mirrors itself instead of being a flipped English picture. It shows a gate task (piling), two tasks in sequence behind it and a **second trade depending on the same predecessor**, so the chain is never rendered as a single file and never as unrelated tasks. Original-versus-shifted is carried by **a dashed bar, a solid bar, a legend and a written sentence per row** — four signals, none of them colour. Its caption states in both languages that it is example data, not a product screenshot, that it is one example project, and that a real dashboard screenshot replaces it later. **The ruler counts working days, never weeks**, so nothing on it can be read as a FieldSync working week — the approved prohibition in [§11](#11-decision-log) applied to a screen that had no obvious reason to trip over it. **Copy is reused rather than invented wherever it existed:** the three value blocks restate `login.html`'s brand-panel strings and §1's own wording for equal users, confidential delegation and non-self-reported trust. **No price, plan name or quota appears anywhere** — Plans is a link, and `subscriptions.html` stays the single source for what plans say. **Zero new JavaScript**; the tracked `lang.js` is the only script, and the navbar language pill drives it through the same two radios every other screen uses. Footer is **Plans · Help & support · Contact**, by owner instruction, and **the language pill is deliberately not repeated there**. Two filenames reserved here: `help.html` and `contact.html`. One decision opened: **[D25](#7-open-decisions)** — whether Terms and Privacy screens should exist at all |
| Register | 1–2 | In progress | Register screen HTML/CSS, a luxury blueprint-board prototype, **now on `develop` at `Frontend/screens/register.html` + `register.css`** — merged 2026-08-27 (`3797fe0`), the first time either file existed outside `feature/register-screen`. **2026-08-19:** specialty list extended to match the profile screens, field-focus movement removed per [§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen), and an **initial availability** selection added to signup. **2026-08-26 (`c402429`, `42b8a27`):** two **independently optional** business phone fields — `טלפון משרד` / *Office phone* and `טלפון עסקי` / *Business phone* — added as a two-column row, deliberately kept as two concepts with no fallback between them and no `pattern`, so no new validation rule was introduced. **Neither is the personal/login phone:** `users.phone` is collected in onboarding step 3 and has never appeared on this screen, so nothing was repurposed. A bilingual QA pass over the same screen fixed a Hebrew placeholder that rendered in English mode, two English-only `aria-label`s and three copy mismatches; the trade list, the availability wording and the brand-panel bullets were **left alone as approved terminology** and the remaining mismatches are listed in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks). Requested as *"the approved D15 changes"*, which is a **label collision** — [D15](#7-open-decisions) is phone visibility on the public profile and is Open; see [§11](#11-decision-log). One decision opened: **[D27](#7-open-decisions)** — whether Register should collect these at all, and how they relate to `users.phone`. **2026-08-27 (`15acaa3`, `d47b2ce`, `048d883`, `5a978e5`, `70d5aca`):** a **required Company name** was added, opening **[D28](#7-open-decisions)** — `company` exists nowhere in the data model, and unlike D27's optional phones this field **blocks account creation** for a value the schema cannot store. The screen adopted login's corrected bilingual mechanism (`c6d53c5`), closing two [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) defects and exposing a third: English had always computed Heebo instead of Inter. The delegation bullet came out of the brand panel, making this the **third** screen swept under [§3.3](#33-hard-product-constraints) constraint 5, and the availability wording was aligned **verbatim** with browse-contractors so signup and the directory cannot disagree. The form was rebuilt as a **12-column grid with a container query on the card**, taking the page from 1585px to **1211px** at 1440×900; a three-across identity row was tried and **measured out again the same day**. **No human has viewed the result** — every check is a computed value from headless Edge. **2026-08-28: the backend for this screen exists** (`feature/register-screen.backend`) and it closes the storage half of [D27](#7-open-decisions) and [D28](#7-open-decisions) — Company name becomes a [[companies]] document the registrant owns, the two phones land on two different documents, and availability becomes the business's. **The screen now owes three contract changes**, recorded and deliberately not made from the backend branch: one `fullname` box becomes `firstName` + `lastName`; one free-text `location` box becomes `city` + a `region` **code** select, closing the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) region defect at the source; and `trade-other` is renamed `specialtyOther` to match the name `edit-profile.html` already uses. **2026-08-28, second pass — approved product rules reshaped what Register writes, though the request contract did not change.** Public Register is explicitly the onboarding flow for an **independent contractor / supplier / company owner**, and it now commits three documents in one transaction. The four `company*` fields came **off** [[users]] into a [[companymemberships]] collection, because an owner will later invite an employee **before that employee has an account** and an invitation with no user cannot be a field on a user. `companyPosition` and `permissions` are separate concepts and neither derives from the other. **None of the employee flow is built** — invitation, employee self-registration and owner approval are documented so a future endpoint cannot contradict the model. Opened **[D29](#7-open-decisions)** on the naming those rules imply, **closed the same day**: `pending_company_approval`, `standing: owner|employee` and **four** permission codes approved — one per already-approved owner default capability, granted to an owner at signup and to an employee never. The list is **closed at four**, enforced by the compiler, so no code can be added ahead of the capability it names. **[D28](#7-open-decisions) also closed 2026-08-28**: Company name is **required** at owner registration — which this endpoint's JOI schema already enforced, so nothing changed — and is **public and searchable by users**, scoped to that field alone. **[D25](#7-open-decisions) and [D27](#7-open-decisions) closed 2026-08-28 too**: consent is now **persisted** as `users.termsAcceptances[]` (version + timestamp, never a bare boolean), and both business phones are confirmed as belonging in Register — independent, optional, on two different documents, no fallback. **2026-08-28, third pass (`7c1e7be`, `feature/register-backend-fixes`): the three owed contract changes were made, and the screen now speaks the endpoint's vocabulary.** All three landed — the name split, `city` + a `region` **code** select (closing the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) region defect on the screen, where the contract had already closed it at the API), and `trade-other` → `specialtyOther`. Six more fields were renamed to the request schema and the six length bounds the endpoint enforces were added; the 23 trade codes and the three availability values already matched and were left alone. Both phones stay **independently optional with no fallback**, and `users.phone` is still asked for by neither side. Splitting one identity field into two costs one grid cell, so First and Last take a half each and Company name moved to a full-width row — every row from Email down keeps its exact prior pairing, and Region joined the existing field stack rather than adding a cell. Card height 1131px → 1321px. Verified by live-DOM measurement over the DevTools protocol at 9 widths × 2 languages × before/after: zero overflow, direction flips, the single-column drop lands at the same width, and the submit gating unlocks only after Region and re-locks when it is cleared — using the existing CSS with **no new rule**. **The screen still has no submit handler**: wiring it is the React work, and where it goes afterwards is [D30](#7-open-decisions). **2026-08-28, fourth pass — Register became the first React screen (`c922c4a`, `feature/client-react-vite`), and the endpoint reached `develop` (`9ee8b43`).** The screen is now a React page over the same stylesheet, submitting a real request; [D10](#7-open-decisions)'s hand-rolled mechanism is applied here first, so the twin-input duplication is gone and every string comes from a resource file. Verified against the endpoint's own JOI schema and in a browser end to end. **Both Register screens now exist**: `screens/register.html` stays as the static prototype and `src/features/register/` is the live one — see [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) for the duplicated stylesheet that costs. **2026-08-29: [D30](#7-open-decisions) closed and it changed what this screen owes.** A successful registration navigates to **Login**, which then navigates to the **Personal dashboard**; and Register **stops issuing tokens**, leaving Login as the only authenticator. **Neither change is made.** The screen still ends at its bilingual success state and still stores the Access Token, because Login is not a React screen yet and the flow cannot be wired to a static page that a production build does not serve. The three owed changes and the order to make them in are in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) **2026-08-29, fifth pass — the client half of [D30](#7-open-decisions) landed (`2c225ca`).** Register **no longer establishes a session**: `useRegisterForm` stores nothing, `RegisterResponse` dropped `accessToken` so no code here can consume one, and a successful registration navigates to `/login`. The bilingual success panel is **deleted**, and its strings with it, because it told the person they were already signed in — which the approved flow no longer makes true. **The server has not caught up:** `POST /api/auth/register` still answers `201` with an `accessToken` and a `Set-Cookie`, verified live, so D30 (a) is the one remaining owed change. **One consequence is left open rather than decided:** registration now lands on Login with **no confirmation that the account was created**. A notice there would be new copy *and* a new success-alert style — `login.css` carries only `.alert--error` — so neither was invented **2026-08-29, sixth pass — the contract is corrected and the employee path exists (`bc6f032`, `6a06d32`, `19ec45b`, `93eb395`).** Register **authenticates nobody**: `201 { user }`, no tokens, no cookie, no session — [D30](#7-open-decisions) is fully paid on both sides. It now carries **`standing`** — `owner` or `employee`, **required, with no default**, which decides what the registration means: an owner creates the company and takes the four default permissions; **an employee claims a seat their employer already opened** and lands on `pending_company_approval`, holding nothing. The company-scoped fields follow standing — `officePhone` and `availability` are **refused** for an employee, and `companyPosition` is **required** for one and refused for an owner. The React screen asks for standing first and hides what does not apply; its standing labels, five position labels and two invitation errors are **new copy and are not approved**. Rate limited at 10 per hour per IP **2026-08-29, seventh pass — the copy is approved and the employee form only shows what an employee may send (`2294b48`, `70f3391`, `e1398dd`, `6441c88`).** `standing` lost its `.default('owner')`: a request that does not say which registration it is is now answered rather than guessed at. **The eight strings I had invented are replaced by approved ones** — the standing question asks *how* somebody is joining, the two options are the plain roles, the employee explanation says what will happen to the registration and names no internal state, and both invitation errors are short and actionable, with the ambiguous one no longer asking a person to work out which invitation row is theirs. **The two company-only fields are now absent from the employee form rather than rendered and refused**, and switching standing clears the values of the path being left, so the form never holds data its own UI does not display — asserted by reading the request body off the wire, not from the form's appearance. **Terminology:** one canonical label per position, `מנהל עבודה` / Site Manager everywhere, with `מנהל אתר` kept only as a synonym **2026-08-29, owner-QA corrections.** The **Terms and Privacy references stopped being links**: they pointed at `#`, and a legal reference that opens nothing is worse than one that does not claim to. They are named in the consent line and the screens themselves are [D25](#7-open-decisions). The consent line also lost its slash form, under the Hebrew voice rule in [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) **2026-08-30, the two-step rework — integrated (`4d1d91b` backend, `bf478ed` React).** **Registration is now two steps, not three.** Step 1 opens with the **registration route**, and the rest of the step follows from it; Step 2 is the email-delivery choice. **Three separate routes** — `קבלנים / בעלי מקצוע מבצעים`, `קטגוריה אדריכלית / בעלי מקצוע`, `ספקים` — and suppliers and the architectural category are **not** forced through the contractor taxonomy. **`registrationCategory` is stored explicitly and never derived** from the specialty: the three lists do not overlap, so it *could* be inferred, and it is stored anyway so Browse can filter on it and so a specialty edit can never move an account between routes. It is not a project role, not a company position, and **grants nothing**. **The father-approved taxonomy replaces the 23-code `TRADES` enum wholesale** — 20 contractor codes, 5 architectural, 12 supplier. No legacy code is kept and **no migration layer was built**, on the owner's finding that no production account needed one; the harness fixtures were updated instead. `קונגו` is gone as a tool rather than a profession, and `קידוחי צנרת ניקוז` is gone as a separate specialty. **`קידוחי החדרה וצנרת PVC` is a nested drilling subtype**, in `drillingTypes`, refining `drilling` exactly as `heavyEquipment` refines `heavy_equipment` — and cleared with the profession that carries it. **`Other` exists only inside a route:** three distinct codes, one per route, so the free text refines a choice already made rather than replacing it. **There is no route-neutral catch-all**, and a supplier sending `contractor_other` is refused. **Step 2's email consent is optional but explicit:** the owner-approved Hebrew is carried verbatim with a natural English equivalent, **neither option is preselected**, one must be chosen, and declining never blocks registration, disables the platform or removes in-app notifications. It is stored as `notificationPreferences.operationalEmail`, required with no default. **The Notification Settings screen that would change it afterwards does not exist yet.** **Edit profile offers only the account's own route**, and the profile service answers `SPECIALTY_OUTSIDE_CATEGORY` (422) to a patch naming another route's code. **Automated verification: green** — backend 1,243 checks across 22 suites (including the new `verify:register-taxonomy`, 64), browser 807 across 16 (including the new `verify:register-ui`, 62), both typechecks clean, Vite build ✓. **Owner manual QA is still pending.** |

Login now passes the same messages through the same `TextField` / `PasswordField` `error` prop, and `login.css` carries the register treatment verbatim. Email dispatch + single-use-token verification wired in Stage 2. **2026-08-25:** both gained the corrected language-pill placement from the shared stylesheet (`4e1cf33`) and were **measured, not assumed** — 12 of the 18 passing assertions are theirs. **Later the same day (`c6d53c5`) both also gained full page mirroring** from that stylesheet, and their back-link chevron was verified pointing to the reading start in each language. **`forgot-password.html` additionally had one bullet reworded on owner instruction** — the anti-enumeration line now reads `מטעמי אבטחה, תוצג אותה הודעה בין אם קיים חשבון לכתובת האימייל ובין אם לא.` in Hebrew, with the English sibling reworded to match; **wording only, the reset behaviour is unchanged**, and the screen's footer note still makes the same point a second time **2026-08-29 — both are React screens (`2c225ca`)**, at `/forgot-password` and `/reset-password`, public because a person who cannot sign in has no session to reach them with; Login's *Forgot password?* points at the first. `src/features/password-reset/` loads the auth stylesheet plus a small sheet holding only what these two add — the stepper, the back link, the status badge — so Login does not carry rules no Login element uses. **Reset now does the confirm-password match** the prototype could not: a stylesheet cannot compare two values, which is why the static screen carried a hint and deferred it. **Neither screen sends a request, and that is now the whole story of this row:** there is no forgot-password endpoint, no reset token and no mail transport anywhere in the backend — five candidate paths were probed against the running API and every one answered `404 ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` — so each screen advances to its approved step and states that nothing was sent or changed. **The anti-enumeration answer was measured rather than assumed:** a real account and an unknown address produce the identical confirmation **2026-08-29 — the server behind these two screens now exists (`bc6f032`, `d0a441e`, `6a06d32`), and the mail-transport decision that blocked everything is made.** `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` and `POST /api/auth/reset-password`, with delivery by **Nodemailer over Brevo SMTP**. Forgot answers `200 { status: 'ok' }` for a known address, an unknown one and a suspended account alike — **and the email is dispatched off the request path**, so a known address cannot answer measurably slower either. The link is 32 CSPRNG bytes, stored only as a SHA-256 hash, valid 30 minutes, single-use, and retired by a newer request; every failure answers one `401 INVALID_RESET_TOKEN`. A successful reset writes the new hash, spends the token and revokes every Refresh Token in **one transaction**. **The email is written in the account's own `users.language`, never both** — an owner decision that closes one of [D10](#7-open-decisions)'s sub-questions. The screens are wired to all of it (`f5fd3e9`, `35955d7`): the approved copy replaced the wording proposed on 2026-08-29, buttons keep their label under a MUI spinner instead of swapping text, and **a reset page opened without a usable link is no longer a dead end** — it offers the email field and sends a new link through the same forgot-password call. Both screens are rate limited (5 per 15 min, and 10 per 15 min) |
| JWT auth + protected routes (server side) | 1–2 | **In progress** | Built on `feature/login.backend`. **Two token types**, not the single JWT this table originally assumed — a 15-minute **Access Token** on `Authorization: Bearer` and a 7-day **Refresh Token** in an HttpOnly cookie, stored as a SHA-256 hash and rotated on every use with family-wide revocation on replay. `POST /api/auth/login` (JOI-validated, bcrypt cost 12) and `POST /api/auth/refresh`; `GET /api/health-auth` is the protected test route. All failures answer `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, `UNAUTHENTICATED` or `INVALID_REFRESH_TOKEN` and never say more. **2026-08-28 (`feature/register-screen.backend`):** `POST /api/auth/register` joins them and **authenticates the new user immediately** — approved owner behaviour, so signup flows straight into the profile wizard with no second Login. It reuses the identical token pair through a `tokenPair` service extracted from the login closure, so **no second token mechanism exists**; verified end-to-end that a Register Access Token opens a protected route, its Refresh Token rotates, a replay revokes the family, and the two types stay non-interchangeable. One new error code, `EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` (409) — the deliberate exception to the anti-enumeration stance, because a signup form must name the field to fix and is already an enumeration surface; Login and password reset are unchanged. **2026-08-28, second pass:** Register now commits [[companies]] + [[users]] + an owner [[companymemberships]] row in **one MongoDB transaction** — the compensating delete it shipped with that morning is gone, and `npm run verify:register-txn` proves that an injected failure at either the user or the membership write leaves **no company, no user and no membership**. Requires the deployment to run as a replica set. **Client side is not built** — no Axios interceptor, no `PrivateRoute`, no token storage. See [§11](#11-decision-log) for the single-JWT reversal **2026-08-29 — a valid signature is no longer sufficient (`d0a441e`, `6a06d32`).** `requireAccessToken` now makes one indexed read per authenticated request, projected to two fields, and refuses a token whose account no longer exists, whose account is not `isSessionPermitted`, or whose `iat` predates `users.security.passwordChangedAt`. That is what makes a **stateless** credential revocable: a password reset and a ban both reach a token already in circulation, immediately, instead of waiting up to 900 seconds for it to expire. **Login, Refresh and every protected route now apply the same `isSessionPermitted` rule**, which was widened to take a status rather than a whole user so all three could call it. The cost is stated rather than hidden: one lookup per request, and a one-second granularity because `iat` is whole seconds — a token minted in the same second as a reset survives, which is the safer half of that trade, since rounding the other way rejects the token Login mints moments later |

It completes the lifecycle [D28](#7-open-decisions) and [D29](#7-open-decisions) described and nothing had walked: `invited` → `pending_company_approval` → `active`. Approving *several* is the single-approval route called per row; a subset endpoint is a small addition if a screen wants one call. ~~**What is missing is the integration, not a design.**~~ *(Corrected 2026-08-29: this row previously said the feature had no approved design, which was wrong.)* **The React screen is now built too (2026-08-29) — implemented on feature branches and NOT yet merged.** Frontend `feature/employee-management-react` (`3ceab43`), backend `feature/employee-management-onboarding.backend` (`c1a4c5a`). **Implemented ≠ integrated:** neither branch is merged into `develop`, and the feature has had **no browser validation** — RTL/LTR and responsive behaviour were checked statically and at build level only, because no browser tooling ran. **One reusable React feature, mounted in two places and shared entirely** — list, invitation form, status rendering, individual approval, bulk approval, loading states, error states, API layer and translations are one implementation; only the surrounding navigation differs. Routes: `/employees` (authenticated) and `/onboarding/employees` (the optional first-time step). It consumes the four routes above plus `POST /api/companies/employee-setup/complete`, uses the existing Axios client, and carries **no mock employee data** — a company with no seats renders an empty state, because empty is a legitimate server answer. **Invitation input is `fullName` + `companyPosition` and nothing else**; the company is derived from the caller's own session, never typed or selected. **Individual approval appears only on rows the backend reports as `pending_company_approval`** — absence rather than a disabled control — and **bulk approval calls the real `approve-all` endpoint**, never a client-side loop. Every action re-reads the canonical list rather than writing a status locally. **This is not Permissions.** Approval moves `pending_company_approval` → `active` and grants **nothing**: no company permissions, no project permissions, no copy of the owner's, and nothing inferred from `companyPosition`. ~~**Permissions remains a separate, unimplemented future feature**~~ *(Superseded 2026-08-30: Permissions is built and integrated into `develop`. The point this sentence was making still holds — employee approval grants nothing and infers nothing from `companyPosition` — but the feature is no longer unbuilt.)* **2026-08-29, gained cancellation and integrated (`13a7e0e`).** A **pending invitation can now be withdrawn** — `DELETE /api/companies/employees/invitations/:membershipId`, backend-authoritative and company-scoped. The row moves `invited → inactive` and is **kept as history**; a claimed or approved membership cannot be removed through it. The screen re-reads the server's list after success, so **the row leaves because the server dropped it, not because React hid it**. A withdrawn seat is filtered out of the list because it is not a member |

It is never inferred from an empty employee list, from whether invitations exist, from browser history or from storage: **an owner may legitimately have zero employees**, so an empty list has never meant the step was skipped |
| **Waiting for Approval (pending employee)** | 2 | In progress | **Implemented on `feature/employee-management-react` 2026-08-29 and NOT merged** (`3ceab43`). Built directly in React on the existing FieldSync design system — **no static HTML prototype was made for it**. The approved employee lifecycle: owner opens an invitation → employee Registers → membership becomes `pending_company_approval` → employee goes to Login → **Login succeeds normally** → authenticated Waiting for Approval screen → owner approves → `active` → normal application access. **A pending employee is a fully authenticated User.** Their Access Token and Refresh Token are untouched, they are not redirected to Register, not treated as banned and not treated as unauthenticated — they simply hold no *active* company membership yet. **No re-registration and no special approval token exist**, and **no second Login is required merely because approval happened**. **Check status** re-reads canonical state through `GET /api/auth/me` on the same token and moves them on when the membership is `active`; approval is never faked locally. **No real-time infrastructure was added** — no WebSockets, no SSE, no polling and no short-interval timers; the manual control plus ordinary session revalidation is the whole mechanism. Route guards distinguish three states from backend data alone — unauthenticated, authenticated + pending, authenticated + active — and the waiting screen sits outside the membership guard while sending an approved employee onward, so the two cannot bounce into each other. The screen names **no internal terminology**: `pending_company_approval`, `CompanyMembership` and *invitation claim* appear nowhere in its copy |

Three closed decisions reached the screen in the move: **[D28](#7-open-decisions)** puts **Company name** under the name as the professional identity; **[D15](#7-open-decisions)** replaces the personal phone row with **email, office phone and business phone** as three separate rows, each reported missing on its own terms and with **no fallback** — `users.phone` is not in the view model at all, so no later edit can render it; and **[D14](#7-open-decisions)**'s gender-neutral labels replace the masculine `מקבל`. **What it renders is mostly not real, and the code says so:** name and email come from the authenticated user Login returned, and everything else is the prototype's representative data, because **there is no `GET /api/users/me`** **2026-08-29, later the same day — the backend behind this screen now exists** (`5ecc46c`, `feature/my-profile.backend`, unmerged). `GET /api/users/me` returns one payload for both profile screens, assembled key by key from [[users]], [[companies]], [[companymemberships]] and the new [[workentries]] — so `companyName`, `officePhone` and `availability` are **resolved from the company** rather than mirrored onto the person, and no hash, token, permission list or Mongoose internal can ride along. Ratings answer honestly: `rating` and `flexibility` are `null` and `ratings` is `[]`, because no rating domain exists to read. Avatars and Completed Work photos are served from `GET /api/users/me/assets/:id`, looked up by id **and** owner together. **The row stays In progress:** the React screen is still reading representative data, because nothing has connected it to these routes yet **2026-08-29, integrated into `develop` (`13a7e0e`).** ~~Both screens render the prototype's representative values because no profile endpoint was written~~ — **superseded.** `representativeProfile()` and the whole `REPRESENTATIVE` block are **deleted**. My profile reads `GET /users/me`; Edit profile reads the same and writes back through **two** routes, because the fields live on two documents: personal fields to `PATCH /users/me`, and **company name, office phone and availability to `PATCH /companies/me`**. Only changed fields are sent, so an untouched value is never rewritten with itself. **Nothing is fabricated any more** — no company name, phones, city, rating, flexibility, bio or work history; a missing value renders as *not provided* or `—`, never as a default or a zero. Rating and flexibility come back `null` (cold start) and render the approved neutral mark. Loading, error and retry states exist on both screens |

It had no row here at all, while [§11](#11-decision-log)'s `2026-08-27` auth entry recorded its absence from the other side (*"Logout is not implemented, so no route revokes a family on purpose yet"*). **Engineering rides the existing auth model rather than adding one:** the server side is the deliberate **family-revocation** route the rotating-Refresh-Token design already anticipates, and the client clears its stored Access Token. **The banned-user token gap in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) is a separate hole and is not closed by this** — Logout gives *the user* a revocation path; a ban still does not revoke until expiry **2026-08-29, built and integrated (`13a7e0e`).** `POST /api/auth/logout`, unauthenticated by design so an expired Access Token cannot trap somebody in a session. It revokes **only the token family the caller presented** (`revokeFamily`, never `revokeAllForUser`), so other devices keep their own sessions, and clears the HttpOnly refresh cookie. **Always 204** — a missing, malformed or already-dead token answers identically, so nothing can probe whether a family existed. **Logout ≠ account deletion:** it writes nothing to the user, the company or any history, and the account signs in again immediately. The client calls it first and clears local state in a `finally`, so a failed request still leaves the device signed out |

Static HTML/CSS on `feature/my-tasks` (`screens/my-tasks.html` + `screens/my-tasks.css`, `9fec69d`) on the filename the dashboard reserved. **One list holding two kinds of work.** A project task and a standalone job sit in the same list, ordered together, and are told apart by a **source tag** — a solid tag naming the project, or a dashed tag reading `עבודה עצמאית` / *Solo job*. Solid-versus-dashed plus an icon plus the wording carry the distinction, so it never depends on colour. **The screen encodes no answer to [D5](#7-open-decisions):** nothing on it says whether the two live in one collection or two, and the tag renders `project === null` identically under either shape. **Three groups, and only two of them are stored states.** *Overdue* is **derived** — past `endDate` and still not `completed` — and is carried by the group, by a leading edge and by the lateness written out in words; **no stored `overdue` status was created and an overdue row keeps its real status chip**, so the enum is never quietly extended to four values. *Open work* and *Completed* read `publicStatus` directly. The overdue lede repeats the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) rule that the list sends no reminders. **Quick actions are the approved binary model, on the row.** *Start* appears on a `not_started` row, *Complete* on an `in_progress` row, and **neither on a completed one** — the action set is a pure function of the stored status. Every row also carries a quiet *Open* into `task-detail.html`. **The pending-proposal marker sits beside the status chip and never replaces it:** one row shows `טרם התחילה` / *Not started* **and** a marker linking to `reschedule-proposal-review.html`, because a proposal is a second fact about the row rather than a state of it. **Filter and sort are four native controls** — Project · Status · Due date · Type, the exact four axes #15 names — **and none of them is wired**, which follows the Browse precedent rather than the My-projects one; the reasoning is in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks). **Three group empty states plus a global one**, all driven by `:has()`, and the global one deliberately carries **no CTA**, because no create control on this screen has been approved. **Zero new JavaScript**; `lang.js` is loaded, so this screen does not repeat the defect §8 records against `register.html` and `browse-contractors.html`. **No new filename was reserved.** One decision opened here: **[D21](#7-open-decisions)** — what a row's counterparty actually is, and what a delegate may be shown in its place **2026-08-30 — React + backend built and integrated into `develop`.** Route `/tasks`, and the navbar link is no longer a disabled span. The static prototype was reconciled into `Frontend/screens/` (`5de5316`) and is retained. **One `tasks` collection with an explicit stored `kind`** (`project \| standalone`) — owner decision on [D5](#7-open-decisions). Progress is two timestamps: the three states are derived on read and **`overdue` is a comparison, never stored** and never one of the states. The four approved filters are wired and nothing beside them was invented. The counterparty is resolved per viewer; a row with nobody in that slot says **`עבודה שפתחתי`** (owner-approved). Action labels are **`התחלת עבודה` / `סיום עבודה`** (owner-approved). 68 backend + 57 browser checks passing. |
| **Create / edit project** | 3 | **Implemented + integrated into `develop` · automated verification passed · OWNER MANUAL QA PENDING** | Approved screen **#12**, on the filename the 404 screen and the Personal dashboard reserved and that My projects already links from four places. Static HTML/CSS on `feature/create-project` (`screens/create-project.html` + `screens/create-project.css`, `36c3d82`). **One file holds both states.** A static file cannot change an input `value`, so Create and Edit are **two `<form>` elements** swapped by a hidden radio pair — the same mechanism the language toggle uses — and the switch is rendered on the screen as an openly-labelled **prototype control** which says in both languages that React turns these into two routes. The alternative, a second `edit-project.html`, was rejected: it would have invented a filename nobody reserved, and My projects' *Edit* control points at `create-project.html`, so the Edit state has to live there. **The fields are the ones [[projects]] already has** — `name` (required), `description`, `location.city` / `location.region` / `location.address`, `startDate`, `targetEndDate`, `photos` — and **location is collected as city + region code + address rather than free text**, because [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) already records that free text can never populate a `region` index. **The working calendar is the one new concept on the screen.** A new project **inherits the contractor's own default**, the days rendered are that contractor's default and the panel says so in words, so nothing here can be read as a FieldSync-wide working week; *Customize for this project* and *Reset to default* are two labels on one checkbox, so both directions are real rather than described; and a second note states that the panel sets the **weekly pattern only**, so holidays and one-off dates are not silently implied. **No schedule-exception workflow was built**, on instruction, and nothing on the screen contradicts the authority model recorded in [§11](#11-decision-log). **Delete Project exists in the Edit state only**, last on the page, in its own treatment, and **neither it nor its `:target` dialog says what deletion does** — that is [D24](#7-open-decisions). **No control anywhere is disabled** (measured: zero), per the standing *absent, not disabled* rule, which is also why Create carries no delete control rather than a greyed one. **Zero new JavaScript** — `lang.js` and `validation.js` are the two tracked shared scripts. **No new filename was reserved.** Three decisions opened here: **[D22](#7-open-decisions)**, **[D23](#7-open-decisions)** and **[D24](#7-open-decisions)** **2026-08-30 — React + backend built and integrated into `develop`.** Routes `/projects/new` and `/projects/:projectId/edit`. Type (four values + free text), **required free-text size**, the shared structured location, the closed date model (`originalTargetEndDate` never rewritten, an immutable overrun allowance and a ceiling the target may never pass) and the C-SAFE calendar panel. Cancellation is **pre-start only** ([D24](#7-open-decisions)) and now derives from the same closed start rule the status does. |

The dashboard **composes** what the other features own — the project through the one shared `toProjectDto` mapper, member counts from the same membership rows, grants from the same Permissions endpoints, the pinned C-SAFE calendar with its adoption history — and stores nothing of its own. **Real Task figures are now connected**: total, open, overdue and completed, counted from task rows, with a project that has no work answering `null` rather than four zeros. ~~Dashboard includes timeline + DAG view + audit~~ **— still NOT implemented: the timeline and dependency-graph *rendering* have no approved visualisation, and the audit log is unbuilt. The stage-level edge data they would draw exists underneath.** Pending-proposal display depends on the unbuilt proposal domain, and the orphan dashboard view is not designed. 78 backend + 71 browser checks passing. |
| Project members + per-project roles | 3 | **Implemented + integrated into `develop` · automated verification passed · OWNER MANUAL QA PENDING** | Role lives on membership **2026-08-30 — React + backend built and integrated into `develop`.** Route `/projects/:projectId/members`. ~~Role lives on membership~~ **— still true, but the role no longer grants anything.** `projectRole` is descriptive and is read by no authorisation check; authority comes from `permissions[]` and `fullAuthority` alone. **An invitation is the only way onto a project**: a row is created as an offer and only the person it names turns it into a membership. An invited person has **no read on the project** until they accept, and sees only the owner-approved disclosure set (name, type, city, both dates, who invited, offered role). **Declining is its own state and is not permanent** — the same row is reused on re-invitation. Invitation authority runs through the **shared grant resolver**, so there is no membership-specific permission code. 129 backend + 70 browser checks passing. |

Only the **performer** may report — and on delegated work that is the delegate, not the delegator. The same Task truth feeds My Tasks, Task Detail and the Project Dashboard. |

A stage may be flagged a true gate, edges are refused when they would close a loop, and **no second task-level DAG exists**. **Delegation is single level** (onward delegation refused), **whole or part at the delegator's choice**, and **own-crew-only forbids it outright**. A delegate is served **no project, no stage, no sequence and no `createdBy`**, and sees the **delegator** as their counterparty; the party above is never told the delegation exists. The **private execution layer** is readable only by its owner — the delegator who owns the task reads zero rows of it — and a tick in it moves nothing public. The **date-change entry point** answers 503 and says the cascade is not built; **work-plan upload is not built** and says so. 61 backend + 47 browser checks passing. |

edit is deliberately absent**: changing the dates of work somebody has committed to is the [D2](#7-open-decisions) cascade, which is unbuilt, and a half-edit that quietly rewrote a committed date is the one thing the closed rule forbids. **Eight owner decisions were taken before a line was written** — recorded in [§11](#11-decision-log) for the same day. **Creation authority is the project grant and nothing else**: `task.create` opens work for yourself, and naming a **different** person additionally requires `task.assign`, checked at creation so the create flow cannot walk around it. **Standalone work is gated on the COMPANY-level `task.create`**, because it sits outside every project grant; active membership never stands in for it. **A project task always names a stage**, so the minimum GC-authorised stage creation (`POST /projects/:projectId/stages`, guarded by `project.edit`) shipped with it rather than allowing a stage-less shortcut. **The assignee is required and must be an active member** — `assignee: null` stays reserved for the closed orphan meaning, and an unanswered invitation is not assignable. **The project window is a hard refusal**, including the overrun ceiling that can never be passed, while **a non-working day is only a warning** computed from the weekly pattern the project already stores; no holiday source was invented. **A delegation supplied at creation never lands**, because the party above may not name the performer. **No dependency picker and no trade field**: both appear in the Decision Map's wording for this screen, and both are stale — the dependency representation closed as stage-level, and the model has never carried a trade. 54 backend + 66 browser checks passing. |
