## 7. Open decisions

> Parked on purpose. We decide each when we reach the stage that forces it. Don't pre-optimize.

| # | Decision | Options on the table | Decide by | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **File storage** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-29) by owner decision: Multer → multipart/form-data parsing and validation → GridFS-backed persistent storage, with `fileassets` as the metadata / ownership layer.** The reasons, verbatim: the project supports files that may reach approximately **30 MB**; MongoDB has a **16 MB per-document limit**, therefore ordinary inline Buffer storage is unsuitable; the Heroku and local filesystems are **ephemeral** and cannot be used as durable storage; **GridFS is the appropriate MongoDB storage mechanism for files beyond the ordinary document-size limit**; and the storage implementation remains **encapsulated behind the file-storage service boundary**. ~~GridFS (keeps full 30MB, more work) vs. cap at ~15MB as a Buffer in the doc (simpler, but bloats docs / bad practice).~~ ***(The rejected alternative — the capped in-document Buffer only works if uploads are capped below 16 MB, which the product does not allow.)*** **Nothing competing may be introduced:** not local durable disk, not S3, not Cloudinary, not another storage mechanism. **Built the same day** with the My Profile / Edit Profile backend (`5ecc46c`, `feature/my-profile.backend`): a custom Multer storage engine streams each upload straight into GridFS, `gridFs.service.ts` is the only module that knows GridFS exists, and every `fileassets` row records `storage.driver` so a later move changes one value rather than the schema. `docs/database-design.html` was updated in the same sitting; **the Decision Map still carries D1 as Open and needs the same closure applied separately by the owner.** | — | **Closed** |
| D2 | **Cascade negotiation semantics** | ✅ **CLOSED as policy (findings 2026-08-24 / 2026-08-28).** ~~Per-task proposals (a downstream rejection leaves that task unresolved + flagged) **vs.** one bundled proposal everyone votes on. Open sub-questions, all undecided: what "one round" means exactly; whether a counter-proposal — which proposes a *different* date and so may have its *own* downstream cascade — can re-trigger the cascade; and the state transitions to pin down.~~ ***(Superseded — the domain answered all of it.)*** **The closed rule set.** **Per-task proposals.** A date change moves **every dependent date automatically**, with **approval requested from each affected professional**; a change anywhere in a chain drags the whole chain, and downstream work that can still proceed, proceeds. A **mandatory impact preview** — which tasks and how many — is shown to the GC, or to whoever holds the granted authority, **before** approval. **Authorized manual include/exclude** of dependents is available to the GC, a `מנהל אתרים` with the GC's approval, or a `מנהל עבודה` down the directive chain — and a professional **never** changes a date affecting others except through the GC or a granted authority. An **alternatives mode** builds several candidate schedules, visible to the **GC and the granted-authority holder only**. **A proposal lives at most 72 hours** — `כן מקסימום 72 שעות` — and **expiry does nothing automatic**: it hands the decision back to the GC, who turns to another professional. **Partial approval resolves at the GC's sole discretion**, including replacing the decliners. A proposal **may be cancelled after partial responses**, with a `בוטל` notice to everyone it touched. **A date move carries an explanation; a cancellation carries only the fact.** **Only the GC** sees the aggregate who-approved / who-declined / who-is-pending view — a professional who filed a request sees the GC's state toward *his own* request and nothing more. **Only the GC cancels a stale proposal.** **Parallel proposals on the same work stack** onto the pending one, days included. **Counter re-cascade — CLOSED BY ENTAILMENT, flagged as such.** No line answers it directly, so it is derived rather than quoted, **and the owner's veto is reserved**: since the closed rule moves every dependent date with per-affected approval on *any* date change, and an accepted counter **is** an approved date change, the engine re-runs beneath it — and for everyone below, that is their **first** round, so the one-round rule is untouched. **The convergence worth recording:** this row's own earlier line — that *"one round" has to be redefined as one round per edge* — is precisely that argument, reached before the findings confirmed it. **Residue: the proposal document's shape**, which rides the schema — and [[rescheduleproposals]] as designed (one atomic chain-scoped document, embedded `items[]`, per-item respondents) already supports every rule above, so what remains is writing it down rather than choosing it. | Stage 4 | **Closed as policy — document shape rides the schema** |
| D3 | **Notification delivery** | Fetch-on-load **vs.** simple polling. (Real-time/websockets is explicitly **out** — too big a rabbit hole, not required.) | Stage 4/6 | Open |
| D4 | **Repo layout** | One repo with `client/` + `server/` (current assumption) **vs.** two repos. | Stage 1 | Leaning monorepo |
| D5 | **Data model relationships** | Embed vs. reference for each entity (memberships, dependencies, messages, …). Includes: do standalone **jobs** and project **tasks** share one polymorphic model (nullable project, no dependencies) or split into two models? | Per stage | Open **✅ The task/job half is CLOSED (2026-08-30) by owner decision: ONE `tasks` collection with an explicit stored `kind` (`project \| standalone`) beside an optional project reference.** The `project: null` shape the companion schema page had drawn was *modelled, not approved*, and this closure is what §8 said was owed — the decision was taken deliberately rather than inherited by inertia. Two collections were considered and rejected: My Tasks would have had to merge and sort them in memory, which cannot be paged from an index, and delegation, files and the private layer would each have been built twice. **The rest of D5 is unaffected and stays as recorded.** |
| D6 | **Flexibility score formula** | The score is "auto-calculated from reschedule behaviour" — but the formula is undefined: weighting of accept / reject / counter, the time window, whether it decays, and the cold-start value for users with no history. **The cold-start *presentation* is settled (2026-08-20): a neutral `—` and no explanatory copy**, on the public profile and the browse card alike. **The formula INPUTS are now CLOSED too (findings 2026-08-28) — what stays open is arithmetic alone.** The closed inputs: measure **accepted versus declined** date changes, where **a decline that comes with an alternative still counts as flexible** — the contractor's own sub, another date, or any workable solution. **Two dimensions**, not one: `גמישות בזמנים` and `גמישות בסדר גודל של העבודה`. **Magnitude is equivalent** — agreeing to a one-day move counts like a week and like a month. **Weight who asked, and how far in advance they said so.** **The justified-decline list never hurts the score**: health, plans not ready, tool failure, no permit from the owner, a GC stop, or materials or tools that did not arrive. **Context is displayed without exposing personal dealings** between contractors. **A single project's history is enough**, and the score updates continuously. **It renders as both a number and a verbal category.** **Attribution closed 2026-08-29: when work is delegated the score impact belongs to the DELEGATOR, not the delegate**, because the delegator remains the party responsible toward the project — the same reasoning that makes [[auditentries]]`.actor` always the delegator and keeps the delegate out of every serializer. ~~What stays open is the arithmetic — the formula, and the `sampleSize` threshold at which a score starts being shown at all.~~ **Still open, and it is arithmetic only:** the weights, the window, whether it decays, and the `sampleSize` threshold. ~~[§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1)'s two-role wrinkle — a contractor accruing behaviour as both delegator and delegate — stands and is still part of this row.~~ **[§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1)'s two-role wrinkle is resolved by that attribution rule** and is no longer part of this row. **No formula, weighting, points, decay or cold-start number has been invented anywhere in this document.** **2026-08-29, the arithmetic gap is now load-bearing:** the approved static Browse screen carries a **Minimum flexibility** filter, and it was **deliberately not migrated to React**. A numeric filter needs a number, and no formula is approved — inventing a default, a weighting or a cold-start value to reproduce an obsolete control would be inventing the score. Unknown flexibility stays `—` everywhere. **The filter is blocked on this decision** | Stage 4 | **Presentation settled · inputs closed (2026-08-28) · attribution closed (2026-08-29) · arithmetic open** |
| D7 | **Delegation depth** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-06-02): single-level only, no re-delegation.** A delegate cannot delegate onward; if the first delegate can't do it, the original assignee picks a different one. Visibility stops one hop in each direction (GC sees delegator; delegator sees delegate; delegate sees delegator but **not** the GC). Full model + derived constraints in **[§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1)**. **Extended 2026-08-29** with the validated addendum in that section: **departures resolve asymmetrically** (a departing delegate returns responsibility to the delegator automatically and silently; a departing *delegator* either hands the delegate to the GC by his own disclosure or takes him along), **whole-or-part delegation is the delegator's choice**, and **on-site exposure is real-world only**. The row's own line — *"if the first delegate can't do it, the original assignee picks a different one"* — stays true and is now the ordinary case of a broader closed rule. | — | **Closed** |
| D8 | **User removal semantics** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-29) by owner decision — the 60-day lifecycle.** ~~Deactivate vs. delete: what happens to the user's projects, tasks, connections, messages, and audit-log entries. You can't hard-delete someone referenced in other people's audit history without breaking it → likely forces soft-delete.~~ ***(Superseded — the row's instinct was right and the answer is wider than "soft-delete".)*** **A deletion request is a deactivation plus a 60-day recovery period.** During it the account is **not active for normal use**; **admin / support may access the details needed for support, recovery or reactivation**; and the **user may request restoration within the window**. **Not restored inside 60 days → the PII is anonymized**, while **shared business / project history is preserved** so no other participant's record breaks. **Messages, tasks, audit / history and other shared records are never deleted solely because the account was deleted** — attribution becomes a **neutral deleted-user identity** where appropriate. **Every immediately-permanent-with-no-recovery wording anywhere is superseded by this**, wherever it is still found. **Keep the names apart, per [§8](#8-known-issues--risks)'s standing deletion vocabulary:** the neutral deleted-user **attribution** is **not** the deleted-message **placeholder** — an anonymized author's messages keep their content, and nothing here turns them into `הודעה זו נמחקה`. **Consistent and unchanged:** login still refuses a non-active account, and **account deletion is still not an orphaned-work trigger** ([§11](#11-decision-log), 2026-08-29). **Flagged open and deliberately not decided here:** the operational handling of **active commitments** during and after the 60 days. | Stage 2 (settings) | **Closed — active-commitment handling open** |
| D9 | **Messaging gate scope** | **The gate half is CLOSED — and it closed by being **reversed**, not answered.** ~~Gated by *connection only*, or *connection OR shared project*?~~ *(Superseded 2026-08-28: the question no longer exists, because connections are not the gate.)* The validated answer, verbatim: `כל אחד יכול לשלוח הודעה בפלטפורמה לקבלן אמורה להיות אופציה לאשר או לדחות את ההודעה או לחסום(section בקשות כמו באינסטגרם)`. So: **anyone may initiate**; a first message from a non-connection lands as a **request**; the recipient **approves** (the thread opens), **rejects** (it is declined), or **blocks** — and **block is the no-contact state**. **Connections remain the network layer and gate nothing.** This reverses the [§1](#1-product-overview) bullet, which was rewritten in the same pass, and it is the standing *domain wins* rule applied: a validated answer overrides an older closed decision. **The thread half is untouched and still open:** what happens to an **existing** thread on disconnect / block / mute is not decided, which is exactly why [D17](#7-open-decisions)'s removal dialog may claim nothing about conversations. Residue beyond that: the request object's shape and the requests section itself ([§6](#6-feature-registry) Inbox). | Stage 6 | **Partially closed — gate half reversed (2026-08-28), thread half open** |
| D10 | **i18n mechanism under React** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-28) on the mechanism; three sub-questions survive it.** The answer is the **hand-rolled** option: a shared `LanguageContext` plus centralized Hebrew/English string resources, **one field per logical value**, with RTL/LTR direction derived from the selected language. `react-i18next` was rejected — not on capability, but because the dependency and its learning curve buy interpolation and pluralisation this project has not needed once across 11 built screens. **The rejected third option is the one that had to be named explicitly:** the static prototypes render every string twice (`he-text`/`en-text` spans, `he-field`/`en-field` twin inputs) and hide one with CSS, because they have no JavaScript to swap an attribute. That mechanism **must not be ported into React as a permanent solution** — it duplicates every control, gives one value two DOM homes, and only ever existed to work around the absence of JS. Porting it for one screen was considered when Register moved to React and was refused for exactly that reason. **The migration cost is now explicit:** whichever screen goes first has to replace the `he-field`/`en-field` rules in its stylesheet, so before any such CSS change the owed step is to enumerate every rule depending on twins and show the replacement for each, then verify both languages at the widths the static screen was verified at. **One of the three sub-questions closed 2026-08-29 by owner decision: server-side artifacts use the stored `users.language`.** The password-reset email is written in the account's own language and **never in both** — a Hebrew account gets a Hebrew message, an English account an English one, and there is no second email-language preference. `Accept-Language` was not chosen: the account already records a preference the person set deliberately, and a header describes the browser rather than the person. The decision is scoped to what it says — it settles the *source* for server-composed text; the notification-digest surface it will also govern is not built. **Still open, and neither adds a field:** does the URL carry the locale (`/he/projects`) or is it invisible state? And does the Hebrew/English toggle stay in the navbar on every screen, or move into Settings once the preference is saved to the account? | Stage 2 | **Closed on the mechanism — sub-questions open** |
| D11 | **Subscription data model & quota enforcement** | **Storage shape now designed** and reflected in `docs/database-design.html`: a seeded `plans` catalogue, one `subscriptions` document per period with a **partial unique** index permitting exactly one `active` row per user, a `usagecounters` document per user × metric × week, and a denormalised `users.planCode` read by the authorization layer. Sale values are snapshotted so a catalogue edit cannot rewrite an issued invoice; the weekly counter uses **fixed Monday 00:00 UTC boundaries** and enforces the quota **inside the update filter** (a read-then-write check loses the race). What remains open is **product, not shape**: every limit and price carries `provisional: true`; no payment provider is chosen (`provider.name` is `none` everywhere); what a *read-only* project concretely forbids after a downgrade; and whether the account country selecting `currency` becomes a field on `users` or is derived at checkout. | Stage 2 (screen) · later (billing) | Open — shape settled, product open |
| D12 | **Compiled-JavaScript tracking policy** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-02): track the TypeScript sources, the compiled `.js`, and the build configuration.** A static prototype must open by double-clicking straight after a download — the same reasoning already applied to `docs/database-design.html` on 2026-07-29 — and a grader who clones the repo should not need Node installed to see a screen work. Rejected alternative: gitignore the artifacts and require `npm ci && npm run build`, which is the more canonical practice but makes a fresh clone render broken screens. Consequences: `screens/*.js` removed from `.gitignore` on every branch that had it; `lang.ts`, `validation.ts`, `register.ts`, `tsconfig.json` and `package.json` now live on `develop`; and **all three artifacts are built from the one tracked `tsconfig.json`**, never ad hoc. The cost accepted knowingly: generated files in history, and a rebuild owed before each commit that touches a `.ts`. | — | **Closed** |
| D13 | **Completed-work storage shape** | The **product** model is decided (2026-08-18, below): entries may be free-standing *or* optionally linked to work coordinated on the platform, and only a linked entry whose specific work is complete carries the `Completed on FieldSync` badge. What is **not** decided is where the data lives: a `workentries` collection **vs.** an embedded array on `users` referencing [[fileassets]]; whether a linked entry stores the `task` id, the `project` id, or both, so that a task-scoped entry can be rendered without implying responsibility for the whole project; and whether the badge is recomputed on read (task still complete?) or frozen at link time. Also unresolved: the provisional delegation rule below has to be enforceable by whatever shape is chosen. Nothing has been added to `docs/database-design.html` yet, deliberately. **Refined 2026-08-19:** the badge wording is **`Completed on FieldSync` / `הושלם ב־FieldSync`**, and it appears only where the work the entry represents is itself complete — a finished task inside a running project qualifies, a whole-project entry only once the project completes, work in progress never. **Delegation rule sharpened, and it now cuts the other way from the 2026-08-18 version:** an image from work done through confidential delegation **may** appear, because forbidding it outright was itself a signal; what must never appear is anything that exposes the relationship. Concretely: a **delegate must not receive the badge** for delegated work, no project or task linkage may be published for it, and there is never a "delegated" label of any kind. Any storage shape chosen has to be able to enforce that. ✅ **The entry-type question CLOSED 2026-08-29 by owner decision, and it constrains every shape below.** **A Completed Work entry may be added for work that was not completed inside FieldSync.** Two entry types are legitimate: a **FieldSync-linked** entry, and a **free-standing** entry with no project or task link at all — a valid professional portfolio entry in its own right, including work predating the account. **So no schema and no endpoint may require a link.** `projectId` / `taskId` are optional by rule, validation must accept an entry carrying neither, and **a free-standing entry must never be presented as FieldSync-verified** — the badge would be the platform vouching for work it has no record of. The corollary for the API: the verification flag is **derived server-side from a link the server proved complete**, never accepted from a request body, or a client could claim verification for outside work. This settles what an entry *is*; **it settles nothing about where an entry lives.** The storage question above is untouched and still open, and it is now joined by the one the two entry types add: **what a free-standing entry validates as**, since it has no task to prove completion against. **The React screens already assume this**: `CompletedWorkEntry` carries no project or task reference at all, and the representative data renders one free-standing entry with no badge. | Stage 2/3 | **Entry types closed (2026-08-29) — storage still open** |
| D14 | **Availability status and heavy-equipment selection — both new fields, neither in the chart** | Two fields were added to the profile UI on 2026-08-19 that `docs/database-design.html` does not define, so **no schema was written for either**. **Availability:** a three-state status (`open` / `limited` / `closed`) shown as a dot plus its written label. Open: is it a plain enum on `users`, does it carry an "until" date so a contractor can mark themselves unavailable for a fortnight, and does it feed the browse filter? **Heavy equipment:** selecting `heavy_equipment` as a specialty opens a picker of ten machine types, because a contractor typically operates only part of the category. Open: is this a second array on `users` (`equipment: [String]`), a sub-document keyed by specialty, or a generalisation of "specialty attributes" that other trades would also use later. Decide before either is built. **Availability now appears on three screens** — chosen during registration, editable on the profile, displayed on the profile and on browse result cards, and filterable in the browse rail — so whatever shape is chosen has to serve a filter, not only a display. **Availability's schema was written 2026-08-28** and it is not on [[users]]: it is `companies.availability`, an `open`/`limited`/`closed` enum on the business, because the owner's rule is that an organization decides whether it takes new work and everyone under it derives their status from that — so there is deliberately no per-user copy and no per-user override. **Reaffirmed 2026-08-28:** this field is the organization's **work** availability — whether the business is accepting new work — and it is controlled by the execution contractor / company owner. It must **not** be reinterpreted as the personal availability of each employee. Managing employee availability is a **separate future internal concept with no approved vocabulary**, and it may not reuse or overwrite this field; no enum for it has been invented. ~~What that leaves open is unchanged: whether the status carries an "until" date, how Browse reads it across the join, and the whole heavy-equipment half, for which **still no schema is written**.~~ ***(Superseded 2026-08-29 — the findings of 2026-08-28 close the "until" question and contradict the sufficiency of the shape that was built.)*** **(a) The "until" question is CLOSED: the `limited` state ALWAYS carries its time range.** Verbatim: `זמינות מוגבלת צריכה לכלול טווח זמן` and `כן בעלי מקצוע/קבלני משנה יודעים להגיד עד מתי הם לא זמינים תמיד`. So the schema owes **range fields on the limited state** — it is not an optional refinement, and the C-minimal badge spec shows the range **with** the badge. **(b) Per-work-type availability must be representable.** Verbatim: `כן יש משמעות לזמינות לפי סוג עבודה`. A single business-wide enum **cannot** express a company that is open for one specialty and closed for another, so the built shape is insufficient rather than merely incomplete. The extended shape is **open engineering** — nothing here picks it — but **the requirement is closed**. How Browse reads it across the join stays open, and the **heavy-equipment half is untouched by these findings** and still has no schema. **Added 2026-08-20:** the three labels are **gender-neutral in Hebrew by requirement**, so the enum may never be rendered through a verb that inflects for the contractor's gender. That is a constraint on the *labels*, not on the shape, but it rules out storing display strings on the document. **Added 2026-08-23:** the Personal dashboard puts the status **inside a counterparty chip**, which is a fourth screen and a genuinely new context — a chip has no room for `אין זמינות לקבלת עבודות חדשות כעת`. The prototype therefore renders a **shortened visible label** (`יש זמינות` / `זמינות מוגבלת` / `אין זמינות` · *Available* / *Limited* / *Unavailable*) beside the dot and carries the **full approved wording in a `.sr-only` span**, so the status is never colour-only and assistive tech still hears the approved sentence. The short forms are noun-based and therefore still gender-neutral, but they are **new copy and are not approved** — confirm them or replace them before this ships. The shape question is unaffected; what this adds is that the enum now needs **two label lengths per state**, which is a rendering concern under [D10](#7-open-decisions) rather than a stored one — **now plus the range on `limited`**. **(c) What the findings leave standing, recorded so the closure is not read wider than it is.** **Organization-level control stands** — the contractor's unit *is* the business, which is what put this field on [[companies]] in the first place. **Region stays a general work-area setting and is never availability-by-region**: `לא ניתן לומר אני זמין שבוע רק באזור x אך ניתן לקבוע אזור עבודה בכללי`. The **employee-availability separation stands** — this is the organization's *work* availability, managing employee availability remains a separate future concept with no approved vocabulary, and this field may not be reused for it. The **heavy-equipment half stands untouched**. And the **shortened dashboard chip labels are still not approved** — unrelated to these findings, and confirm-or-replace still applies. **What owes work, in the same sitting per golden rule 3:** `docs/database-design.html`'s [[companies]] entry, the backend company model and the Register endpoint's `default('open')`, the Browse filter and result cards, and the Register availability selector. **(d) The heavy-equipment half is now BUILT, on owner instruction (2026-08-29, owner-QA corrections), in the narrowest of the three shapes this decision named.** `users.heavyEquipment` is a plain array of the ten approved machine codes, chosen because it mirrors `specialtyOther`: both refine one specialty and neither is a filter. **The rule the code enforces is that machines cannot outlive their trade** — the service clears the list whenever the specialties a patch leaves behind do not include `heavy_equipment`, so the two can never disagree however the values arrive; an empty array means the trade is held with no machine named yet. **The vocabulary was not invented** — the ten codes are the ones the approved profile screens have always offered. **What remains open is the shape, not the storage:** the *specialty sub-document* and the *general specialty-attributes* alternatives are untouched, so this is an implementation choice that **awaits ratification** rather than a closure. Nothing filters, sorts or publicly displays it, because none of those was approved. | Stage 2 | **Partially closed (findings 2026-08-28) — the semantics bind the shape; the extended availability shape is open · heavy-equipment storage BUILT 2026-08-29, its shape awaiting ratification** |
| D15 | **Phone visibility on the public profile** | ⚠️ **Renumbered 2026-08-20: this decision was opened as `D13`, which was already taken.** It is now **D15**; every reference to it in this document and in `docs/database-design.html` was repointed in the same commit. `D13` continues to mean *Completed-work storage shape* and nothing else. ✅ **CLOSED as policy (findings 2026-08-24 / 2026-08-25).** ~~Is `users.phone` **public to anyone viewing a profile**, or **withheld until a connection is accepted**? Gating it makes the §1 connection gate mean something and keeps the platform from becoming a sales-spam channel; publishing it matches how contractors actually reach each other on a job site, where a number on a profile is the point.~~ ***(Superseded — the question was a false binary, and its gating rationale died with the [D9](#7-open-decisions) reversal: there is no §1 connection gate for it to make mean something.)*** **The validated policy, in four parts.** **(1) The personal phone is never displayed, anywhere** — `לא מציגים טלפון אישי אף פעם`. A profile shows **email plus the office phone and/or the business phone**, if either exists. **(2) Within a shared project, exactly three roles see a professional's number:** the **GC**, the `מנהל עבודה` and the `מנהל אתרים` — and no other subcontractor on that project does. **(3) Under a Work Commitment where a task has already been created, visibility is MUTUAL between the two committed parties** — `שני הצדדים המחויבים יכולים לראות זה את הטלפון של זה`. **(4) Every case not covered by (2) or (3) is the professional's own control alone** — they decide whether to expose their number. Voluntary in-chat sharing remains something people simply do. **Residue is implementation only, and one part of it is already paid.** Still owed: the self-control setting's shape; the **encoded matrix** in the viewer-aware serializer — including how `מנהל עבודה` and `מנהל אתרים` map onto app membership and granted authority, and **where the task-existence check under the Work Commitment runs**; and the gated states on four screens. **Already paid — the convergence worth recording:** [D27](#7-open-decisions) built exactly the two fields this policy needs, `companies.officePhone` and `users.businessPhone`, on two different documents **with no fallback between them**, so the schema half of the residue landed before the policy that required it. The `.fact__value--gated` state serves the new policy unchanged, and the prototype's displayed number is still a placeholder rather than the decision. **Connection state confers nothing.** | Stage 2 (settings / profile) | **Closed as policy — implementation residue open** |
| D16 | **Does a 404 also answer "exists, but not for you"?** | When a viewer requests a resource they are not permitted to see — a delegated task, a connection-gated profile — does the server answer **404 (indistinguishable from missing)** or **403 (explicitly forbidden)**? This is a privacy question, not an HTTP-semantics one. A 403 confirms the resource *exists*, which is precisely what [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) protects: a GC probing task URLs could detect a delegate's work from the response code alone, with no access to any content. The same argument already settled the login screen's unified *invalid credentials* error (2026-06-07). Against it: 404 for a permission failure is misleading to a legitimate user who simply lost access, and it makes support harder. **Parked deliberately by the owner (2026-08-20)** — the 404 screen was built with copy neutral enough to serve either answer, so nothing has to be rewritten when this closes. Decide when the viewer-aware serializer is actually built, since that is the layer that would implement it. | Stage 5 (serializer) | Open **✅ CLOSED (owner, 2026-08-30) and implemented: 404 wherever existence must stay undisclosed** — with the owner's explicit caution not to generalise it blindly to every authorisation failure. Built and asserted across Projects, Project members, the Project Dashboard, My Tasks and Task Detail: an unreachable resource and a non-existent one return **byte-identical** bodies, tested by comparing the two responses directly. The neutral 404 screen copy needs no rewrite, exactly as it was designed not to. |
| D17 | **How a connection edge is torn down** ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-29 by owner decision, and built the same day** | Three controls on My Network have no state behind them, and they are all one question. `connections.status` is `pending \| accepted \| declined \| blocked` — there is no `removed` and no `withdrawn`, so **removing an accepted connection** and **cancelling a request you sent** are both undefined. Options: **delete the document** (frees the unique `pair` index so the two can connect again later, but erases that they ever were connected — and [[auditentries]] style history is not kept for connections); **add a `removed` / `withdrawn` state** (keeps the history, but then every "are these two connected" check must exclude it, and the unique `pair` index means a fresh request has to reuse the existing row rather than insert); or **reuse `declined`**, which is the trap and should probably be rejected — `declined` is kept deliberately *"so the same person cannot re-request in a loop"*, so routing an ordinary disconnect through it would silently make it permanent. That same note surfaces a third, previously unstated consequence: **declining a request is already irreversible for the requester**, and no screen says so. Entangled with [D9](#7-open-decisions), which asks what happens to an existing thread on disconnect — which is exactly why the removal dialog on the screen claims only that the *gate* closes and says nothing about the conversation. ✅ **Closed as follows.** `status` is `pending | accepted | declined | removed | withdrawn`. `accepted → removed`; a `pending` request the sender abandons → `withdrawn`; a refusal stays **`declined`**, which is a **distinct historical state and explicitly NOT permanent** — the same pair may ask again, and the request reuses the one unique pair row, rewriting the direction rather than adding a second edge. **A refusal never creates a Block**; [D19](#7-open-decisions)'s [[blocks]] domain is the permanent mechanism. Relationship history is preserved because nothing is deleted | Stage 2 | **CLOSED 2026-08-29 · built · awaiting owner manual QA** |
| D18 | **Where a dismissed cleanup item is remembered** | *Things to clean up* on the Personal dashboard lists **contract exchanges that were never logged as a task** — an [[messages]] document carrying an `agreementForm` whose `createdTask` is still `null`, which the sparse index `{ 'agreementForm.status': 1 }` already serves. Reading the list costs nothing new. **Dismissing one has nowhere to be written.** The approved behaviour is that dismissal is **permanent for that specific exchange**, with no reminder and no recovery flow, and `agreementForm.status` is `draft \| sent \| accepted \| declined` — none of which means *"I have decided not to open a task for this"*. Options: **a `cleanupDismissedBy: [ObjectId]` array on the message** (per-viewer, which is what the rule actually needs, since two people see the same exchange and either may dismiss it independently — but it puts a per-user list on a collection that is otherwise append-only and never updated); **a fifth `agreementForm.status` value such as `abandoned`** (cheapest to query, but it is a *shared* status, so one party dismissing it would silently remove it from the other party's list); or **a separate small `dismissals` collection** keyed by user × message (cleanest separation and the only one that leaves [[messages]] untouched, at the cost of a join or a second query on a dashboard widget that should be cheap). **Reusing `declined` must be rejected for the same reason [D17](#7-open-decisions) rejects it there:** `declined` already means the other party turned the agreement down, and routing a private housekeeping dismissal through it would rewrite a fact about the negotiation. The control **ships anyway** on the prototype, per the standing rule that a gap in the model is not a reason to drop a control. Note the second-order question nobody has answered: whether dismissing should also be possible from the Inbox, where the same exchange is visible. | Stage 3 (dashboard) · Stage 6 (inbox) | Open |
| D19 | **Which side of a blocked edge did the blocking** ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-29 by owner decision, and built the same day** | Opened 2026-08-23 by the third Browse review, which established that a contractor the viewer has blocked **must not appear in Browse at all**. That is a query rule, and `connections` cannot express it: the document carries `requester`, `recipient`, `pair`, `status`, `requestedAt` and `respondedAt`, and **none of them records who blocked**. `requester` means *who sent the connection request* — if A and B were connected and A then blocks B, the row flips to `blocked` with `requester` still pointing at whoever originally asked, which may be B. The schema can therefore answer *"are these two blocked"* but not *"did A block B"*, and the exclusion rule is directional by definition. Options: **a `blockedBy: ObjectId` field**, set when the status becomes `blocked` and cleared on unblock (smallest change, and it makes the browse filter a single indexed clause — but it is a field that is meaningful for exactly one of four enum values); **overload `requester` at block time** by rewriting it to the blocker (no new field, and it is the trap — it destroys the record of who originally asked, which the [§7 D17](#7-open-decisions) teardown discussion needs); or **split blocking out of `connections` entirely** into a `blocks` collection keyed by blocker and target (cleanest semantically, since a block is not really a connection state, and it is the only shape that lets A block B with no prior edge without inventing a fake request — at the cost of a second lookup on every directory query and a `status` enum that loses a value). Entangled with [D17](#7-open-decisions), which is already asking what other states the enum needs. The second-order question nobody has answered: whether a block should also hide **the blocker** from the person they blocked, which every option above can serve but none implies. ✅ **Closed as follows: a dedicated [[blocks]] collection, not a `connections` status.** A block needs no connection to exist. `blockerUserId` and `blockedUserId` are stored separately, so the platform always knows who acted; the *effect* is mutual exclusion from Browse and from each other's public profile, and the direction is **never disclosed to the blocked person**. Messaging, when it exists, must respect an active block | Stage 2 | **CLOSED 2026-08-29 · built · awaiting owner manual QA** |
| D20 | **What a project card's pending-action count actually counts** | Opened 2026-08-23 by My projects. The denormalised counter `projects.stats.openProposalCount` carries a field note saying it exists *"so the `My projects` cards can render pending-action badges without an aggregation per card"* — so the schema already nominated a source for this badge, and **it answers a different question from the one the screen asks.** It counts open [[rescheduleproposals]] **on the project**, regardless of who has to respond; the screen needs what is waiting on **this viewer**. The schema's own sample document makes the gap concrete: Rothschild 42 carries `openProposalCount: 2` while only one of those items names the viewer as respondent, so the documented source would render **2** where the truth for this viewer is **1**. Two members of one project would also see the same badge, a general contractor with nothing to answer would see one, and it counts no project invitations and no agreement forms. Options: **relabel the badge to the project fact it already holds** ("2 open proposals in this project") — free, but the same phrase would then mean one thing on Home and another here, and the Personal dashboard's definition of a pending action is *what others need from the viewer*; **compute it viewer-scoped**, which is cheaper than it looks, because the multikey index `{ 'items.respondent': 1, status: 1 }` already exists for the dashboard's own Pending actions list and this screen can group that same result by `project` — at the cost that `stats.openProposalCount` loses its stated justification and becomes a project-dashboard counter only; or **keep both**, one per screen, and accept two counters that can disagree the first time a write path updates one and not the other. Unresolved regardless of shape: **what counts as a project-level pending action at all.** The dashboard's widget mixes reschedule proposals, project invitations, agreement forms and connection requests, and only the first two are project-scoped. The prototype counts **proposals only** and represents the invitation as the Accept / Decline control on the card instead, so nothing is counted twice — that is a rendering choice made to avoid double-counting, not a decision. | Stage 3 (projects / dashboard) | Open |
| D21 | **What a task row's counterparty is, and what a delegate is shown in its place** | Opened 2026-08-23 by My Tasks. Approved screen **#15 does not mention a counterparty**; the owner's build instruction requires every row to show one. Nothing in the model is *named* the counterparty, so the screen had to choose a field, and it renders **`tasks.createdBy`** — whoever opened the work, which is the general contractor on a project task and the other party on a standalone job that arrived through an agreement form (`originMessage`). Two things stay unresolved. **(a) There is often no counterparty at all.** When `createdBy` is the viewer — a task they opened in a project they own, or a solo job they created for themselves — the slot has nobody in it. Five of the eleven prototype rows are in that state and render `נוצרה על ידכם` / *Created by you*, which is **wording, not a decision**; the alternatives are to drop the line entirely on such a row, or to name the project owner instead on a project task. **(b) `createdBy` must never reach a delegate.** [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) makes the party above invisible to the delegate, and the schema's own field note on `createdBy` says exactly that: *"Hidden from a delegate — this field alone would expose the party above."* So a delegate's own My Tasks **cannot** render this line from `createdBy`; it has to resolve to the **delegator**, which is a different field (`assignee`) for a different viewer. That makes the counterparty **viewer-relative**, and nothing in the model records it as such today. **PARTIALLY CLOSED (findings 2026-08-28) — the identity half is settled; only the rendering mechanism is open.** The delegate's counterparty **is the delegator, never the GC**, and the **GC-facing performer is the delegator**. Verbatim: `ה-delegate יודע מראש שהוא קבלן משנה/בעל מקצוע של ה-delegator` and `רק למי שהעביר את העבודה אמורה להיות גישה למי מבצע בפועל`. So the question *"what should a delegate see in that slot"* is answered, and it confirms what [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) already required. **What stays open is the mechanism** — which of the three options below carries it — **and sub-case (a)**, the rows with no counterparty at all, where `נוצרה על ידכם` / *Created by you* remains **wording, not a decision**. Options: **compute it in the viewer-aware serializer** as *"the party this viewer answers to"* — correct, and that layer already has to decide it, at the cost of a derived value no index serves; **render `createdBy` and omit the line for a delegate** — cheapest, and it silently shows a delegate less than everyone else sees, which is the kind of asymmetry the privacy model is supposed to hide rather than advertise; or **store an explicit `counterparty` reference on the task** — queryable, but it duplicates a fact `createdBy` / `assignee` / `delegate` already carry between them, giving three places to disagree. Entangled with the Stage-5 serializer and therefore with [D16](#7-open-decisions). The prototype is unaffected either way: every row it renders shows the viewer as an ordinary assignee, which is correct under all three options. | Stage 3 (screen) · Stage 5 (serializer) | **Partially closed (findings 2026-08-28) — identity settled, rendering mechanism and sub-case (a) open** |
| D22 | **Working-calendar storage — the contractor default and the project override** | Opened 2026-08-23 by Create / edit project. **The domain model is decided** and recorded in [§11](#11-decision-log): a main contractor keeps one reusable **default working calendar**, a new project **inherits** it, everyone taking part in a project is scheduled by **that project's** calendar, and a project may carry its own changes to the inherited one. **Nothing in the schema holds any of it.** [[users]] has no calendar field, [[projects]] has no calendar field, and [[tasks]] carries `startDate` / `endDate` with no notion of a non-working day — so *"two working days later"* is not computable anywhere in the model today. Every part of the storage question is genuinely open. **Where the contractor defines the default:** the profile, Settings, or a screen of its own that no approved list contains. **How the default is stored:** a seven-value weekday map on [[users]], a bitmask, or a `calendars` collection referenced by id so two projects can share one document. **How the project override is stored:** a full copy on [[projects]], which is trivial to read and silently stops tracking the default the moment the contractor changes it; a sparse diff, which stays small and needs a resolution step on every read; or a reference to a second calendar document, which is the only shape that lets two projects share an override. **How one-off exceptional dates are represented at all:** a weekly pattern cannot express a holiday eve, and holidays, Chol HaMoed and similar periods **vary by contractor**, so a single shared holiday table would be wrong for some of them by construction. **Where holiday data comes from.** **Whether changing the contractor default reaches existing projects** — ~~it must not silently move dates inside a running project~~ ***(refined 2026-08-29: not "must not move". Moves are **approval-gated**, and then they **cascade**.)*** The propagation path itself is still unchosen; what is settled is that nothing moves silently. ~~And **what a calendar change does to already-scheduled tasks and their dependencies**, which is [D2](#7-open-decisions)'s question arriving from a different direction.~~ ***(Superseded — answered below.)*** **The behaviour set is CLOSED (findings 2026-08-24), and the open list above overstated what is open.** Mid-project calendar changes **move task dates — including work already started — only after the affected professionals' approval**, and the change **drives the cascade** to every affected task. A **full history of calendar changes is kept**. **Hours** are part of the calendar: 7:00–16:00 is the common default, with **after-16:00 and night work as exceptions requiring the professionals' approval**. **Exception scoping and validity windows** are closed — project / task / professional / single date / whole period, each with a **preset window that is displayed and applies automatically** (see [D23](#7-open-decisions)). **Holidays and Shabbat are the one fixed constant and are set by sector** (`מגזר`), with Friday and `חול המועד` varying per contractor and **no work-type distinction** for what may run on those days. And the **conflict flow** is closed: when the new calendar lands on a date a professional is already committed elsewhere, the GC (or an explicitly authorized `מנהל אתרים`) **proposes** a date, the sub **approves or counters**, and if the GC rejects the counter `העבודה נופלת`. The prototype renders the inherited state, the customised state and the return to the default, and **stores none of it**: it is markup plus checkbox state with nothing behind it. **What is genuinely still open is storage, and only storage:** where the contractor's reusable default lives; whether a project **copies, diffs or references** it; the **exception object's stored shape**, now that hours and validity windows are required inside it; **holiday sourcing per sector**; and the **mechanics of default-edit propagation** — approval-gated by rule, but with no chosen path. The create-project panel's *"weekly pattern only"* note stays true. | Stage 3 (storage) · Stage 4 (arithmetic) | **Behaviour closed (findings 2026-08-24) — storage open** |
| D23 | **Schedule-exception requests — who may ask, what one covers, and where it goes** | Opened 2026-08-23 alongside [D22](#7-open-decisions), and **its authority half is decided rather than open**: a schedule exception may be approved by the **Main Contractor / CEO**, or by a person **explicitly delegated the authority to approve schedule exceptions**; when an authorised delegate approves within the authority they were granted, that approval is **final**, and the request must **not** afterwards be sent to the Main Contractor / CEO for an unnecessary second approval. ✅ **CLOSED as policy (findings 2026-08-24 / 2026-08-25) — storage rides [D22](#7-open-decisions).** ~~Everything around it is open. **Who may request** one — any project member, only the party whose work it is, or only a `gc`. **What a single request covers** — one date on one task, a range, or a whole project period. **Where the request goes and what is notified**, which is entangled with [D3](#7-open-decisions).~~ ***(Superseded — all three were answered.)*** **Requester:** every `קבלן משנה` / `בעל מקצוע` may submit a specific exception request **for themself only, never for another**, and **in both directions** — to work a day that is not a working day, and to **not** work a working day. **Coverage:** the whole project, one task, one professional, a single date, or a whole period — with a **preset validity window that is displayed to the subcontractors and applies automatically**. **Routing and the notification matrix:** the request **travels with its change history**; **only the authorized approver may modify it** before approving, and **every modification routes back through the submitting professional**; if the request **affects nobody else, only the approver receives it**; a **rejection reaches only the submitter**; and on **approval, a new derived request is sent to each affected professional**, each carrying **only what is relevant to them**, and **only after** the original was approved. And the part with nowhere to live: **delegated approval authority is not expressible in the model.** [[memberships]] carries a per-project `role` and nothing else — there is no field saying *this person may approve schedule exceptions*, no record of who granted it, and no bounds on it, so *"within their granted authority"* cannot be evaluated and the [[auditentries]] line proving an approval was **final** rather than pending cannot be written either. Note that this is a **second and different sense of delegation** from [D7](#7-open-decisions): D7 fixes *who does the work* and is deliberately capped at one hop, and it says nothing about *who may approve* — so the depth cap does not automatically apply here, and whether it should is part of this decision. **No exception workflow was built on Create / edit project**, on instruction, and nothing on that screen contradicts the authority model above. **What genuinely stays open, now that the workflow is closed:** the **model-expressibility gap** — [[memberships]] still carries no grant fields, so *"within their granted authority"* remains unevaluable and the [[auditentries]] line proving an approval was final still cannot be written; the **approval-chain depth question** — the findings named the grantable roles, not a depth, so whether D7's one-hop cap applies here is still unanswered; and **storage, which rides [D22](#7-open-decisions)**. The **finality rule stands exactly as written** — an authorized delegate's approval within their granted authority ends the matter, and no redundant second approval follows. | Stage 4 | **Closed as policy — model expressibility, chain depth and storage open** |
| D24 | **What Delete Project does** | Opened 2026-08-23 by Create / edit project, which **renders the control and deliberately claims nothing about the behaviour**. **PARTIALLY CLOSED (findings 2026-08-28) — the domain layer is settled and it answers the "is deletion allowed" sub-question.** Verbatim: `לפני שהתחילו עבודה – ניתן להגיע למצב שהפרויקט בוטל, אחרי תחילת עבודה לא`. So: **cancellation exists pre-start only**; **after work starts a pause / freeze state exists and deletion does not**; and **the GC notifies the participating professionals**. ~~Nothing is decided: not hard delete versus soft delete versus archive, not a retention period, not whether the action is reversible~~ ***(Superseded in part — whether deletion is allowed while active is now answered: it is not.)*** **What stays open is the record question, for a pre-start cancelled project only:** which lifecycle state it takes, and the treatment of everything it touches — the project's [[tasks]], its [[memberships]], its [[fileassets]], its [[threads]] and [[messages]], its [[rescheduleproposals]] or its [[auditentries]]. Two constraints already exist elsewhere and **neither is a decision**: [[auditentries]] is append-only and snapshots the actor's name precisely so history survives a removal, which is the same argument that pushed [D8](#7-open-decisions) toward soft-delete for users and applies here with equal force; and `projects.status` already carries **`archived`**, so an archive path exists in the enum with no rule anywhere saying deletion should use it. That is also the trap, and it is the one [D17](#7-open-decisions) and [D18](#7-open-decisions) both name: **reusing a nearby status because it is nearby.** `archived` today means *this project is finished and put away*, which is a statement about the work rather than about a deletion, and routing a delete through it would rewrite that meaning for every project already carrying it. One further question nobody has asked: whether a project with members can be deleted **by its owner alone**, given that the other parties' record of what was agreed lives inside it — the same objection [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) already records against a mutual conversation delete. The prototype's confirmation dialog states only that the behaviour is undecided, in both languages — and it may **later** state the pre-start rule, once the record semantics close. **The archived-reuse trap and the owner-alone-deletion question both stand unchanged.** | Stage 3 | **Partially closed (findings 2026-08-28) — cancellation is pre-start only; the record semantics stay open** |
| D25 | **Terms and Privacy: whether the screens exist, and how acceptance is recorded** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-28) on the recording question; the standing constraint survives it.** Opened 2026-08-24 by the Landing page, which had nothing legitimate to point a footer link at. It noted a second-order question nobody had asked: *"the register screen collects an email and a password today and links to no terms of any kind."* That is the half now decided. **`acceptedTerms` must be persisted, not merely validated.** Register requires it to be `true` and now **records the acceptance**: `users.termsAcceptances[]`, each entry carrying the **Terms version** and the **timestamp**. A boolean alone was explicitly ruled out — it cannot say *what* was agreed to once the Terms change, which is exactly what the record has to prove later. The boolean itself is **not** stored, since it carries nothing the dated entry does not. The array is **appended to, never overwritten**, so agreeing to a later version does not erase proof of the earlier one. **The version comes from the server's `TERMS_VERSION` config, never from the request body** — the server knows which version it is serving and a client could claim any value. **The owner-stated constraint from 2026-08-24 is unchanged and still binds:** if Terms and Privacy are added, they are **two separate screens**, never one combined `Legal` screen. **What this does not do, stated plainly rather than implied:** **no Terms or Privacy document has been written**, no screen exists, and `register.html` still links both to `href="#"`. So the version currently recorded is the honest placeholder `draft`, and the deployment sets a real one the moment a document is published. Whether the client should also echo the version it actually rendered is worth deciding then; today there is nothing to echo. | — | **Closed — recording settled; the documents themselves still do not exist** |
| D26 | **How a contractor discovers confidential delegation without the party above learning it exists** | Opened 2026-08-25 by the removal of the delegation copy from `login.html`. [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) constraint 5 forbids naming the feature on any surface a non-delegate can read — which closes the leak and immediately opens the opposite problem: **a capability nobody can find is a capability nobody uses**, and [§1](#1-product-overview) calls delegation field-confirmed as critical. Both requirements bind at once and they pull against each other: a contractor entitled to delegate must be able to learn that they can, and the party it is hidden from must not learn that the mechanism exists, who may use it, or how it works. Nothing is decided. **Where it surfaces** — inside a task the viewer is assigned, in a collaborator or connection context, in a help surface reachable only when signed in, at first use, or somewhere no current screen covers. **What triggers it** — every assigned task, only a task the viewer cannot meet, or an explicit action the viewer goes looking for. **Who qualifies**, which the model cannot express today: [[memberships]] carries a per-project `role` and nothing that says whether this viewer may delegate this task, so *"show it only to someone who may use it"* has no condition behind it. And the hardest part: **whether any in-product surface can satisfy both halves at once.** The same account is a GC on one project and a sub on another — [§1](#1-product-overview)'s founding rule — so the two audiences are the same people, and any rule keyed to a *person* rather than to a *task and a viewer* leaks by construction. Note that help text, empty states and notification copy all count as surfaces, and a screenshot or a shared screen moves any of them to the wrong reader. **Status of the surfaces, 2026-08-25:** the public disclosure was removed from `landing.html` (`3a9fd52`) and from `login.html` (`aedca18`) **without a replacement explanation**, per owner instruction, so the leak is closed and the discovery gap is now total — **no screen in the project tells an eligible contractor that this capability exists.** That is the state this decision has to resolve, and **no replacement Landing copy may be written before it closes**. | Stage 5 (privacy layer) · sooner if the Landing page is corrected first | Open |
| D27 | **Whether Register collects business phone numbers at all, and how they relate to `users.phone`** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-28).** Opened 2026-08-26 by two optional phone fields added to `register.html`, which collected something the data model did not have. All three parts are now answered. **(a) Do these become stored fields — yes, and on two different documents.** `companies.officePhone` is the business office line, because several people under one contractor share it; `users.businessPhone` is the individual professional's own. **(b) Does Register remain the place to ask — yes.** Both belong in Register, both stay **optional**, and they stay **independent**: a contractor may supply one, the other, both, or neither, and neither becomes required because the other is present. Verified across all four combinations. **(c) Relationship to `users.phone` — three separate concepts, and no fallback in any direction.** `users.phone` remains the personal / login number collected in onboarding step 3, and **Register still never asks for it**. Because the office number lives on a *different document*, a fallback is not merely forbidden but **unreachable** by an accidental edit. **No validation rule was invented** — each field carries a length bound and still **no format rule**, because what counts as a valid number here has never been approved and this row did not decide it. This closes the storage and collection questions only. It says nothing about **visibility**: which of the three a public profile shows is [D15](#7-open-decisions), still open. | — | **Closed** |
| D28 | **Where a company name is stored, and whether it belongs on Register at all** | Opened 2026-08-27 by the **required** Company name field added to `register.html` on owner instruction (`15acaa3`). **The screen now blocks account creation on a value the data model cannot hold.** The string `company` appears nowhere in [[users]] in [§4](#4-data-model) and nowhere in `docs/database-design.html`. This is [D27](#7-open-decisions) one step worse: the phone fields are optional, so a contractor can finish signup while the schema question stays parked, whereas **this one cannot be deferred past the first real registration**. Undecided: **(a) What shape does it take?** A `company` string on [[users]], or a field on the contractor profile, or its own collection if a company is ever to have more than one member — the last matters because the platform's actors are equal users and nothing yet says whether two contractors can share an employer. **(b) Should it be required?** It is required today by owner decision, but the profile wizard exists precisely to move detail out of signup, and Full name + Company name are now the two fields a contractor must supply before an account exists. **(c) What is its relationship to the displayed identity?** Browse-contractors searches `שם או חברה` / *Name or company* — that placeholder is where the terminology came from, and it implies the value is searchable and public, which nothing has approved. **No schema change was made** at the time, deliberately. **(a) was decided 2026-08-28 by the owner and built by the Register backend; (b) and (c) stay open.** The answer to (a) is a **[[companies]] collection**, not a string on each person: a User is an individual and a Company is the business, and the model has to hold a Main Contractor operating through one, the `מנהל אתרים` / Construction Managers and `מנהל עבודה` / Site Managers working under it, and a specialty contractor employing another contractor **of the same trade** — none of which a descriptive string can express. The link is a **[[companymemberships]] document**, not fields on the person — revised the same week, because an owner may invite an employee **before that employee has an account**, and an invitation with no user cannot be a property of a user. **Ownership is `standing: 'owner'` on that row and is recorded exactly once**, since [[companies]] deliberately has no owner field. **Trade never implies ownership.** **(b) and (c) were approved 2026-08-28 and this row is now closed.** **(b) `companyName` is required** for owner / independent-contractor registration — which is what `register.html` had assumed since `15acaa3` and what the Register endpoint's JOI schema already enforced, so approval confirmed the behaviour rather than changing it. **(c) `companyName` is public, and searchable by users.** That retroactively justifies the terminology's origin: the browse-contractors search placeholder `שם או חברה` / *Name or company* had implied both, and neither had been approved until now. **This settles `companyName` and nothing else, deliberately.** It grants no visibility to `companies.officePhone`, which stays bound to the still-open [D15](#7-open-decisions) question of what a public profile shows; it does not make a company a browsable entity, give it a profile, a route or a screen; and **it does not make the name unique** — two unrelated businesses may legitimately share one, and searchability does not change that. **No index was added.** Nothing in the backend reads or searches companies yet, and the project's standing practice is that an index lands with the query that needs it. When search is built it will have to pass a Hebrew collation, for the same reason [§3.2](#32-data-model-entity-index--full-design-in-the-companion-html) already records for user names — default byte order is not alphabetical in Hebrew. ~~One thing this row exposed and does not resolve, because it belongs elsewhere: **there is no company-invitation flow built**, so Register can only ever create an owner — the model can express the whole employee path but no endpoint walks it yet.~~ ✅ **Built 2026-08-29 (`19ec45b`).** The owner's invitation endpoint, the matching claim inside Register and the approval routes all exist, so Register creates an owner *or* claims an employee's seat. **The searchability this row approved is what made the match safe to build**: the name is public and therefore proves nothing, so it only narrows the search for a seat somebody already opened, and because the name is deliberately **not unique** every company holding it is searched and more than one match is refused rather than guessed at. | — | **Closed** |
| D29 | **The employee vocabulary the Register model carries** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-28), with one standing rule that outlives it.** Opened the same day by the Register backend, which coined three pieces of naming while implementing approved rules about employees, invitations and permissions. **(a) `pending_company_approval` — approved** as the membership status for an employee who has completed registration and is waiting for company-owner approval. It was coined because nothing existing covered it: `memberships.status` is project-scoped and `connections.status` is a different graph. **(b) `standing: 'owner' | 'employee'` — approved**, with its meaning pinned: standing is the person's **organizational standing within the company**, and it is *not* their professional position (`companyPosition`) and *not* their permissions (`permissions`). Three fields, three questions, none derived from another. **(c) Four permission codes — approved, and the list is closed at four.** `project.create`, `task.create`, `company.manage` and `company.invite_employees` each name a capability that was **already approved as an owner default**: create projects, create tasks, manage their own company, add/invite employees. Public Register grants an owner all four at signup; an **employee receives none of them by default**. *(This row was briefly recorded on 2026-08-28 as rejecting all four and shipping an empty vocabulary. That was an over-correction and is retracted — see the Decision Log. What was never approved is a **broader** vocabulary for capabilities that do not exist, which is a different thing from the owner defaults that do.)* **The standing rule this leaves, and the reason the row is worth keeping:** the code list is **closed**. A fifth code may be added only when the capability it names is implemented and approved — never ahead of it — and it needs its own approval at that point. The rule is enforced rather than described: `CompanyPermission` is derived from the list, so an unapproved code is a compile error. Owner and employee defaults remain two separate decisions that may differ; an employee never inherits an owner's set, and neither set may be derived from `companyPosition`. ~~**Nothing reads `permissions` yet** — none of the four capabilities is built~~ ✅ **`company.invite_employees` is now read (2026-08-29, `19ec45b`)**: it is the single check guarding all four employee-management routes, and it is read from the caller's own **active** membership — never inferred from `standing` and never from `companyPosition`. The other three codes are still unread, and the closed-at-four rule is untouched. The row's own point held in practice: because the defaults were written at signup, the first capability to be implemented needed no backfill and no owner was silently left without authority. | — | **Closed** |
| D30 | **Where Register lands once the account exists** | ✅ **CLOSED (2026-08-29) by owner decision. The approved navigation flow is `Register → Login → Personal dashboard`:** a successful registration navigates to **Login**, and a successful login navigates to the **Personal dashboard** (approved screen #5). Opened 2026-08-28 by the React Register integration. **This closure supersedes two things this row previously recorded as settled, and both reversals are deliberate.** *(1)* It stated that a new account *"arrives already authenticated and no second Login is required"* — the approved flow **does** require a second Login, so signing up no longer carries the person into the application. *(2)* It stated that the profile wizard is *"part of the Register flow itself"* with *"no separate wizard screen to redirect to"*. The approved flow names three screens and the wizard is not among them, so **where the wizard sits is no longer answered by this row**. **One consequence was decided with it, and one is left open.** **Decided 2026-08-29: Register stops issuing tokens.** Signing up creates the account and nothing more; **authentication happens solely at Login**. The pair Register currently returns is a session the client has no use for — the person authenticates again seconds later — and issuing a credential that is immediately discarded is worth removing rather than tolerating. **The code does not match this yet**: the endpoint still answers `201` with an Access Token and a `Set-Cookie` Refresh Token, and the React client still stores the Access Token. Both are recorded as owed in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks); **nothing was changed in this documentation pass.** Left open: **where the profile wizard now sits.** This row had fixed it as part of the Register flow, the approved flow does not name it, and it still has **nowhere to save to** — the API mounts `/health`, `/health-auth` and `/auth` and nothing else, and `features/users/` remains a model and a repository with **no controller and no route**. `profileComplete` stays on the wire from Login, and still nothing reads it. **Two of the three owed changes landed 2026-08-29 (`0911948`, `2c225ca`), and they are the client ones.** **(b)** the React client no longer stores the token — `useRegisterForm` calls nothing, and `RegisterResponse` dropped `accessToken` so no code there can consume one; the success panel was deleted with the behaviour it described. **(c)** Register now navigates to `/login`, which it could not do before Login was a React screen. **(a) is still owed and is a server change:** `registration.service.ts` still issues the pair and `handleRegister` still sets the cookie, so `POST /api/auth/register` answers `201` with an `accessToken` and a `Set-Cookie` — verified against the running API, not inferred. Until it lands a browser that registers holds a Refresh cookie for a session nothing uses. **The order this row prescribed was followed**: (b) before (a), so no deployed client is ever storing a token the server has stopped sending. **All three landed 2026-08-29.** (a) shipped in `bc6f032`: `registration.service.ts` no longer takes a token-pair dependency at all, so signing up cannot open a session even by a later accident, and `handleRegister` answers `201 { user }` with no `Set-Cookie`. Verified against the running API rather than read off the source. **This row is now fully paid on both sides and owes nothing.** | Stage 2 | **Closed — and fully implemented 2026-08-29** |

## 8. Known issues & risks

~~**Merging `fix/owner-qa-corrections.react` as it stands would resurrect every static prototype the cleanup deleted (found 2026-08-29).**~~
**CORRECTED the same day — it does not. The claim confused a two-dot diff with what a merge does,
and the difference is the whole point.** `fix/owner-qa-corrections.react` was cut from `68dcc04`,
which is before the static deletion at `ced43ba`, so `git diff develop fix/owner-qa-corrections.react`
(**two dots**) does list `browse-contractors.html` + `.css`, `login.html` + `.css`,
`register.html` + `.css`, `edit-profile.html` + `.css`, `my-profile.html` + `.css`,
`forgot-password.html`, `reset-password.html`, `profile.css` and `validation.ts` / `.js` as
**additions**. That diff answers *"how do these two trees differ"*, which is not the question a merge
asks. **A merge is three-way**: its base is the branch point, and for a file that one side deleted
and the other never touched, **the deletion wins**. The branch never touched `Frontend/screens/` at
all — `git diff --name-only 68dcc04 fix/owner-qa-corrections.react -- Frontend/screens/` is **empty**.
**Verified by actually doing it** on a throwaway branch off current `develop`: after
`git merge fix/owner-qa-corrections.react`, `Frontend/screens/` held only `lang.js`, `lang.ts`,
`my-network.css`, `my-network.html`, `personal-dashboard.css` and `personal-dashboard.html` — every
deleted prototype stayed deleted. The throwaway branch was removed and **neither correction branch
was merged**. **So no rebase is owed and no follow-up deletion commit is owed**, and the advice to
take one of those two paths was wrong. Nothing was done to either branch — they are unmerged and
awaiting owner QA, and §0A forbids reproducing their work elsewhere. **The lesson worth keeping: a
two-dot diff is not a merge preview. When a merge's outcome matters, run the merge on a throwaway
branch and look, rather than reading a diff and inferring.**
**CLOSED 2026-08-30 — the real merges happened and confirmed it.** `fix/owner-qa-corrections.backend`
and `fix/owner-qa-corrections.react` are now merged into `develop` (`929adf1`, `9f93e0e`). The React
merge raised **no conflict in any source file**, and afterwards `Frontend/screens/` still holds exactly
its six retained files. Nothing was resurrected and root `screens/` was not recreated. This item needs
no further action.

> Awareness items to keep in front of us. Not all are decisions — some are just traps to avoid.

- **Current implementation dependencies after the 2026-08-29 integration (`13a7e0e`).** Each is a thing the code is
  waiting on, not an open product question — except where marked.

  - **Settings screen is not built.** The account menu carries a Settings entry, deliberately **disabled**. It cannot be
    pointed anywhere: `/settings` is not a route and the catch-all sends unknown paths to `/login`, which would log an
    authenticated person out.
  - **The navbar chip shows initials even when the person uploaded a photo**, because `SessionUser` — what Login and
    `/auth/me` return — carries no `avatarUrl`. Cosmetic, and the fix is a payload field rather than a screen change.
  - **Minimum flexibility cannot be built** until [D6](#7-open-decisions)'s arithmetic exists. The approved static Browse
    screen has the control; React deliberately does not. **This one is a genuine open decision, not just a dependency.**
  - **Browse's Sort control was approved on the prototype and had not been migrated.** Found on 2026-08-29 while
    checking that nothing unique was stranded in the static files before deleting them, and recorded here **before**
    `browse-contractors.html` was removed. The prototype offered four options — `רלוונטיות` / *Relevance*,
    `דירוג — מהגבוה לנמוך` / *Rating — high to low*, `גמישות — מהגבוה לנמוך` / *Flexibility — high to low*, and
    `הכי קרוב אליי` / *Nearest to me* — and its own comment called them *visual; real sorting wired in React Stage 2*.
    ~~**Three are being restored**~~ — **built on `fix/owner-qa-corrections.*`, and it is two of the four, not three.**
    **Relevance** is the discovery order Browse already returned, and is the default. **Rating — high to low** is a real
    aggregation sort: there is no `ratingSummary` field to read, so the average is computed in the pipeline from
    [[ratings]], and the cursor carries that computed key so paging keeps the order it started in. An unrated contractor
    sorts **below** every rated one on a distinct key rather than as a zero, which is the same rule the minimum-rating
    filter already follows. **Two are deliberately not built, for different reasons.** *Flexibility* is
    [D6](#7-open-decisions) — the same block as the filter, and an order would be inventing the score. *Nearest to me*
    would need a driving-distance calculation over the **whole** candidate set rather than one page: sorting a page that
    a distance filter already measured is free, but sorting everything is a new Google cost, and that is the still-open
    quota / caching question rather than an engineering detail. **The server refuses any other value**, so a sort it
    cannot produce is answered `400` instead of being silently ignored.
  - ~~**Heavy-equipment selection still does not persist**~~ — **superseded 2026-08-29: it persists.** `heavyEquipment`
    is a top-level array of the ten approved machine codes on [[users]], written by `PATCH /api/users/me` and cleared by
    the service whenever the specialties the patch leaves behind no longer include `heavy_equipment`. Edit profile loads
    it, shows the chosen machines back as tags without opening the dialog, and a reload returns them. **The shape is an
    implementation choice awaiting ratification, not a closed decision** — [D14](#7-open-decisions) named three
    candidates and this is the narrowest, chosen because it mirrors `specialtyOther`. **Nothing filters or sorts on it**,
    and it is **not** on the public profile: neither has been approved.
  - **`RatingEligibilityPort` has no real implementation**, so every viewer is `no_shared_completed_task`. It needs the
    **Tasks / Completed Work** domain. Implementation dependency only — the rule is decided. **Consequence on the public
    profile, changed 2026-08-29:** the Rate control is now **absent unless the server says `canRate`**, rather than an
    explanation of why it is unavailable, which is the standing *absent, not disabled* rule applied to a control whose
    domain does not exist. **Second consequence, and it is the larger one — the Minimum Rating filter cannot return
    anybody.** `POST /api/ratings` answers **403 `RATING_NOT_ELIGIBLE`** to every attempt, measured against the running
    API on 2026-08-29, so **no rating can be created through the product at all**; the working database held **zero
    ratings across 81 active contractors**. An unrated contractor correctly fails every floor — that is the approved
    rule, and it is the opposite of counting them as a zero — so **any minimum rating correctly returns an empty list,
    every time, until the Tasks / Completed Work domain exists.** The filter is not broken; it is starved. See
    [§10](#10-bug--issue-log) for the manual-QA report this produced.
  - **Terms of Service and Privacy Policy have no screen**, so Register names them in the consent line rather than
    linking to them. They were `href="#"` and are now plain text — a legal reference that opens nothing is worse than
    one that does not claim to. Whether those screens should exist at all is [D25](#7-open-decisions).
  - **A work-entry photo is replaced, never removed on its own.** Editing an entry with a new file swaps the picture and
    deletes the file it replaced; there is no control that clears a photo while keeping the entry, because removing an
    optional asset on its own has not been approved. Deleting the entry still deletes its picture.
  - **The `בטא` / Beta badge is still on the four auth brand panels.** It came from the approved prototypes and is a
    statement about the platform, not about a screen's build state, so it was left alone rather than removed under the
    no-engineering-copy rule. **It is one line to delete if the owner wants it gone.**
  - **The browser verification scripts leave their accounts behind.** They register through the real endpoint and there
