### [2026-08-19] Placeholders appeared to jump when a field was focused
- Symptom: on Edit Profile and Register, the placeholder moved when the field received focus, and the layout shifted slightly with it.
- Cause: **not** a floating-label pattern — there is none in the project. `transform: translateY(-1px)` on `.form-input:hover` and `:focus` lifted the entire control by a pixel, carrying the placeholder with it.
- Fix: removed the transform from both states in `edit-profile.css`, `register.css` and `browse-contractors.css`, and dropped `transform` from the field `transition` list. Button transforms were left alone — a button is not a text field.
- Notes: recorded as **[§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen) rule 1**. `login.css` was checked and never had it on fields; `subscriptions.html` has no form fields at all. The tell for this class of bug is a `transform` inside a `:focus` rule on anything the user types into.

### [2026-08-19] Labels regressed into micro-text on three screens
- Symptom: City, Phone, Region, Work area and Specialty labels were too small to read comfortably — the same problem Register had already been corrected for.
- Cause: each screen set its own label size independently, so the Register correction (raised to 14px on 2026-06-28) never propagated. `profile.css` was at 11.5px, `browse-contractors.css` and `login.css` at 11px.
- Fix: raised labels to **14px** on all three, matching Register, and lifted the associated metadata with them (tags, hints, card org line, rating and flexibility values, connection badge).
- Notes: recorded as **[§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen) rule 3** with Register named as the reference size, so the next screen has a number to copy rather than a judgement to re-make. The root cause is duplicated per-screen CSS; it disappears when the shared layer lands.

### [2026-08-18] English profile screens placed some content inconsistently
- Symptom: in English, the contractor's name, the bio and the rating comments **sat in the middle of the block** instead of lining up with the rest of the English layout, so the English screen read as a separately composed screen rather than a mirror of the Hebrew one.
- Cause: ten elements carried `dir="auto"` on a parent that contained **both** the `.he-text` and `.en-text` spans. `display: none` does not remove text from the bidi algorithm, so `dir="auto"` resolved from the first strong character — the Hebrew — and applied RTL to the block even while English was showing.
- Fix: moved `dir="auto"` off the shared parents and onto each single-language span (`my-profile.html` ×6, `edit-profile.html` ×4).
- Notes: only affects the duplicate-markup prototypes; under React one string is rendered at a time and `dir="auto"` belongs on the element again. Recorded as **[§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) rule 7** because it is invisible in Hebrew and therefore survives review. Grepping for `dir="auto"` is not enough — the check must test whether the element *contains both languages*.

### [2026-08-18] `\u200E` appeared as visible text in the Hebrew copy
- Symptom: the Hebrew bio rendered as `u200E14` instead of the number, on both profile screens.
- Cause: five literal `\u200E` escape sequences were written into the markup. HTML has no `\uXXXX` escape, so the browser printed the characters instead of inserting a left-to-right mark.
- Fix: removed all five and used the project's documented mechanism instead — `<bdi>` around the embedded runs (`<bdi>14</bdi>`, `<bdi>JPG</bdi>`), and plain digits inside the `<textarea>`, which cannot hold markup.
- Notes: the marks were never needed. Digits are weak-directional and already sit correctly inside Hebrew, and `<bdi>` handles the Latin runs. If a control character is ever genuinely required, write the actual character — never an escape sequence. The Hebrew bio was also translated properly (`בעיקר במגדלי מגורים`) rather than left as a half-rendered string.

### [2026-06-16] Left register panel looked visually inconsistent with the right panel
- Symptom: the left side appeared to use a different shade/feeling of blue than the right side.
- Cause: the left brand panel had a stronger dark overlay/gradient treatment than the right blueprint-board surface, even though the colors were derived from the approved palette.
- Fix: changed only the left panel background so it uses the same calmer dark blueprint-board base as the right side.
- Notes: future split-screen screens should keep both sides on the same board/surface system unless a deliberate contrast is needed.

### [2026-06-16] Decorative `01` appeared on the left side of the register screen
- Symptom: a large generated `01` was visible in the left/brand panel.
- Cause: CSS pseudo-element content on `.brand-panel__inner::after` was used as decoration.
- Fix: removed the generated pseudo-element text.
- Notes: avoid decorative generated text unless it has clear product meaning; it can look like accidental content.

### [2026-06-16] Register validation needed clearer CSS-only error feedback
- Symptom: empty required fields did not get a clear red border after user interaction/submission, and password length had no inline bilingual error message.
- Cause: the first static prototype relied partly on native browser validation and only had inline text for some errors.
- Fix: added a bilingual password-length error in the HTML and CSS `:user-invalid` red borders for required inputs/selects/checkboxes.
- Notes: browser-native validation popups may follow browser language; inline `.he-text`/`.en-text` errors are the reliable bilingual layer until JavaScript validation is added.

### [2026-06-15] Approved color exceptions restored for register screen
- Symptom: after enforcing the strict palette rule, the Google sign-up icon no longer used Google's recognizable brand colors and error states no longer appeared red.
- Cause: the palette cleanup removed useful visual conventions that the project owner wants to keep as explicit exceptions.
- Fix: restored Google's original SVG fills and restored the red error token in `screens/register.css`.
- Notes: the standing rule is still the approved project palette, but Google logo colors and red error states are allowed exceptions after owner approval.

### [2026-06-15] Register screen contained non-palette functional/brand colors
- Symptom: the register screen used a red error token and the Google OAuth SVG carried Google's default blue/green/yellow/red fills.
- Cause: those colors came from common UI conventions and third-party branding, but the current project rule is stricter: only the approved palette may be used unless explicit approval is given.
- Fix: changed register error states to palette-derived primary/secondary treatments and removed hard-coded Google SVG fills from the HTML; the icon is now colored through CSS using only `--primary` and `--secondary`.
- Notes: superseded later on 2026-06-15 after explicit owner approval to keep Google logo colors and red error states.

---

## 11. Decision Log

> Every change (code, structure, scope) gets a dated line **with the reason**. Newest at top.
>
> **Format:** `[YYYY-MM-DD] What changed — why.`

- `[2026-08-30]` **Ratings work context — the model stops saying a Task is the only possible evidence.** Owner decision closing the ratings evidence/context model, built on `fix/ratings-work-context`. **A schema change, not a rewording.**

  **The closed rule.** Eligibility rests on real completed professional work between two parties, and **two contexts may prove it: completed professional participation at Project level, and a completed shared Task.** A Task is therefore **not** the permanent exclusive evidence type. Unchanged and re-asserted: **Browse presence never creates eligibility**; **being listed as a Project member never creates it**; project-level eligibility would mean *completed real professional participation between those two parties inside that project*, never `both accounts belong to Project X`; a supplier who actually supplied is eligible under the same system; a completed supplier Task remains one valid proof; there is **no separate supplier ratings model**; self-rating stays forbidden in the backend; and **a delegate is never exposed as the counterparty** — the delegator stays responsible and keeps the relationship.

  **The schema.** `ratings.task`, a flat required Task reference, is replaced by `ratings.context = { kind, project, task? }` with `kind` in `project_task | project_participation`. `project` is always present because both contexts live inside a project; `task` only for `project_task`. The old unique index `rating_rater_ratee_task_unique` is **dropped**, not kept alongside, and replaced by `{ rater, ratee, context.project, context.task }`. The API field `taskId` becomes **`workId`** — it names the completed work being rated, not the kind of record behind it. **20 rating documents existed, every one written by a seed or verification script against a fabricated task id**; a migration script moved what it could and removed what it could not, leaving nothing shapeless counting toward an average.

  **What is actually implemented, honestly.** **`project_task` only.** The audit found that the sole authoritative record of two *named* parties completing work together is a completed project Task — its counterparty (`createdBy`) and its responsible party (`assignee`). **Project-level participation cannot be proved today and was not faked:** `projectmemberships` records who was listed and their status, never what they did with whom; `projects` holds no per-party completion; `workentries` is a one-sided portfolio claim whose verification is still a stub returning `null`. **Nothing was invented to close that gap** — it needs product state that does not exist, and it is reported rather than stubbed.

  **Open, and deliberately not answered.** If project-level evidence is ever built, somebody must decide **rating granularity**: whether a pair may hold both a per-task rating and a per-project rating for the same relationship, or whether the project-level one supersedes. The index permits both today only because no project-level rating can be created at all. **That question is surfaced, not invented.**

  **Verification.** `verify:rating-eligibility` now 27 checks, covering self-rating, Browse-only, membership-without-work, completed shared Task, supplier delivery, open work, unrelated users, Public Profile following the real eligibility result, the stored context, and delegation confidentiality in both directions. No commercial field exists on any of it. **Owner manual QA remains pending.**

- `[2026-08-30]` **Rating eligibility corrected — evidence, not directory presence, and no longer Task-only by definition.** Owner correction to the wording integrated hours earlier, built on `fix/rating-eligibility-project-participation` (`4b5b577`) and **merged into develop as `e92ba95`**. **This was not a documentation fix:** the port it corrects was still wired to a stub that refused every rating.

  **The rule.** The gate asks one question: **does the platform hold evidence that these two people completed real professional work together?** **Browse presence is never evidence** — discoverability is not a working relationship. **Membership alone is not evidence either:** being listed on the same Project proves two people were listed, and it is refused. **Actual professional participation in a shared Project can be evidence.** A **completed shared Project Task is the one source provable today**, and it is explicitly **not** the permanent definition of shared work — a later source answers through the same `RatingEligibilityPort` without the ratings rules moving.

  **What the port now proves.** The pair on one completed piece of project work: **the counterparty who ordered it and the responsible party who answered for it.** A **supplier fulfilling a delivery commitment is that same shape**, which is why supplier participation needs no separate path and got none. **A delegate is deliberately not a party** — the delegator stays responsible, and the closed confidentiality rule means the ordering side must never learn who performed the work. Standalone work is self-assigned, so it can never produce a shared pair and needs no special case.

  **The stub was the real defect.** `noTaskDomainEligibility` refused everything, written when `tasks` was unbuilt and never revisited after Create Task shipped — so no rating could be written at all, and the Public Profile hard-coded `canRate: false` for every viewer with the reason `no_shared_completed_task`, which encoded *Task only* in a user-facing string. The port is now the real adapter, the profile asks it, and the reason is **`no_shared_completed_work`** — it names the missing evidence, never which kind of record would supply it.

  **Unchanged, and asserted:** self-rating prevention, one rating per shared completed work relationship, no separate supplier-rating model, and **no pricing, payment terms, invoices or money flow** — the delivery commitment is a Task, never a purchase order. **No schema field changed.** New suite `verify:rating-eligibility` (19 checks) covers all seven required cases; **re-run after the merge**, backend **1,261** checks / 23 suites and browser **807** / 16 pass, both typechecks clean and the Vite build green. **The Public Profile now resolves `canRate` through the eligibility port itself** rather than hard-coding a refusal, and reports **`no_shared_completed_work`** when there is nothing to show. **Standalone supplier ordering outside a Project stays a future shared Task/Agreement dependency**, and standalone self-assignment was not touched. **Future schema residue:** `ratings.task` is a required task reference, so the first non-Task evidence source approved will need that field reconsidered — flagged, not built. **Owner manual QA remains pending.**

- `[2026-08-30]` **Register reworked and integrated — three registration routes, the father's taxonomy, and an explicit email choice.** Merged `feature/register-taxonomy-notifications.backend` (`2c94412`) → develop as **`4d1d91b`**, then `feature/register-taxonomy-notifications-react` (`5e0f821`) → develop as **`bf478ed`**. **No conflicts, and no reverse merge** — both branches were cut from `3903376` and merged forward only.

  **The model.** `registrationCategory` is a **stored, required, indexed** field with three values, and the reason it is stored rather than derived is the point: the three specialty lists do not overlap, so the route *could* be inferred from a code, and inferring it would mean a specialty edit could silently move an account between routes. It drives which taxonomy is offered, it is available to Browse and to any future route-specific behaviour, and **it is not a project role, not a company position, and grants nothing** — no permission reads it. The 23-code `TRADES` enum is **deleted, not deprecated**: 20 contractor codes, 5 architectural, 12 supplier, with **no migration layer**, because the owner confirmed no production account needed one. `קידוחי החדרה וצנרת PVC` is a **nested subtype** in `drillingTypes`, not a profession. **`Other` is per-route** — three codes — so it can refine a route but never replace one, and there is **no global catch-all**.

  **The email choice.** `notificationPreferences.operationalEmail` is **required with no default**, which is the whole design: the person must actively choose, so neither answer is assumed from silence. Refusing it **never blocks registration, disables the platform, or removes in-app notifications**. The email is a delivery channel only — never an approval, a rejection, or any other response.

  **Ratings — unchanged model, and the wording matters.** The architectural category uses the **existing** ratings mechanism; nothing separate was invented. **A supplier may be rated by somebody who actually ordered from or worked with them, where the platform holds real evidence of a shared completed Task.** A supplier **Project Task may represent an operational delivery commitment** — what is being supplied, by whom, against the applicable schedule. It is **NOT a commercial Purchase Order system**: there is no pricing, no payment terms, no invoices and no money flow, and none was added. **Corrected 2026-08-30 — see the later entry: the wording here was too narrow.** Browse alone never creates eligibility; same-Project membership alone is not enough; but **real professional participation in a shared Project may create it** where the platform holds evidence of completed shared work, and a completed shared Task is **one** valid evidence source rather than the permanent exclusive definition. Self-rating prevention is unchanged. **Verified against the integrated build:** a supplier-category account can be invited to a Project, accept, and be assigned a delivery Task; the created Task carries no commercial field; and an open shared Task is still refused with `RATING_NOT_ELIGIBLE` rather than for being a supplier. **Standalone supplier ordering outside a Project remains a future dependency** — standalone Task creation is self-assigned by a closed rule, so it cannot produce a *shared* Task; that needs a later shared Task/Agreement flow and was not invented here.

  **Status.** Automated verification green — backend **1,243** checks / 22 suites, browser **807** / 16, both `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vite build ✓. A stale `verify:owner-qa` assertion expecting two inert navbar links was corrected (`5e0f821`) rather than `AppNav` being changed to satisfy it — Projects and My Tasks became real destinations two batches ago. **Owner manual QA is still pending; this is integrated, not accepted.**

- `[2026-08-30]` **Owner decisions closing the Proposal/Cascade residue, the notification model, and a rework of the BUILT Register screen.**

  **Cascade, reconciled rather than re-asked.** **(1) Affected work is DERIVED, not decided:** walk `dependsOn` forward from the changed task's stage, transitively; the tasks in those stages are candidates; a candidate is affected only where the dates actually collide, because `downstream work that can still proceed, proceeds`. A **gate** widens the hold where the sequencing rule requires it; **partial release** narrows it, and only as an authorised human act. The GC's manual include/exclude is the last word. **No proposal goes to a task merely for belonging to a downstream stage.** **(2) A response never writes the live schedule.** Accepted, declined and pending are **facts** the authorised decision-maker reads; the GC's resolution is what moves dates. The platform must not make the final scheduling decision for him, and expiry decides nothing. **(3) Substitute is Delegation** — the existing capability, not a second system: the contractor keeps the work and the responsibility, confidentiality holds, single level, no re-delegation, `ownCrewOnly` still blocks it. **Formal replacement stays a separate event**, only when the responsible contractor actually leaves and the GC formally accepts another responsible party. **(4) One request may carry several requested changes** — extra days, an alternative date, both, or another supported arrangement. One field is not one request. **(5) D2b is AFFIRMED, no longer an entailment:** an accepted counter re-runs the impact beneath it and downstream parties receive proposals against the newly computed impact. No stale downstream dates, and no task-level DAG. **(6) The response window is CONFIGURABLE** — a project default the GC sets, overridable per proposal at send time. This **supersedes 72 hours as an immutable maximum**; `48–72 שעות` remains domain context. Expiry still makes no automatic work decision.

  **Notifications.** **Blocking coverage is free on every tier** — an alert whose absence could stall work is never withheld for payment. A blocking event **appears immediately in-app and does not immediately email**: it opens a **90-minute grace window**, and **if the in-app notice has been seen in that window the queued email is cancelled**. The email is a delivery channel only — it is never an approval, a rejection or any other response, and every operational action happens inside FieldSync. **Non-blocking events never email individually**; they aggregate into the **end-of-day digest**, which carries only what is still relevant — an item already handled in-app is not resurfaced. Tiers: **Free** = blocking coverage; **Basic** = the digest; **Premium** = the digest plus timing controls configurable per notification class. **Premium buys control, never access to essential coordination information.** **Early upstream completion notifies the GC on every tier, free** — and never releases downstream work: the GC decides, and only after an authorised release does the next professional hear anything.

  **Register — corrections to a screen that is already built and integrated.** The screen ships today as a **single flat form** on the 23-code `TRADES` enum. Three corrections are approved. **Two steps, not three:** Step 1 opens with the registration route and the rest of the step follows from it; **three separate routes** — `קבלנים / בעלי מקצוע מבצעים`, `קטגוריה אדריכלית / בעלי מקצוע`, `ספקים` — and suppliers and the architectural category are **not** forced through the contractor taxonomy. **Step 2 is the email-delivery choice:** optional and explicit — refusing it never blocks registration, never disables the platform and never removes in-app notifications, but the choice is prominent, **neither option is preselected**, and one must be chosen; it is changeable afterwards in Notification Settings. **The father's taxonomy replaces the stale list verbatim**, with `קידוחי החדרה וצנרת PVC` nested under `קבלן קידוחים`; `קונגו` is removed as a tool rather than a profession, and `קידוחי צנרת ניקוז` is removed as a separate specialty because it belongs under drilling.

- `[2026-08-30]` **`schedule.partial_release.manage` — partial release gets its own capability, and the permission/authority distinction becomes a written rule.** Owner decision, built on `fix/partial-release-permission` and merged (`fc897de`). **Representation only: no endpoint reads it and no partial-release behaviour was invented.** It is deliberately **not** `schedule.exception.approve` — that answers a different question — and **not** `project.stage.manage`, which shapes the sequence rather than releasing against it. It follows the configurable-grant model in full: a template may carry it, a contractor may add or remove it per person per project, Full Project Authority covers it through the existing flag (asserted in a test, because a code added *after* a grant was made must still be covered by it), and no title grants it.

  **The rule this closes, which is bigger than the code.** The permission catalogue now states it in the file itself: **a code answers only whether somebody HAS a capability. It never answers whether a particular exercise of it is lawful.** The closed domain chains constrain that separately, and **a grant may not override one**. For partial release the chain is closed and unchanged — the GC originates; a `מנהל אתרים` / `סמנכ״ל` acts only on the GC's instruction; a `מנהל עבודה` only where that chain reaches him. **Grantable capability ≠ licence to bypass a closed authority chain**, and the two are not interchangeable.

- `[2026-08-30]` **INTEGRATION — Create Task is merged into `develop` (`649876d`, `73d0b3b`), and a reverse merge I should never have made was corrected.** Two merges, one per layer, in the only direction the workflow allows: **feature → `develop`**.

  **The mistake, stated plainly.** To give the feature branches the new `project.stage.manage` code without copying it, I merged **`develop` into both feature branches**. That is backwards, and it is now a standing rule that it must never happen: **`develop` is merged into by feature branches and never merges back down.** The audit and what each case allowed: on **`feature/create-task-react`** the reverse merge was `2ab5451`, **never pushed**, with **zero** dependent commits — removed cleanly by resetting the branch to its pushed tip `0c80890`, losing no feature work. On **`feature/create-task.backend`** the reverse merge was `69fe94f`, **already pushed**, and the stage-permission fix `e2fe31b` **depends on it** — it uses a permission code that only reaches the branch through it. Rewriting a pushed branch with a dependent commit was rejected as the more dangerous option, so that one was **integrated forward and the branch closed**; the merge survives in history and is recorded here rather than hidden. **No duplicate fix was copied between branches.**

  **The rule this leaves.** A post-merge correction never reopens a merged feature branch: it gets **a new branch cut from the updated `develop`**. None was needed here — the integration required no fixes at all.

  **Verified on integrated `develop`, not on the branches.** Backend **56 + 64 + 68 + 133 + 130 + 78**; browser **66 + 70 + 71 + 57 + 47 + 60**; both layers typecheck clean. Checked explicitly after the merge: the React label and type mirror for `project.stage.manage` survived (the React branch predated it and did not revert it), the stage-dependency guard no longer reads `project.edit`, and no authority anywhere is derived from a role, title, position, membership or creator status.

- `[2026-08-30]` **`project.stage.manage` — sequencing becomes its own grant, correcting an assumption that had already shipped.** Four things are deliberately kept apart in this entry: what the **owner decided**, what the **father/domain findings closed**, what was an **engineering correction**, and what is **still open**.

  **OWNER DECISION.** A tenth project permission, **`project.stage.manage`**, governs the whole of stage and construction-sequence management: **creating** stages, **editing** their names and details, **reordering** them, **setting their dependencies**, and **setting the `isGate` flag**. `isGate` is a real sequencing threshold — a `תנאי סף` — not a visual ordering hint: downstream work must not pass a gate stage until the applicable sequencing rule allows it. **Order and the gate flag do NOT get codes of their own**; they are one capability. **`project.edit` is project METADATA only** and must never imply authority over the construction sequence. The permission follows the existing **configurable-grant** model in full: a template may include it, the contractor may remove it from one person and add it to another, Full Project Authority continues to include it through the existing flag, and **no title grants it automatically**.

  **FATHER / DOMAIN RULE (closed, and unchanged by the above).** `יש שלבים ברורים אבל בכל פרויקט השלבים משתנים – נקבע לפי הקבלן הראשי/המנכ״ל בלבד` — the stages of a project are the GC's. The fifth delivery's `המנהל אתרים אחראי על כל התכניות ועל כל השלבי ביצוע` describes **professional responsibility, and is explicitly NOT converted into an authorization entitlement** — the standing rule `כן יותר נכון שהמערכת תתבסס על הסמכות שהוענקה בפועל ולא רק על שם התפקיד` governs, so the system reads the grant and never the title. Stage management is **not** in the closed non-transferable set (`מסירת דירות · סגירת שינויים בסכומים · סגירה מול בעלי מקצוע`), which is why it is grantable at all.

It is now `project.stage.manage`. One coupled assertion was repaired rather than worked around — a browser check ticked the permission checkbox **by list position**, so a tenth code silently moved it onto a different permission; the checkbox now carries its code as its `value` and the test addresses it by code.

  **STILL OPEN — partial release (`שחרור חלקי`).** Audited against the current Decision Map, this document and the later findings, and **not implemented**. **Closed:** the authority chain, verbatim — `רק הקבלן הראשי/המנכ״ל … יכול להחליט על זה או מנהל אתרים/סמנכ״ל … רק לאחר קבלת הנחיה מהמנכ״ל … או מנהל עבודה שמקבל הנחיה מהסמנכ״ל/מנהל אתרים שההנחיה הגיעה … דרך המנכ״ל`; the Closures sheet records it as *"שחרור חלקי under CEO-originated authority"*, and the related rule that **only the GC declares** `שוחררה`. **Also closed** (fifth delivery): its *expression* — `צריך להיות רשום בהודעה מה מבקשים`. **NOT closed:** the permission representation. The Decision Map still lists *"a שחרור חלקי mechanism **wired to the granted-authority chain**"* under **REMAINING FOR FULL CLOSURE**, and its own item for it stands at `st:'need'`. **No code was invented for it**, and `schedule.exception.approve` was **not** reused merely because both touch scheduling. The distinction the owner drew is recorded as a standing rule: **a configurable permission grant establishes that somebody HAS a capability; it never licenses bypassing a closed domain authority chain.** Whatever representation is chosen must satisfy both.

- `[2026-08-30]` **Project type, size, the C-SAFE working calendar and the permissions foundation, all on `feature/projects.backend`. IMPLEMENTED · VERIFIED BY AUTOMATED TESTS · NOT MERGED · OWNER MANUAL QA PENDING.** `npm run verify:projects` is now **99 checks**.

  **Type and size, exactly as the findings support them.** `projectType` is `villa | private_house | building | other` — `וילה` and `בית פרטי` are **separate values by owner decision**, and the six-way list of `בנייה רוויה / מגדלים / מסחרי / תשתיות / שיפוצים` was **not** used, because it appears only inside an interview question and never in an answer. `אחר` requires free text, stored in `projectTypeOther` **apart from the enum**: free text beside a canonical type is refused, and `other` without it is refused. **`size` is required free text** — never a category — because the father's own examples (`בניין 10/12 קומות`, `2 בניינים`) are two different measurements of the same idea and a `small/medium/large` field would discard exactly what the GC must know upfront. **A project is a site**; several sites are several projects, and no nested model was introduced.

  **The working calendar is C-SAFE, and the safety is structural rather than procedural.** A company default is a chain of **immutable versions** (`companycalendarversions`); editing it **appends** a version and rewrites nothing. A project **pins one version id** and may hold its own overrides. So a company edit cannot reach a live project — not by convention, but because the project points at a frozen row that keeps saying what it always said. Moving to a newer version happens **only** through `POST /api/projects/:id/calendar/adopt`, which takes an explicit `keepOverrides` and appends to `calendarAdoptions[]`. `GET /api/projects/calendar/outdated` counts the projects sitting behind so the GC can review them one at a time — **surfaced, never applied**. Proved: four consecutive company edits leave the original project's pin untouched; a project created after an edit pins the new version; overrides survive adoption when asked and are dropped when asked; the version a project used is still readable afterwards and still says 07:00.

  **The permissions foundation keeps eight concepts apart and collapses none of them.** `createdBy` (provenance) · company membership · `companyPosition` (a title, never read) · project membership · `projectRole` (descriptive, never read) · individual `permissions[]` · **`fullAuthority`** · work delegation ([D7](#7-open-decisions), untouched). **Full Project Authority is stored as a flag, not an expanded list**, which is the whole point: a copied list would freeze at the permissions that existed the day it was made, while the flag keeps meaning *all of them, including ones added later*. It grants management only — it never moves ownership and never rewrites `createdBy`, and it is never implied by a company position or by being a Main Contractor. **Contractor-defined permission templates** (`permissiontemplates`) are company-owned and reusable; applying one **copies** its grants at that moment, so editing the template later does not reach back into memberships already made from it — the same reasoning as the calendar pin. **No universal role-keyed templates exist.** A guest from another company reaches a project **only** through their project membership; every project stands on its own. **No permissions UI was built**, and none belongs on these screens.

- `[2026-08-30]` **My projects and Create / Edit project gained type, size, the calendar panel and real grouping, on `feature/projects-react`. IMPLEMENTED · VERIFIED BY AUTOMATED TESTS · NOT MERGED · OWNER MANUAL QA PENDING.** `npm run verify:projects-ui` is now **52 checks**.

  **Type is a four-value select and `אחר` reveals its free-text box only when chosen** — proved in the browser: the box is absent until `other` is selected, required once it is, and the **card then shows the free text rather than the word "אחר"**. **Size is a plain required text field** with the domain's own examples as its hint, and the card prints it exactly as typed.

  **The calendar panel states what the project works by and never applies anything.** It shows the effective working days and hours, marks a project that carries its own adjustments, and shows the adopt control **only when a newer company version exists and only to a viewer who may manage the project**. Adoption asks whether to keep the project's adjustments before it moves.

  **My projects groups by the real current management relationship.** The API answers `viewerManages` per row — computed from the caller's company grant or their project grant, **never from `createdBy`** — and the screen groups on it: `הפרויקטים שלי` and the approved `פרויקטים שאינם בניהולי`. **The heading only appears when both groups have rows**, because one heading over everything is noise. **Edit is rendered only where the viewer actually holds authority**, which is a courtesy — the server refuses either way.

- `[2026-08-30]` **Project authority was moved behind one seam, so the future permissions model does not require redesigning Projects. ENGINEERING ONLY — no product behaviour was decided here.** Every authority question now lives in `projectAuthorization.ts` and the service asks it rather than checking for itself. **Nothing branches on `standing` or `companyPosition`** — a job title grants nothing and is never read — so authority is something *granted*, matching the grant-based model [D23](#7-open-decisions) selected. The four separations the permissions work will need are kept apart and none of them is collapsed: **company position** (a title) · **membership** (belonging) · **granted permission** (what may be done) · **work delegation** ([D7](#7-open-decisions), a different mechanism entirely). **No project-scoped grant field was invented**, because none is decided; when one exists it is consulted inside these functions and no call site changes.

  **One engineering reading, stated so it can be overridden:** editing and cancelling are gated on the same `project.create` grant that governs creating, because **no finer-grained project permission has been defined**. The alternative was to let any active member edit, which would have made editing depend on membership rather than on a grant — the weaker of the two, and the opposite direction from the model. Proved by test: with the grant removed and the standing left as `owner`, create, edit and cancel are all refused while reading still works, and restoring the grant restores the ability. **`verify:projects` is now 63 checks.**

  **No permissions UI was added to My projects or Create / Edit project**, and none belongs there: the current Decision Map places no permission-management surface on either screen.

- `[2026-08-30]` **Projects backend built on `feature/projects.backend`. IMPLEMENTED · VERIFIED BY AUTOMATED TESTS · NOT MERGED · OWNER MANUAL QA PENDING.** A new `Backend/src/features/projects/` module: `POST /api/projects`, `GET /api/projects` (cursor-paginated), `GET /api/projects/:id`, `PATCH /api/projects/:id`, `DELETE /api/projects/:id`. **56 checks in `npm run verify:projects`, all passing.**

  **Four owner decisions are implemented as given, and this entry records the fourth.** *(1)* **The company owns the project** — `company` is the owning context and `createdBy` is provenance that grants nothing; a forged `company` or `createdBy` in a request body is stripped and changes nothing, because authorisation reads the session. *(2)* **The closed date model** — start + target + original + `x`, with `x` immutable, the ceiling fixed at `original + x`, and the actual overrun recorded on every move. *(3)* **Status is derived, never stored** — the old four-value enum is gone, there is no manual control, and a request that tries to set one is refused. *(4)* **[D16](#7-open-decisions) — the owner's decision, recorded here:** where existence itself must stay undisclosed, the answer is **404, and it must not leak through a different body or copy**. Projects apply it: another company's project and an id that never existed return **byte-identical** responses, asserted as such in the suite. **It is deliberately not generalised** to every authorisation failure in the product — a caller who may know the resource exists but lacks a permission still gets a 403, as `COMPANY_PERMISSION_DENIED` does on create.

  **Two closed rules the system cannot evaluate yet are named rather than approximated.** *Started* = the GC set a date **and the first task started on it**; *completed* = every task closed with quality execution. Tasks are unbuilt, so both sit behind a `ProjectExecutionPort` that answers `false` today — which makes every project `planned` and therefore cancellable. **The rule was not replaced by "the start date passed"**, which is a different rule nobody decided; it starts working the day Tasks exist.

  **One real defect was found and fixed by its own test**: clearing a project location left the previous city and region in place, because an explicit clear was being treated as "no value supplied". An explicit clear now `$unset`s the whole sub-document, so a stale legacy location can never surface under a place that was removed.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Owner-QA corrections, on `fix/owner-qa-corrections.backend` (`a104f2c`) and `fix/owner-qa-corrections.react` (`15dad2a`). Not merged. Nothing here is approved and no screen is closed.**

  **Four claims, and this entry keeps them apart** — they are not stages of one process, and every
  item below sits in several at once. **Implemented** means the code exists and runs; it does *not*
  mean it is on `develop`. **Integrated into `develop`** means merged, and **nothing in this pass
  is** — a `develop` checkout still shows the pre-correction screens. **Verified by automated
  tests** means named suites assert the behaviour and pass: 621 backend checks across 16 suites and
  281 browser checks across 7, all green, on a throwaway branch holding both correction branches
  together; it does *not* mean a person has used it. **Awaiting owner manual QA** is true of every
  line here and is the default until the owner says otherwise. A **dependency still open** is a
  fifth, separate note: the code is finished and something it needs does not exist yet.
  **The theme, and it is one theme.** Almost every defect in this pass was a control that *looked* like it worked. A picker that discarded what was typed; a Save that reverted and called it success; an error message no stylesheet could show; two picture URLs pointing at routes that did not exist; a navbar of links to nowhere; a rating explanation where a rating control belonged. **The rule the owner drew is that production-facing UI may not knowingly pretend** — implement it, or make it visibly inert, but never leave it looking live.

  **Two new copy rules, both cross-cutting.** **Hebrew addresses one person**, and plural is not an acceptable way to avoid gender — the fix is to nominalize (`בחירת מיקום`, not `בחרו מיקום`), slash forms are out, and **English keeps its natural singular "you"** rather than being flattened to match. Applied across every migrated screen and written into [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) as rule 8. **A busy button keeps its label and shows the MUI spinner**, so `שומר…` / `מוסיף…` / `מחשב…` / `Saving…` and the rest were deleted from both resource files rather than translated — the password-reset screens had already set that pattern and five other screens were quietly doing the opposite.

  **What was built.** Work entries became editable end to end (`PATCH /api/users/me/work-entries/:id`, owner-scoped, with an MUI `EditOutlined` control on every tile) **and their photos are finally rendered**, which they never were. `users.heavyEquipment` persists, closing the storage half of [D14](#7-open-decisions)'s equipment question in its narrowest shape — **the shape awaits ratification, so the decision is not closed**. Browse gained back its **Sort** control (Relevance and Rating; Flexibility is [D6](#7-open-decisions), Nearest is the open Google quota question) and its **multi-select availability**, and lost the viewer from its own results, server-side. Travel radius takes any whole number in range instead of presets, in both places that ask for one. Two missing public-asset routes were registered. Login was given the same error language Register already had.

  **What was deliberately not built, and why each is a decision rather than an omission.** *Minimum flexibility* and *flexibility sort* — [D6](#7-open-decisions) has no arithmetic, and an order is as much an invention as a number. *Nearest to me* — it needs driving distances for the whole candidate set, not one page, and that is the open quota / caching question, so it is offered as a cost proposal instead. *Terms and Privacy screens* — [D25](#7-open-decisions). *A control to remove a work photo without removing the entry* — never approved.

  **One thing was left alone on purpose:** the `בטא` / **Beta** badge on the auth brand panels. The no-engineering-copy rule names "Beta", but this badge came from the approved prototypes and says something about the platform rather than about a screen's build state. It is flagged for the owner, not removed.
- `[2026-08-29]` **Seven branches were integrated into `develop` (`13a7e0e`), and this document was brought up to the code. Nothing here is approved: every screen below is `IMPLEMENTED + INTEGRATED INTO DEVELOP + AWAITING OWNER MANUAL QA`.**

  **Provenance.** Seven merges, in this order: `7f36a54` Browse/Blocks/Connections/Ratings/Location backend (`1a5ecdb`) · `32e225b` invitation cancellation + Main Contractor guard (`4193d79`) · `eba6f98` owner is Main Contractor (`90ee895`) · `61361b3` Browse React (`133683b`) · `067de0b` profile real data + avatar (`619ab2e`) · `c57bbf4` invitation cancellation UI (`7a09ecb`) · `782b509` account menu (`69cd578`). Then `6bd379e` (shared location module, Places localization, server-side logout) and `13a7e0e` (integrated browser verification). **One `develop` checkout now runs the whole system** — no branch or worktree switching to reach a screen. `main` was not touched.

  **Five merge conflicts, all resolved by hand, none by taking one side wholesale.** Three were script lists in `package.json` where each branch appended at the same anchor — resolved as a **union**. One was `companyMembership.model.ts`, where two branches added overlapping constants — resolved by keeping each **once**. One was `Frontend/.env.example`, where `develop` carried a bare `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY =` stub beside the branch's documented block — the **stub was dropped**, the same duplicate-entry cleanup the backend example needed. `AppNav.tsx` auto-merged and was checked by hand: both the Browse router link and the new account menu survived.

  **(1) The profile screens stopped inventing data.** `representativeProfile()` returned a fabricated profile — only name and email were real, and company name, phones, city, bio, rating, flexibility and completed work were hard-coded constants shipped in both languages. Its own comment said *"The API has no profile endpoint"*, which had been true when it was written and **was false by the time it mattered**. Both screens now read `GET /users/me` and write through `PATCH /users/me` and `PATCH /companies/me`. **This was a display fault, never data loss** — the database always held what was typed.

  **(2) The avatar exists as a control, not only as a backend.** There was **no file input anywhere in the client**. There now is: multipart `PUT /users/me/avatar` → Multer → GridFS → `avatar.fileId`, rendered from `avatarUrl`. The asset route answers only to a Bearer token, which an `<img src>` cannot carry, so the bytes are fetched through the app's own client and handed to the browser as an object URL, revoked on replace or unmount. **No base64 storage and no frontend-only preview stands in for persistence.**

  **(3) A pending employee invitation can be withdrawn**, and **(4) a company has exactly one Main Contractor** — its owner, recorded at registration. Both are written up on [[companymemberships]] in [§3.2](#32-data-model-entity-index--full-design-in-the-companion-html).

  **(5) [D17](#7-open-decisions) and [D19](#7-open-decisions) were closed by owner decision and built.** A refusal is `declined` and is **not permanent**: the same pair may ask again, reusing the one unique pair row. Blocks became a **collection of their own**, because a block needs no connection to exist.

  **(6) One shared structured-location module.** `Frontend/src/location/` is now the only client code that talks to Google Places, and Browse, Register and Edit profile all consume it; the backend has one `structuredPlaceSchema` imported by both validators. Places results follow the **active interface language** and are cleared when it changes. **Legacy `city`+`region` accounts stay readable and are never given a fabricated Place ID.**

  **(7) Logging out now ends the session on the server.** `POST /api/auth/logout` revokes only the presented token family and clears the cookie. **Logout is not account deletion** and writes nothing to the account.

  **Two test assertions were corrected, and the distinction matters.** Both asserted that a registered owner has **no** `companyPosition` — the behaviour this pass deliberately reversed on owner instruction. They were rewritten to assert the new rule (and one gained a stronger check that the company holds exactly one Main Contractor). **No test was weakened or skipped to obtain a pass.**

  **A disclosure.** `verify-owner-main-contractor.ts` exercises the real backfill, and it ran against the shared development database — so **owner memberships in that database, including the project owner's own, were backfilled to `main_contractor` as a side effect**. That is exactly the approved change, and no user document was written, but it happened without an explicit migration run and is recorded rather than left implicit.

- `[2026-08-29]` **The five corrections this document was still missing were written in — four closures and one rewrite. Documentation only: nothing was decided in this pass, no code, screen or schema file was touched.**

  **(1) [D8](#7-open-decisions) closed — the 60-day lifecycle, and it retires a wording rather than only answering a question.** A deletion request is a **deactivation plus a 60-day recovery period**: not active for normal use, reachable by admin / support for support, recovery or reactivation, restorable at the user's request inside the window. **After 60 days the PII is anonymized and shared business / project history is preserved** — messages, tasks and audit entries are **never deleted solely because an account was**, and attribution becomes a **neutral deleted-user identity**. **Every immediately-permanent-with-no-recovery wording is superseded wherever it survives** — and the search for it in this document found **none**, so the correction lands on D8 and on the [§6](#6-feature-registry) Settings row, whose confirmation copy is now constrained to state the window and what survives. The **attribution is not the deleted-message placeholder**, per [§8](#8-known-issues--risks)'s four-name rule. **Active-commitment handling is flagged open, not decided.**

  **(2) [§8](#8-known-issues--risks)'s chat-deletion item stopped contradicting the rules written beside it.** Its blanket — *none should be added anywhere until this is decided* — and two of its three questions were **struck rather than silently rewritten**, per this document's own convention: per-participant won for the whole chat, and messages-versus-thread is answered by there being **two operations**. **The item's remaining question is now stated as the one that survives** — whether a later message brings back a per-side-deleted chat — with the `deletedFor` / per-message-deleted / kept-marker fields recorded as **shapes owed, not decisions open**.

  **(3) The project lifecycle gained its post-completion tail**, which this document did not carry at all: **GC-only repairs during occupancy**, **תיקונים שנתיים** after a year, and an extended **יזם warranty** (5 or 10 years). **The tail's product scope is flagged open** — record only, repair tasks, or out of MVP.

  **(4) Ratings gained a security rule (owner): a user may never rate themself.** The backend **rejects** `raterUserId === ratedUserId`; **UI hiding is not enforcement**. It is written on the [[ratings]] index row because **[§6](#6-feature-registry) has no ratings row and no ratings endpoint exists** — recorded as a gap rather than used as a reason to leave the rule unwritten. **Both halves of that gap closed on 2026-08-29 (`13a7e0e`): the endpoint is built and §6 now carries a Ratings row. The rule it anchored survived the build unchanged — the self-check is the first thing `rate()` does.**

  **(5) The travel and location model closed (owner), across three places.** A travel radius is **real driving distance, never straight-line** — **Google Routes (Compute Route Matrix)** — with **Google Places** and Place IDs for structured selection; Browse ships **two separate location filters**, driving-distance km and an explicit place; and contractor preferences carry **`travelRadiusKm` and `approvedTravelLocations`**, where **Google's suggestions are only suggestions and the manually edited list is authoritative**, removals and out-of-radius additions included. [§2](#2-tech-stack) gained a **Location & routing** row, [§3.2](#32-data-model-entity-index--full-design-in-the-companion-html)'s [[users]] row carries the field semantics, and the [§6](#6-feature-registry) Browse row carries the query consequence. **Kept open exactly:** the schema placement of `approvedTravelLocations`, the stored shape of a Place-ID entry, quota / caching for the two APIs, and the Browse query design. **Because every one of those is open, `docs/database-design.html` was correctly left alone** — golden rule 3 binds a model *change*, and no field shape was chosen here.

  **Two things this pass deliberately did not do.** It did not touch the **My-Network screen ledes and dialog copy**, which still describe the old gate on the static screen — a screen edit owed whenever that screen is next opened. And it did not repair [§3.2](#32-data-model-entity-index--full-design-in-the-companion-html)'s [[connections]] note, which still reads *"Gates messaging"* against the [D9](#7-open-decisions) reversal — **flagged here rather than fixed**, because it was outside the five items.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Employee Management + owner onboarding built, and the model tightened to one company per person.** Frontend `feature/employee-management-react` (`3ceab43`), backend `feature/employee-management-onboarding.backend` (`c1a4c5a`). **Both are implemented and NEITHER is merged** — this entry records built code awaiting integration, not integrated code, and the feature has had **no browser validation** (RTL/LTR and responsive were checked statically and at build level only).

  **(a) One reusable React feature, mounted twice.** `/employees` for ordinary authenticated use and `/onboarding/employees` for the optional first-time step share one list, one invitation form, one status renderer, one approval path, one bulk path, one API layer and one set of strings; **only the surrounding navigation differs.** Two implementations of the same screen was the failure this avoided, and the shared component is what makes *skip does not lose the feature* true by construction rather than by review.

  **(b) The approved owner flow is `Register → Login → optional Employee Management onboarding → Personal dashboard`.** **Register still does not authenticate anybody and Login is still the authentication boundary** — the step sits *after* Login. **No onboarding token, temporary session, post-Register access token, automatic Login or frontend-only authentication was created**, and **Login is not prefilled**: the registered address travels through no query parameter, router state, `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, global state or cookie. Register navigates to a blank Login and stores nothing.

  **(c) The durable state is `companies.employeeSetupCompletedAt`, and it is on the Company on purpose.** Absent means the step is still owed; a timestamp means it was completed *or skipped*. **Skip and Finish record the same business fact** through the same endpoint, because *the business was offered the step and dealt with it* is one fact and a separate `skipped` state would be a distinction nothing downstream asks about. The write is **idempotent and keeps the first stamp**. It is on the **Company** rather than the person because a company's staff is what was set up — on the person it would be a fact about whoever signed up first, and a second manager joining later would be walked through it again for a company that already has staff. **No `localStorage` flag participates**, and whether the step is owed is read from canonical backend state, never from an empty employee list: **an owner may legitimately have zero employees.**

  **(d) The session gained a company half, because a stateless token cannot learn it.** Login and the new `GET /api/auth/me` answer the same `SessionUser`, whose `company` is a `CompanyContext` of `id` / `standing` / `membershipStatus` / `permissions` / `employeeSetupComplete`. `/auth/me` **issues nothing and rotates nothing** — it exists because nothing that happens after an Access Token is minted, an employer approving a membership most of all, can reach the copy a client holds. It is what the Waiting for Approval screen's *Check status* calls, and an employee moves from waiting to working **on their original token, with no re-registration and no second Login.** Password verification, Access Token behaviour and Refresh Token behaviour are **unchanged**; this added a payload, not a security change.

  **(e) `membershipStatus` is a code, not a boolean, and that is the whole point.** Waiting for a company's approval and having left that company are different facts, and no boolean separates them. A pending employee stays fully authenticated — tokens intact, not redirected to Register, not treated as banned — and simply holds no active membership yet.

  **(f) One person, one company — closed, and enforced by the database.** A user belongs to **exactly one** company at a time; the live states are `pending_company_approval` and `active`, and **`user_current_unique`** (unique on `{ user }`, partial over both) makes *active at A + pending at B*, two pending rows and two active rows unstorable. Unclaimed `invited` seats carry **no user** and `inactive` rows are **history**, so both sit outside it — a company may hold any number of open seats, and **somebody who genuinely left may later join elsewhere.** This **supersedes** the old `user_1` index, which constrained `active` alone and left the invalid state writable.

  **(g) A read bug the rule exposed, found by re-auditing rather than by a user.** `findCurrentByUser` used to fall back to *any* row, so an **ended** membership was served as the current company context **carrying its stale permission list** — a deactivated owner was routed into employee onboarding and shown management controls that then answered `403 NO_ACTIVE_COMPANY`. An *enabled* fake action, worse than the disabled one the project already forbids. It now reads only the pending-or-active row and answers `null` otherwise; `user_current_unique` guarantees at most one match, so there is no ordering rule left to get wrong.

  **(h) Historical permissions are kept, by owner decision, and are never current capabilities.** An `inactive` membership **retains** its `permissions` array for history and audit and is **deliberately not cleared** — an audit trail that erases what somebody was allowed to do cannot answer what they were allowed to do. **Stored historically ≠ currently authorized:** the read enforces it, because an ended row never becomes a company context and its permissions therefore have nowhere to reach.

  **(i) Authorization is the capability, everywhere.** Every Employee Management action is gated on the caller's own `company.invite_employees` permission on an active membership — **never** on `standing`, `companyPosition` or a job title, and the client holds no *if owner, allow everything* rule. The backend stays the security boundary; the client renders the server's 403. **Approval grants nothing** — `pending_company_approval` → `active` copies no permissions and infers none from a job title, and ~~**Permissions remains a separate, unbuilt feature.**~~ *(Superseded 2026-08-30 — built and integrated. The rule it was supporting is unchanged: approval copies no permissions and infers none from a job title.)*

  **(j) UI visibility is a courtesy, not a gate.** A user whose `standing` is `employee` sees neither the Employee Management navbar entry nor the Personal dashboard **`ניהול` / Management** section, and **no empty Management section is rendered for them**. That section is compact, single-item-safe and multi-item-ready: one entry today, drawn as a **list** so a second real tool is one more row rather than a redesign, with **no placeholder cards, no "Coming soon" and no fixed empty slots**, and hidden entirely when there is nothing real to show.

  **(k) An engineering migration, not a product decision.** `user_current_unique` and the old `user_1` share the `{ user: 1 }` key, so Mongoose's `autoIndex` raises `IndexOptionsConflict` and **silently skips the new one** — the constraint would sit in the code and be absent from the database. **`npm run migrate:membership-uniqueness`** drops the old index, syncs the new one, and **refuses to run, reporting the offending users, if any user already holds two live memberships** rather than guessing how to merge them.

  **(l) `main_contractor` is now `קבלן ביצוע ראשי` in Hebrew; English stays Main Contractor.** The old `קבלן ראשי` collided with the Hebrew for the **per-project role** `gc` on [[memberships]] — a job title on a company relationship and a role on a project must not share a string. `קבלן ראשי` therefore remains correct where it labels the project role. **The code did not move**, exactly as `site_manager` did not when its Hebrew was reworded twice. **This is documentation-only so far: `Frontend/src/i18n/strings.he.ts` on `feature/employee-management-react` still ships `קבלן ראשי` and is now non-canonical** — recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks).

  **(m) [D6](#7-open-decisions) attribution closed.** When work is delegated the Flexibility Score impact belongs to the **delegator**, not the delegate, because the delegator remains the party responsible toward the project — the same reasoning that makes [[auditentries]]`.actor` always the delegator and keeps the delegate out of every serializer. [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1)'s two-role wrinkle is resolved by it and is no longer part of that row. **What stays open in D6 is arithmetic alone, and no formula, weighting, points, decay or cold-start number was invented here.**

- `[2026-08-29]` **[D1](#7-open-decisions) is CLOSED by owner decision: Multer → GridFS, with `fileassets` as the metadata and ownership layer.**
  The approved architecture, in the owner's terms: **Multer** for multipart/form-data parsing and validation, **GridFS-backed persistent storage**, and `fileassets` as the metadata / ownership layer. The reasons: the project supports files that may reach approximately **30 MB**; MongoDB has a **16 MB per-document limit**, so ordinary inline Buffer storage is unsuitable; the Heroku and local filesystems are **ephemeral** and cannot be used as durable storage; **GridFS is the appropriate MongoDB mechanism for files beyond the ordinary document-size limit**; and the storage implementation stays **encapsulated behind the file-storage service boundary**. **Nothing competing may be introduced** — not local durable disk, not S3, not Cloudinary.
  **The decision cost the schema nothing, which was the point of how it was written.** `fileassets.storage` has always been `{ driver, fileId }` rather than a GridFS-shaped field, precisely so that closing D1 would set a value instead of forcing a migration. `driver` now reads `"gridfs"`; a later move to object storage still changes one recorded value.
  **The Decision Map still carries D1 as Open.** It is read-only for this pass and was not touched. **The same closure has to be applied there separately by the owner.**

- `[2026-08-29]` **The schema page catches up with the profile backend: `workentries` added, `fileassets` reconciled, and the stored avatar URL removed.**
  Golden rule 3 — change the model, change `docs/database-design.html` in the same sitting — applied to `5ecc46c`. Four changes, and one of them is a design being **withdrawn** rather than implemented.
  **(1) `workentries` is documented as built.** Ten fields, two indexes, four relations, both entry-type samples, and the real Mongoose schema. The row it earns in §3.2 says plainly that this is **one of the two shapes [D13](#7-open-decisions) names and that the owner has not closed the storage half** — the implementation chose a shape because the feature had to ship, and a build is not a decision.
  **(2) `fileassets` is split into what exists and what is only designed.** The built half is narrower than the sketch: `scope.type` is `avatar` or `work_entry`, `scope.id` is **nullable** because an avatar has no second document to point at, and the allow-list is images only. `versionGroup`, `version`, `isCurrent` and `visibility` are kept and **labelled *Designed, not built*** rather than deleted — they serve the Stage-3 versioned work-plan uploads and the delegation-private file rule, and deleting them would throw away approved design. Keeping them unlabelled would have been the real fault: a stale schema page is trusted.
  **(3) The denormalised `avatar.url` is removed, not implemented.** The page designed `avatar: { fileId, url }` so a picture could render without a second lookup. The saving turned out not to exist — the client never fetches the [[fileassets]] row either way, because the API derives `/api/users/me/assets/<fileId>` from the id it already holds. What the stored copy would have added is a second source of truth, rewritten on every replacement and silently stale the day the serving route changes. The rule this restates: **denormalise a value that is expensive to compute, never one that is merely a formatting of a value you already have.**
  **(4) `travelRadiusKm` moved inside `location`, and `schedulingPrefs` lost its defaults.** The first is where the implementation put it, because it is a property of where somebody works and is written by the same update as `city` and `region`. The second is deliberate: the page designed `default: 2` and `default: 1`, and the code stores nothing until somebody chooses, because a default here is a claim about a contractor the contractor never made, and the profile has to be able to say *not set*.
  Also added: a **Profile upload → GridFS** flow walking the real path — browser → multipart → Multer validation → custom streaming storage engine → GridFS → `fileassets` row → owner-scoped serving → compensating cleanup on every failure.

- `[2026-08-29]` **The My Profile / Edit Profile backend is recorded as built, and the feature is deliberately NOT marked Done.**
  `5ecc46c` on `feature/my-profile.backend` implements the authenticated profile read, allowlisted profile updates, company-data resolution, Completed Work persistence, Multer upload handling, GridFS storage, avatar upload and removal, Completed Work media upload and removal, owner-scoped asset serving, and the server-derived verification boundary. Both registry rows stay **In progress** because **React is still not connected to any of it** — the screens render representative data, Save still saves nothing, and a feature nobody can use from the product is not Done however complete the server is.
  **Three properties are worth naming because they are structural, not incidental.** Company values are **resolved from [[companies]] on every read** rather than mirrored onto the person, so renaming a company changes every profile that shows it and there is no second copy to go stale. The write path is an **explicit allowlist twice over** — JOI strips unknown keys, then the repository writes named fields one at a time, so there is no `$set: req.body` anywhere and a body carrying `status` or `passwordHash` loses it at the boundary. And an **asset id is not authorization**: every file lookup filters on id **and** owner in one query, so a wrong owner and a wrong id are the same code path and give the same 404.
  **The `Completed on FieldSync` badge has no field in the request contract at all.** `fieldSyncVerifiedAt` is written by `workEntryVerification.service` or by nothing. [[projects]] and [[tasks]] do not exist, so today no link can be proved: an entry naming one is **refused** with `WORK_LINK_NOT_VERIFIABLE` rather than stored with the link quietly dropped, and a free-standing entry is stored normally with no badge. Refusing is also what protects §3.3 — a wrongly granted badge would publish the delegation relationship the visibility model exists to hide, and the serializer that could tell the two apart is Stage 5.
  **What was left out on purpose, and written down instead of designed:** heavy equipment ([D14](#7-open-decisions) has no approved schema), ratings (no domain — the payload answers `null` / `null` / `[]` rather than inventing a number), and [[companies]] / [[companymemberships]] schema-page entries (they belong to the organizational pass, not to a profile-shaped slice of them). All four are in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks).

- `[2026-08-29]` **Owner corrections: Employee Management is a defined feature, the standing default was wrong, and one role had two names.**
  Three things in the same pass, all of them corrections to work I had just delivered.
  **(1) Employee Management is not an undesigned feature.** I had recorded it as absent from the approved 36-screen list and therefore without a design. It is a defined product feature — company member management, opening invitations, employee status, pending approvals, individual and bulk approval, member details — and the four routes built on 2026-08-29 are what it will call. **What is missing is the integration, not the design**, and the three places in this document that said otherwise are corrected above. The same misreading as the lifecycle correction below, one layer up: *"no screen is wired yet"* is not *"no screen is designed"*.
  **(2) `standing` had a silent owner default and should not have.** `.default('owner')` meant a request that never mentioned standing was quietly treated as an owner registration. It is now **required**: the discriminator that decides which of two registrations this is should be answered, not guessed at. The React client already sent it explicitly, and the fourteen request-collection examples that had relied on the default now carry it, plus one that proves omission is refused.
  **(3) A role must have one name.** `site_manager` was rendering as `מנהל עבודה / מנהל אתר`, which reads as two roles to anyone who does not already know they are one. The canonical UI label is **`מנהל עבודה` / Site Manager**, everywhere; `מנהל אתר` survives as a synonym for a glossary or a search alias and never as a competing primary label. The English set is title-cased with it. **`regional_construction_manager` was added** — the terminology distinguishes Construction Manager, Regional Construction Manager and Site Manager, and the enum had codes for only two of the three. **The codes did not move.** `site_manager` has now survived two rewordings unchanged, which is the point of a code being an identifier rather than a label.

- `[2026-08-29]` **The employee Register form stops showing fields an employee may not send.**
  `officePhone` and the company availability control are **absent from the employee form** — not disabled, not rendered-then-refused, not shown with an explanation. Showing somebody a field and then rejecting what they put in it is a worse failure than never asking. Switching standing also clears the values belonging to the path being left, so the form never holds data its own UI does not display.
  **The backend refusal stays**, because the UI not offering a field is not the same as the contract not accepting one: a client that bypasses the form still meets a 400. **And the test now reads the request body off the wire** rather than trusting the form's appearance — an employee registration provably carries no `officePhone` and no `availability`.
  **`businessPhone` is untouched and is not related to either.** It is the individual's own number on their own user document; `officePhone` is the company's. Neither is a fallback for the other, and nothing copies one into the other — the same rule [D27](#7-open-decisions) closed, now visible in the form as well as in the schema.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Owner correction: the employee registration lifecycle was never an open decision, and reporting it as one was the error.**
  I built the employee half of Register as "creates an account and nothing else" and reported the invitation → registration → approval flow as an unresolved product decision. **It was already closed**, when Employee Management was defined. The owner said so, and the evidence was in the repository the whole time: [[companymemberships]]'s own status comments spell out `invited` → `pending_company_approval` → `active`, `invitedFullName`'s field note says it exists *"to match a later self-registration"*, the `{ company, status }` index note names *"the invitation lookup a self-registration has to match against"*, and `Backend/README.md` carried the three-step flow in full under a heading saying none of it was implemented.
  **The mistake worth naming is the reading, not the code.** A model documented as *"not built"* is not the same as a decision that is *"not made"*, and I collapsed the two. The correct question was never *what should the employee path do* — that was written down — it was only *does an endpoint walk it yet*. **Rule to carry: when a lifecycle is spelled out in schema comments and a README, treat it as decided and look for the missing endpoint, not the missing decision.**
  Implemented the same day (`19ec45b`), exactly as described.

- `[2026-08-29]` **The employee lifecycle is built: invite → claim → approve.**
  An owner opens a seat with a full name and a company position; the row has **no user** and no permissions. An employee registering with `standing: 'employee'` is matched on **a company holding that name, an `invited` seat with that `invitedFullName`, and that `companyPosition`** — all three. A match claims the seat and it becomes `pending_company_approval`; the owner then approves it, singly or every waiting one at once, and only then is it `active`.
  **Four properties were designed in rather than checked for afterwards.** The match runs **before** the transaction, so a registration with no seat writes nothing at all — no user, no company, no membership — and answers `409 INVITATION_NOT_FOUND`. The claim is **conditional on the seat still being `invited`**, so two registrations racing for one seat cannot both win. Company names are deliberately **not unique** ([D28](#7-open-decisions)), so every company holding the name is searched and **more than one match is refused** rather than guessed at. And the three company-scoped fields — `officePhone`, `availability` — are **refused** for an employee rather than ignored, so the endpoint cannot receive them on a path that could act on them.
  **Authority comes from one place and it is not this one.** All four management routes are guarded by the caller's own `company.invite_employees` permission on an **active** membership — never `standing`, never `companyPosition`. An employee is created with no permissions and **approval does not add any**; admitting somebody and empowering them are two different acts. This is the first time anything reads [D29](#7-open-decisions)'s permission vocabulary.
  **What it does not have is its integration.** ~~Employee management is not on the approved 36-screen list~~ — corrected 2026-08-29, see the entry above: Employee Management is a defined feature and these routes are what it will call. Until it is wired, the flow is complete and reachable only over the API.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Auth rate limiting, and the first 429 in the project.**
  Login, Register, forgot-password and reset-password are limited per caller IP; `/auth/refresh` deliberately is not, and ordinary application traffic is untouched. **Audited before writing anything:** 429 appeared nowhere — not in code, not in the error-contract table, not as a code name — so there was no convention to follow and one was introduced deliberately. It answers through the contract that *does* exist: an `AppError` rendered by the shared handler as `{ code: 'TOO_MANY_REQUESTS', message }`, not the library's own body.
  **Keyed on IP and never on the submitted email.** A forgot-password limiter keyed on the address in the body is a way to lock a chosen person out of password recovery, which is a worse outcome than the flooding it would prevent. The limiter also sits **in front of validation**, so a flood costs a counter increment and never reaches bcrypt.
  **The enumeration guarantee was re-verified under the limiter**, not assumed: a known and an unknown address answer identically *while limited*, so the limiter cannot be used as an oracle either.
  The numbers — 10/15min, 10/hour, 5/15min, 10/15min — are **engineering defaults, not approved product values**, and they are named in one place rather than scattered through route handlers.

- `[2026-08-29]` **A stateless Access Token is now revocable, by state the server controls.**
  A password reset already revoked every Refresh Token, but an Access Token is a signed JWT with no server-side record, so one already in circulation outlived the reset for up to its 900-second life. It is not revoked — it is **invalidated**: the reset writes `users.security.passwordChangedAt`, and `requireAccessToken` refuses any token whose `iat` predates it. The same lookup also refuses a token whose account is no longer `isSessionPermitted`, so **a ban reaches a live token immediately** instead of at expiry.
  **One rule, three callers.** `isSessionPermitted` was widened to take a status rather than a whole user, so Login, Refresh and every protected route ask the identical question. No second account-status policy was created, and none of this deletes anything: closing access is not removing work, and the company, membership and consent records were asserted untouched by a ban.
  **The cost is stated rather than hidden:** one indexed read per authenticated request, projected to two fields. That is the price of making a stateless credential revocable, and there is no cheaper honest version.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Password reset, end to end — and the mail-transport decision that unblocked it.**
  `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` and `POST /api/auth/reset-password` exist, with delivery by **Nodemailer over Brevo SMTP** — Brevo the service, Nodemailer the client, ordinary authenticated SMTP, so no vendor SDK and no API key in the application. This closes what the Decision Map had called the earliest-biting open decision on the whole map.
  **Forgot answers the same way for everybody** — known address, unknown address, suspended account — and the email is handed off **without being awaited**, because reaching an SMTP relay takes far longer than any database work on that path and awaiting it would make a known address answer measurably slower. An enumeration oracle by stopwatch is still an enumeration oracle.
  **The link:** 32 CSPRNG bytes, stored only as a SHA-256 hash, valid **30 minutes** — the number the screen already promised, in both languages — single-use, and retired by a newer request. Unknown, expired, superseded and spent all answer one `401 INVALID_RESET_TOKEN`. A successful reset writes the new hash, spends the token and revokes every Refresh Token in **one transaction**, and issues nothing: the person signs in.
  **Storage diverges from `docs/database-design.html` on purpose** — a collection rather than two fields on [[users]] — and that divergence is recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) as the largest piece of documentation debt now outstanding.
  **Configuration fails loudly or not at all:** `FRONTEND_URL` is required, and the four SMTP variables are all-or-nothing; with none of them set the server runs in an explicit log mode that cannot be mistaken for working.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Owner decision: server-composed text uses the account's saved language, and the reset email is never bilingual.**
  A Hebrew account receives a Hebrew message, an English account an English one. `Accept-Language` was not chosen — the account already records a preference the person set deliberately, and a header describes a browser rather than a person — and **no second email-language preference was created**. This closes one of [D10](#7-open-decisions)'s three surviving sub-questions. The language is read well away from the response, so the forgot-password answer still carries no fact about the account, which was verified rather than assumed.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Frontend: the approved reset copy, loading that does not replace a label, and MUI for one component.**
  The owner supplied the final Hebrew and English pairs for all five password-reset messages and all three actions; the wording I had proposed is replaced. **Loading stopped swapping button text** on all four auth screens: the approved label stays, the button gains a real `disabled` and `aria-busy`, and a **MUI `CircularProgress`** sits absolutely positioned inside it so neither the label nor the button moves. MUI was installed on owner instruction and is deliberately confined to that one component — no theme provider, no `CssBaseline`, no MUI form controls, `color="inherit"` — so it is a component source rather than a second design system.
  **Reset password stopped being a dead end.** Opened without a token, or holding a link the server refused, it shows the approved lede and an email field and sends a new link through the **same** forgot-password call the other screen uses; the request form and the confirmation are shared components, so no second flow and no second API function exist.
