  **And the client got one source of truth for the session.** `AuthContext` had cached a `hasToken` boolean beside the token store — a second answer to a question the store already answered, which went stale the moment anything cleared the token without going through the provider. It is gone: `user` and `isAuthenticated` are read from storage on demand, the only React state left is a revision counter, and a `storage` listener means one tab no longer renders a session another has ended. The approved storage mechanism is untouched.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Owner decision: Completed Work may be free-standing, and a free-standing entry is never FieldSync-verified.**
  **The decision, verbatim in effect:** *"Completed Work may be added as a free-standing professional portfolio entry even when the work was not completed inside FieldSync; such an entry must not be presented as FieldSync-verified."* Two entry types are legitimate — a **FieldSync-linked** entry, and one with **no project or task link at all**, including work predating the account.
  **Why it matters more than it looks:** it forbids a shape rather than choosing one. **No schema and no endpoint may require a work link**, so `projectId` / `taskId` are optional by rule and validation has to accept an entry carrying neither. It also splits the badge from the entry: the verification indicator is **derived server-side from a link the server proved complete**, never taken from a request body, because a client that could set it could claim FieldSync verification for outside work — the platform vouching for something it has no record of.
  **What it does not decide, stated so the closure is not read wider than it is:** [D13](#7-open-decisions)'s storage question is untouched — collection versus embedded array, which ids a linked entry stores, whether the badge is recomputed or frozen — and [D1](#7-open-decisions) still owns where an uploaded image lives. One new open question arrives with it: **what a free-standing entry validates as**, since it has no task to prove completion against.
  **The React screens already assume it** (`e7e40fd`): `CompletedWorkEntry` carries no project or task reference at all, so a link is a property some entries have rather than a precondition for existing, and the representative data renders one free-standing entry with no badge.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Owner correction: Edit profile's Completed work is a manager, not a read-only list — and the reason the mistake was made is the lesson.**
  The React Edit profile shipped with Completed work read-only, on the reasoning that [D13](#7-open-decisions) is open, so an add button and a per-tile remove would be controls with nowhere to write. **The owner reversed it the same day**, and restored the approved behaviour: a user can add entries and remove them, and the screen needs a real manager. The backend support is **a gap to be implemented, not a reason to downgrade the UI**.
  **The reasoning that produced the error is worth naming, because it inverts a rule this project already has.** [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) has said since the Personal dashboard that *a gap in the model is not a reason to drop a control* — the same rule that shipped Browse's filter rail, Create / edit project's photo controls and the dashboard's *Dismiss permanently*. An open storage decision is exactly that kind of gap. Reading it instead as licence to remove the control was applying an honesty rule where a scope rule belonged.
  **Restored in `e7e40fd`**, with the persistence still absent and said out loud rather than faked: removing takes the tile off the screen and reports that the change was not saved, adding reports what is missing instead of inventing a tile, and a reload restores the list so nothing can read as persisted.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Login, My profile and Edit profile migrated to React; the flow now runs `Register → Login → Personal dashboard`.**
  `0911948` on `feature/client-react-vite`, over the architecture Register established: one router, one `LanguageContext`, one Axios client, one token storage, one `VITE_API_URL`. **Login is the authentication boundary and behaves like one** — the real `POST /api/auth/login`, one unified message for every bad credential (verified byte-identical for a wrong password and an unknown address, with `REQUEST_VALIDATION_FAILED` folded into the same answer rather than given its own), the server's HttpOnly Refresh cookie untouched, and `users.language` applied on sign-in, which is the first time [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr)'s account-preference rule has reached the client.
  **Four closed decisions reached the screens in the move, and each is a change from the prototype:** [D9](#7-open-decisions)'s reversal replaced Login's brand-panel bullet, with the owner approving the wording; the `// PLATFORM ACCESS` eyebrow came out, which [§11](#11-decision-log) `2026-06-17` had said would happen the next time this screen was touched; [D15](#7-open-decisions) removed the personal phone from both profile screens and put **email, office phone and business phone** in its place, independent and with no fallback; [D28](#7-open-decisions) put **Company name** on the profile as the professional identity; and [D14](#7-open-decisions)'s gender-neutral labels replaced the masculine `מקבל`.
  **What was deliberately not done:** the section order was left alone — Completed work then Ratings, Ratings last — because this was a migration and reordering an approved screen is a redesign; no [D14](#7-open-decisions) `limited` range, no per-work-type availability and no heavy-equipment storage were invented; and no profile endpoint was written, so both screens render the prototype's representative data over the real name and email — **superseded 2026-08-29 (`13a7e0e`): the endpoint existed by then, the representative data is deleted, and both screens read and write the real API. Kept here as provenance for why the mock was ever correct** Login returns, and say so in the code.
  **Nothing about the visual was taken on trust.** A computed-style comparison of each static screen against its React counterpart, in both languages across 54 selectors, reports zero differences on Login and zero on the Hebrew profile screens; the English differences are the font bug being fixed and the widths that follow from it.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Forgot password and Reset password migrated to React, and neither sends a request — because there is nothing to send one to.**
  `2c225ca`. Both are public routes, because a person who cannot sign in has no session to reach them with, and Login's *Forgot password?* now points at a real one. **The backend has no password-reset anything:** no endpoint, no token, no mail transport, no provider — five candidate paths probed against the running API all answered `404 ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`. So each screen advances to its approved step and states that nothing was sent or changed.
  **Two contracts were deliberately not invented**, and this is the reason to record it: posting to an endpoint that does not exist, and reading a `?token=` off the URL. Nothing anywhere names that parameter, so picking one would have fixed the token mechanism from a frontend branch — the same class of mistake as inventing an error code.
  **One thing the prototype could not do is now done:** Reset checks that the two passwords match. A stylesheet cannot compare two values, which is why the static screen carried a hint and deferred it to "Stage 2". **And the security posture was measured rather than assumed** — a real account and an unknown address produce the identical confirmation.
  Four auth screens now share one shell (`AuthShell`) instead of four copies of it, which is the duplication the separate documents could not avoid.

- `[2026-08-29]` **[D30](#7-open-decisions)'s client half: creating an account no longer signs anyone in.**
  `2c225ca`. `useRegisterForm` stores nothing, `RegisterResponse` dropped `accessToken` so no code in the client can consume one, and a successful registration navigates to `/login`. The bilingual success panel was **deleted along with its strings**, because it told the person they were already signed in and the approved flow no longer makes that true.
  **The order the decision prescribed was followed** — client before server — so no deployed client is ever storing a token the server has stopped sending. **The server has not caught up:** `POST /api/auth/register` still answers `201` with an `accessToken` and a `Set-Cookie`, confirmed against the running API. That is D30 (a), still owed, and it is a backend change that was deliberately not made from a frontend branch.
  **One consequence is left open rather than decided:** registration now lands on Login with no confirmation that the account was created. A notice there is new copy *and* a new success-alert style — `login.css` carries only `.alert--error` — so neither was invented.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Owner decisions: the messaging-deletion layer and Logout — four rules plus one capability, and the GC's authority deliberately untouched.**
  **Not findings-based.** These are owner decisions, recorded as such so their provenance is never confused with the domain deliveries.
  **Deletion, four rules.** **(1) A whole chat deletes per-side only** — it leaves your list and history and **remains fully available to the other participant**; never a global deletion. **(2) An individual message deletes for everyone**, its position kept as a neutral placeholder, `הודעה זו נמחקה` / *"This message was deleted."*, the rest of the conversation unchanged. **(3) Reported content survives**: a message reported *before* deletion keeps its internal moderation copy under the existing retention rules — **user-visible deletion and internal moderation retention are separate concepts**. **(4) The GC's deletion authority is preserved unchanged** — Project-Room moderation removal with its neutral tombstone, and the removal-time keep-or-delete choice. Nothing here narrows it.
  **One intersection was reconciled rather than assumed, and it is [§8](#8-known-issues--risks)'s new edge:** where the **GC explicitly decided a message must be kept**, that **overrides** the author's delete-for-everyone right — **the original message itself is preserved**, not a shadow copy behind a placeholder, and it is never replaced by the deleted-message placeholder. That is deliberately *unlike* the reported-message case, where the message may still leave the visible conversation while the moderation copy survives. **No new retention rule and no new permission was created**; this defines where two already-closed rules meet and nothing more.
  **This replaces a §8 blanket rather than qualifying it.** That item said deletion *"is not defined anywhere"* and that no delete control *"should be added anywhere until this is decided."* It is decided. What is still open is narrower and is now named there: **whether a later message brings back a chat one side deleted** (explicitly not decided), the **schema shapes** — per-viewer hidden thread state, per-message deleted state, the placeholder path, and the **kept marker** — and whether the **moderation tombstone shares the deleted-message wording**. A **standing vocabulary rule** landed with it: four concepts, four names — own-view chat delete · for-everyone message delete · GC-authorized deletion · internal moderation retention — the same discipline [D17](#7-open-decisions)/[D18](#7-open-decisions)/[D24](#7-open-decisions) apply to statuses, and the reason **D17's connection teardown is not chat deletion**.
  **Logout / Sign out is now a closed product capability** and has a [§6](#6-feature-registry) row for the first time. The engineering rides the auth model already designed: the **family-revocation** route the rotating Refresh Token anticipates, plus the client clearing its Access Token. **The banned-user token gap is untouched** — logout gives *the user* a revocation path; a ban still does not revoke until expiry.
  **Nothing was implemented.** Every shape above is owed, not chosen, and no screen or schema file was edited.

- `[2026-08-29]` **Two closed domain sets that this document did not carry at all: the project lifecycle, and removal / orphaned work / replacement.**
  Both were validated on 2026-08-28 and both were **absent** here rather than merely stale — recorded now so Stage 3 and Stage 5 build against them instead of against the bare enums.
  **Project lifecycle and dates (closed as given).** A project is **`started`** when the GC set a date **and** the first task started on it; **`completed`** when all works are finished to quality and **every task is closed first**. **Statuses are computed automatically from the working schedule** — a *record*, not a decision, which is what keeps it inside the never-manages principle. A **frozen / paused state exists**, which the current `planning | active | completed | archived` enum cannot express. **Cancellation is pre-start only** ([D24](#7-open-decisions)); the GC notifies the participating professionals. And the date model is **three values, not two**: start, target, and an **overrun allowance `x` set by the יזם** that **may not be exceeded and is never edited** — when the target moves the **actual** overrun is recorded beside it and the **original target is always kept**. Annotated onto [§8](#8-known-issues--risks)'s *"`projects.status` is collected on no screen"* item, which is no longer a gap in meaning but a gap in write paths.
  **The post-completion tail (findings 2026-08-28, closed as given) — the part the lifecycle above stops short of.** Completion is not the end of the relationship: after handover the **GC alone performs repairs during occupancy**; **תיקונים שנתיים** follow after a year; and an extended **יזם warranty** — e.g. 5 or 10 years — may run beyond both. **Flagged open and deliberately not decided:** the tail's **product scope** — whether FieldSync holds it as a **record only**, generates **repair tasks** for it, or leaves it **out of the MVP** entirely. Nothing in the enum, the schema or any screen expresses it today.
  **Removal, orphaned work and replacement (closed as policy).** **Removal:** the GC / יזם / מנכ״ל may remove a professional **even with active work**, and the platform **must not block removal until commitments close** — `לא אין לחסום`. Removal **strips every commitment**; a professional removed from *future* work while still holding one sees **only that work's details** until it closes. Notification comes from the GC/מנכ״ל or the סמנכ״ל/מנהל אתרים, with מנהל עבודה only under an explicit grant, and **the reason reaches the removed party only with the GC's approval** — which makes the neutral wording already in the design domain-backed rather than merely cautious. **Orphaned work:** the validated triggers are **exactly two** — removal by the GC, and **breach-driven departure** (not paid, agreement terms violated, needs not supplied). **The GC resolves every orphan**; the fact that a different professional was originally responsible is **always preserved**; **ban and restriction remain non-triggers**, and **account deletion was not validated as one** and stays outside the set ([D8](#7-open-decisions) is unaffected). **Dependents are held by default and the GC approves which continue** — `יש עבודות שיכולות להמשיך ויש עבודות שצריכות להיחסם – באישור הקבלן הראשי` — and a dependent **sees only the schedule impact, never that the responsible changed**. **Replacement mid-work:** the successor takes the **same work plus defect fixes**, **how much was already done is documented** at handover, and formal closure with the outgoing professional may happen before or after appointing — but **only after notifying him**, and only with the GC. The **predecessor is hidden from the successor by default** (he may ask the סמנכ״ל / מנכ״ל / מנהל עבודה); the **מנהל עבודה and מנהל אתרים** receive the responsible-change notice; **dependents hear only about schedule changes**; and the platform may carry the are-you-interested outreach message. **Messages on removal are the GC's per-case choice** — `לפעמים מוחקים את ההודעות ולפעמים לא`, never automatic, with the lawsuit case being why *keep* must exist; recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) beside the rest of the deletion layer.
  **The percent-done rule was reconciled against a non-goal rather than left to collide with it** — see [§1](#1-product-overview): it is a one-time handover record, not progress tracking, and binary start/complete is untouched.
  **Residue, all engineering:** the removed-but-committed restricted-visibility state, the notification grant check, and the progress-documentation field. None is chosen here.

- `[2026-08-29]` **The validated domain findings were reconciled into this document, and the standing rule that governs the pass is *domain wins*.**
  **Nothing here was decided in this sitting.** Every change below restates a closure already recorded on the Decision Map — now saved in the repository at [`docs/decision-map.html`](docs/decision-map.html) as the current approved reference — against the four validated findings deliveries (2026-08-24, two on 2026-08-25, 2026-08-28). The owner's rule is that **where a validated answer contradicts an older closed decision, the decision changes to match the industry**, so several rows below are corrected rather than extended. **No product behaviour, wording, schema shape or permission was invented**, and **anything the map marks open stayed open**.
  **The reversal, which is the largest correction.** [D9](#7-open-decisions)'s gate half is **closed by being reversed**: `כל אחד יכול לשלוח הודעה בפלטפורמה לקבלן אמורה להיות אופציה לאשר או לדחות את ההודעה או לחסום(section בקשות כמו באינסטגרם)`. **Connections do not gate messaging** — anyone may initiate, a first message from a non-connection lands as a **request**, and the recipient **approves / rejects / blocks**, where **block is the no-contact state**. Connections remain the network layer. [§1](#1-product-overview)'s bullet was rewritten first because it is the source other copy cites; the [§6](#6-feature-registry) Inbox row gained the **message-requests section** as core messaging rather than an add-on; My Network's documentation stopped claiming a gate in both [§6](#6-feature-registry) and its `2026-08-20` log entry; and D9's **thread half stays open**, which is why the removal dialog may now claim *nothing* about messaging — it was wrong twice, once for the reversal and once because the thread question is the undecided half.
  **One live screen still states the old rule, and no replacement copy was written.** `screens/login.html`'s brand panel reads `הודעות נפתחות רק לאחר אישור בקשת חיבור` — chosen on 2026-08-25 *because* it restated §1's gate, which is exactly why it is now wrong on a shipped, signed-out surface. It is the only user-visible remnant. **The replacement wording needs owner approval and was deliberately not invented here**; [D26](#7-open-decisions)'s constraint still binds it.
  **Rows corrected because they trailed a closure.** [D2](#7-open-decisions) closed as policy — per-task proposals, automatic full-chain movement with per-affected approval, mandatory impact preview, 72-hour expiry that does nothing automatic but hands the decision back to the GC, partial approval at GC discretion, `בוטל` cancellation notices, GC-only aggregate visibility, stacking parallel proposals; **counter re-cascade is closed by *entailment*, flagged as such with the owner's veto reserved**, and the row's own earlier line — *"one round" has to be redefined as one round per edge* — turned out to be the same argument. [D15](#7-open-decisions) closed as policy, with its connection-gate rationale deleted since the gate it appealed to no longer exists; [D27](#7-open-decisions) had already built the exact two fields the policy needs, so the schema half of its residue was paid before the policy landed. [D22](#7-open-decisions), [D23](#7-open-decisions), [D6](#7-open-decisions), [D21](#7-open-decisions) and [D24](#7-open-decisions) were each narrowed to what actually remains open. The [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) cascade note was annotated rather than deleted: **the rules are closed, so the options are no longer parked, but drawing the state machine before coding is still good advice.**
  **[D14](#7-open-decisions) is the one place a finding contradicted something already built.** The 2026-08-28 schema wrote `companies.availability` as a single org-wide enum and listed the "until" date as open. The findings close it — `זמינות מוגבלת צריכה לכלול טווח זמן`, the limited state **always** carries its range — and add a requirement the built shape cannot express: `כן יש משמעות לזמינות לפי סוג עבודה`, per-work-type availability. **The requirement is closed; the extended shape is open engineering.** Organization-level control, general-work-area region, the employee-availability separation and the heavy-equipment half all stand. Golden rule 3 work is **owed, not done**: `docs/database-design.html`, the backend company model, the Register selector and the Browse filter.
  **One principle was missing from this document entirely and is now recorded**, beside §1's non-goals because it is one: `אסור להגיע למצב שבו המערכת מנהלת את הקבלן!` — with the four decisions the platform must never take automatically (`תחילת עבודה` · `דחיית עבודה` · `בחירת ספקים` · `בחירת קבלני משנה`) and the companion rule `הקבלן הראשי חייב לראות תגובות של קבלנים`. It already governs closed behaviour elsewhere, and it **binds the Stage-7 rules engine before that feature is designed**.
  **[§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) gained an addendum, because the delegation model owns three rules it did not carry.** **Departures are asymmetric** — a departing *delegate* returns responsibility to the delegator automatically and silently, while a departing *delegator* either hands the delegate to the GC **by his own disclosure** (and the GC may then appoint the delegate responsible) or takes him along; permanent confidentiality survives both, because the handoff is the delegator's reveal and never the platform's. **Whole-or-part delegation is the delegator's choice**, which closes the sub-task-granularity question the private layer was carrying — the delegated part rides the existing private sub-task model. And **on-site exposure is real-world only**: that a GC may be told `אני מפעיל של x` on site changes nothing about the serializer, and [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) constraint 5 must never be "corrected" against it. [D7](#7-open-decisions) points at all three.
  **What this pass deliberately did not do.** No screen file was edited — the copy corrections on `login.html` and `my-network.html` are recorded as owed. No schema file was touched. No open question was closed, narrowed by inference, or given a wording it did not have.

- `[2026-08-29]` **[D30](#7-open-decisions) closed by owner decision: the flow is `Register → Login → Personal dashboard`, and Register stops issuing tokens.**
  A successful registration navigates to **Login**; a successful login navigates to the **Personal dashboard**. Signing up creates the account and nothing more — **authentication happens solely at Login**.
  **It reverses two things this document had recorded as settled, and saying so plainly is the point of this entry.** The Register backend was built on the rule that *"signup issues authentication rather than requiring a second Login"*, which is why the endpoint answers `201` with an Access Token and a Refresh cookie. The approved flow **does** require that second Login, so the pair Register issues is a credential nobody will ever use — and issuing a session that is discarded seconds later is worth removing rather than tolerating. D30 had also fixed the profile wizard as *"part of the Register flow itself"*; the approved flow names three screens and the wizard is not among them, so **where the wizard sits is reopened** and is not answered here. It still has nowhere to save to either.
  **Nothing was implemented, deliberately.** This was a documentation pass, and each consequence is a behaviour change that deserves its own approved work: Register must stop issuing tokens (backend, plus its README and `.http` contract), the React client must stop storing that token, and Register must navigate to Login. [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) records all three with **the order they have to land in** — the client stops storing *before* the server stops sending, or a deployed client briefly stores `undefined`.
  **The flow cannot be wired yet, and the cheap way of faking it was rejected.** Only Register exists in React: **Login is still `screens/login.html`** and the **Personal dashboard is not even on `develop`** — it lives on the unmerged `feature/personal-dashboard`. Navigating from the React app to the static `login.html` would work in development and break in a production build, since Vite does not serve `screens/`, so it was refused as a shortcut that would be deleted within days. **A router is now owed too**, which it was not the day before: three named destinations need routing between them, and the dashboard needs the `PrivateRoute` half.
  **One standing rule for what comes next:** the remaining React work is the **migration of screens that already exist** — Login, My profile, Edit profile — not a rebuild. Their current behaviour and design change only where an approved decision says so.

- `[2026-08-28]` **The client became a React application, and Register is the first screen on it (`c922c4a`, `feature/client-react-vite`).**
  **The foundation is deliberately additive rather than a replacement.** Vite 8 + React 19 + TypeScript live at `Frontend/`, which is where [§3.1](#31-repo-structure-skeleton--grows-over-time) had already reserved them, and `screens/` is untouched beside them: the old `tsc` build moved to its own `tsconfig.screens.json` and still emits **byte-identical** `lang.js` and `validation.js`, so every static prototype keeps working and [D12](#7-open-decisions)'s double-click requirement still holds. Vite only ever sees `index.html` and what `src/` imports. **No screen was rewritten except Register.**
  **[D10](#7-open-decisions) stopped being a decision and became code**, and the interesting part was auditing what the old mechanism had been quietly doing. Eight stylesheet rules depended on the twin-field duplication. Six were simply deleted. **One was a trap**: `.form-label > .he-text { white-space: nowrap }` reads as a language rule and is a **layout** rule — it stops a label wrapping, and a wrapped label makes its column a line taller than its neighbour, which pushes the two inputs out of alignment. Deleting the spans without replacing it would have produced a React screen that was subtly wrong with no visible cause. It became `.form-label > .form-label__text`. The eighth moved: `html:has(#lang-en:checked)` became `html[data-lang="en"]`, same declarations, attribute written by the language context instead of by an invisible radio.
  **The submit gating moved out of CSS and into state, and that is the one real design change.** The prototype greyed the button with a chain of `:has()` rules because it had no JavaScript; React holds validity already, and deriving it a second time from the DOM gives one answer two sources that can disagree. The five declarations moved verbatim onto `.btn--primary:disabled`, so the appearance is identical — but the button is now **genuinely** disabled rather than click-blocked, which a screen reader announces and the tab order respects. It also picked up the **passwords-do-not-match** check, which CSS cannot express at all: that message had been drawn and left inert since the prototype, with a comment saying it needed JS.
  **The wire contract lives in one pure function.** `buildRegisterPayload` performs three conversions that each prevent a rejected request: an untouched optional phone is **omitted rather than sent as `""`** (Joi treats the empty string as invalid, not absent), `specialtyOther` is **omitted unless the trade is `other`** (forbidden otherwise), and `acceptedTerms` is a **real boolean**, not a checkbox's `"on"`. The two phones are built from separate expressions that never read each other, so **no fallback between them is expressible** — the product rule is now enforced by shape rather than by discipline. Only the **Access Token** is stored, in `localStorage`; the Refresh Token is HttpOnly and has **no code at all** in the client, because copying it into storage would move the long-lived credential out of the one place script cannot reach.
  **Checks, and what they were run against.** 55 assertions drove the real builder through the **backend's own JOI schema**, lifted out of `feature/register-screen.backend` rather than transcribed — all 23 trades, all 9 regions, every phone combination, and five payloads that must be rejected. 22 browser assertions ran end to end against a stub using that same schema: the wire payload, the `201`, the token in storage, the Refresh cookie unreadable from JavaScript, the success view, a real `409` rendering the email-taken message **in both languages**, and the mismatch block. The React screen was then compared against the prototype from the live DOM at **9 widths × 2 languages**: card dimensions **identical in all 18**, same row counts, zero overflow, direction and font flipping correctly, no JS errors. **Three early failures were bugs in the test harness, not the screen** — React detects checkbox changes from `click` rather than `change`, maps `onBlur` to `focusout`, and the browser profile was carrying a stored language between runs; each was chased down rather than papered over by relaxing the assertion.
  **What it does not do.** There is **no router** — Register is the only migrated screen and the wizard is part of its own flow, so there is nothing to route between. Success stops at a bilingual confirmation in the existing design: [D30](#7-open-decisions) is open, and no destination or endpoint was invented for it. **`register.css` now exists twice**, which [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) records with its resolution — deletion of the prototype, not synchronisation of the copies.

- `[2026-08-28]` **The Register endpoint reached `develop` (`9ee8b43`), and the merge conflicted only on documentation.**
  It was merged because the React client had nowhere to send its request: `develop` mounted Login and Refresh and nothing else, so a correct request answered `404` — see [§10](#10-bug--issue-log). **All 23 `Backend/` files merged automatically; not one source file conflicted.** The two documentation files did, because both sides had edited them, and they were resolved **in `develop`'s favour by file-level checkout** rather than cherry-pick. That direction matters: `develop`'s copies were the newer superset — already carrying everything the branch documented about Register, **plus** the frontend contract fixes, [D10](#7-open-decisions), [D30](#7-open-decisions) and the `OPEN_DECISIONS` reconciliation that came afterwards — so taking the branch's copy would have silently reverted four later sittings.
  **Verified before it was applied, not after.** The resolution was staged on a throwaway branch in a separate worktree, and three things were checked by checksum: both documentation files **byte-identical to `develop`**, all 23 backend files **byte-identical to the branch**, and **zero frontend files touched**. Then typecheck, build, the branch's own `verify:register-txn` (all checks passing), and a server started from the merged tree answering `201` on Register and `200` on Login against a throwaway database. Only then was `develop` fast-forwarded — no history rewritten — and pushed. **No product decision was changed by the merge.**

- `[2026-08-28]` **The Register screen was aligned with the Register endpoint, and the three contract changes the backend branch recorded as owed were paid (`7c1e7be`, `feature/register-backend-fixes`).**
  **The comparison came before the code, and it is what kept the change small.** Every field on the screen was diffed against `registerBodySchema` before anything was edited. Fourteen fields, and the screen agreed with the endpoint on **two** of them: the 23 trade codes matched exactly and in order, and the three availability values matched with `open` pre-checked — which is also the endpoint's `default('open')`. Both were left untouched. The personal/login phone was the third agreement and the quietest one: neither side collects it, `users.phone` is still not introduced, and there was nothing to change.
  **All three owed changes landed, and the reason each had to be a field rather than a fix-up is the same reason.** The **name split** is two inputs because one box cannot be divided into `firstName` and `lastName` reliably — no parsing heuristic was added, on instruction, and the split is the one `edit-profile.html` already collects. **Location** became `city` plus a canonical `region` **code** chosen from the nine approved codes, copied verbatim from `edit-profile.html`; parsing `"תל אביב, מרכז"` would have produced exactly the unusable region the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) defect describes, so the screen now asks the question the index can answer. **`trade-other` → `specialtyOther`**, the name `edit-profile.html` already used — two approved screens had two vocabularies for one concept and one had to give.
  **Six renames and six bounds, none of them a judgment call.** `company`→`companyName`, `trade`→`specialty`, `office-phone`→`officePhone`, `business-phone`→`businessPhone`, `password-confirm`→`confirmPassword`, `terms`→`acceptedTerms`; and the bounds the endpoint enforces are now on the inputs — 100 per name, 120 company, 254 email, 80 city, 200 both passwords, 30 each phone. `confirmPassword` is kept because the live schema **does** require it; that was checked rather than assumed. The two phones stay **independently optional with no fallback** — the product rule the screen has carried since D27 and which the backend makes unreachable by putting them on two documents.
  **One row was added to the form and that is the whole layout cost.** Splitting one identity field into two costs one grid cell, so First and Last take a half each and Company name moved to a full-width row; every row from Email down keeps its exact previous pairing, and Region joined the existing field stack rather than adding a cell of its own. `col--narrow` and `col--wide` lost their last user and were removed along with the comment that reasoned about a field that no longer exists. Card height 1131px → 1321px.
  **Measured in both languages rather than eyeballed.** Live DOM over the DevTools protocol using `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`, so the ~492px headless window clamp this log records never applied and no measurement was a cropped larger layout: **9 widths × 2 languages × before and after** — zero horizontal overflow anywhere, `direction` flipping `rtl`/`ltr`, and the single-column drop landing at the same width as before. The submit gating was then filled one required field at a time: the button unlocks **only** after Region and re-locks when Region is cleared, so the new required select is enforced by the existing CSS with **no new rule**. A first screenshot pass at 390px showed the card clipped — `develop` clipped identically, which is the headless clamp and not a regression, and is why the measurement was redone properly.
  **What this deliberately does not do.** The screen still submits to `action="#"` and has **no handler**, because serialising this payload needs three things that were undecided at the time: the client origin (`.env.example` says *"no client origin is decided yet"*, and a `file://` page has none a strict allowlist can admit), where the Access Token lives, and where Register goes afterwards. The first two are answered by the React work; the third is now **[D30](#7-open-decisions)**.

- `[2026-08-28]` **[D10](#7-open-decisions) closed on the mechanism, and the answer names a third option in order to reject it.**
  The React frontend gets the **hand-rolled** mechanism — a shared `LanguageContext` plus centralized Hebrew/English string resources, **one field per logical value**, with RTL/LTR derived from the selected language. `react-i18next` was rejected on cost rather than capability: interpolation and pluralisation are the things it buys, and eleven built screens have not needed either.
  **The option that had to be written down is the one nobody had listed.** The static prototypes render every string twice and hide one with CSS — `he-text`/`en-text` spans, `he-field`/`en-field` twin inputs — and that exists **only** because a prototype with no JavaScript cannot swap an attribute. Porting it into React would have been the zero-risk choice for the first screen and was refused: it duplicates every control and gives one value two DOM homes, which is a workaround being promoted to an architecture. [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) already forbids inventing a *second* bilingual mechanism; this row is what stops the *first* one being carried forward past its reason.
  **The cost is now explicit instead of discovered later.** The first screen to migrate has to replace the `he-field`/`en-field` rules in its own stylesheet, so the owed step before any such CSS edit is to enumerate every rule that depends on twins, show the replacement for each, and then verify both languages at the widths the static screen was verified at. **Three sub-questions survive and none adds a field:** whether the URL carries the locale, which language server-side artifacts use, and whether the toggle stays in the navbar or moves into Settings once the preference is on the account.

- `[2026-08-28]` **[D30](#7-open-decisions) opened: where Register lands once the account exists — and one half of it was closed before it was asked.**
  Register arrives **authenticated**: `201` carries an Access Token, a safe user and the Refresh cookie, so there is no second Login. The **profile wizard is part of the Register flow itself**, which approved screen #4 has said all along (*"part of registration"*) — so no separate wizard screen is to be created or reserved, and the earlier framing of "a wizard screen to redirect to" was the wrong question. What is open is only what follows the success state.
  **It was opened rather than guessed because the wizard cannot be built either way today.** The API mounts `/health`, `/health-auth` and `/auth` and nothing else; `features/users/` is a model and a repository with **no controller and no route**. The three steps have nowhere to save to, so building them would have produced a form that cannot submit. `profileComplete` is already on the wire from Register and Login precisely to drive the profile-incomplete nag #4 specifies, and **nothing reads it yet** — which is the thread whoever closes this row should pull.

- `[2026-08-28]` **Documented the silent `.env` drift risk around `TERMS_VERSION` — no behaviour changed.** Registering the trap in §8 while it is still harmless, because it stops being harmless the day Terms are published and the failure mode is invisible: the server starts normally and writes `draft` into an append-only consent record that [D25](#7-open-decisions) requires to stay provable. Also noted in `Backend/README.md` and `.env.example`, since whoever sets up next is who hits it.

- `[2026-08-28]` **[D25](#7-open-decisions) and [D27](#7-open-decisions) closed: consent is now recorded rather than thrown away, and the two business phones are confirmed where they already were.**
  **D27 needed no code, and that is the whole entry for it.** `companies.officePhone`, `users.businessPhone`, both optional, both independent, no fallback, `users.phone` never collected by Register — all of it was implemented and verified when the Company entity landed. The approval **confirms shipped behaviour**; what it adds is that the arrangement is now backed by a decision instead of by an instruction inherited mid-branch. The one thing it deliberately does **not** settle is **visibility** — which of the three a public profile shows is [D15](#7-open-decisions), still open.
  **D25 did need code: `acceptedTerms` was validated and then discarded.** Register required it to be `true` and stored nothing, on the reasoning that D25 had not settled whether the documents it names would exist. The approved rule is that **acceptance must be provable later**, and a boolean cannot do that: it cannot say *what* was agreed to once the Terms change. Each consent is now recorded as `users.termsAcceptances[]` — the **Terms version** and the **timestamp**. The boolean itself is still not stored, because it carries nothing the dated entry does not.
  **Appended to, never overwritten, and embedded rather than a collection.** Accepting a later version adds an entry instead of replacing the earlier one, so the proof of the first acceptance survives the second — which is the actual point of the requirement. It stays on [[users]] because it is small and bounded: one entry per Terms version a person has seen, which is the schema document's own embed criterion.
  **The version comes from the deployment, not the request.** A new `TERMS_VERSION` config value is read by `config/env.ts` — still the only reader of `process.env` — and passed into the Register use case. Taking it from the request body was rejected: the server knows which version it is serving, and a client could claim any value.
  **What this does not do, said plainly rather than left to be discovered.** **No Terms or Privacy document has been written**, no screen exists, and `register.html` still links both to `href="#"`. So the version recorded today is the honest placeholder `draft`, and a real one replaces it when a document is published. Whether the client should also echo the version it actually rendered is worth deciding then — today there is nothing to echo. The owner's 2026-08-24 constraint is untouched and still binds: **if** they are added, Terms and Privacy are two separate screens, never one combined `Legal` screen.
  **Verified rather than assumed.** The transaction script now asserts the acceptance is persisted with a version and a real `Date`, and that no `acceptedTerms` boolean reaches the document — 19 checks, all passing. Over HTTP: a consented signup stores `[{ version: "draft", acceptedAt: … }]`, `acceptedTerms: false` is still refused with `400` and creates **neither a user nor an orphan company**, the subdocuments carry no useless `_id`, and the safe login response does not expose the acceptance record. Both phone placements re-checked in the same run.

- `[2026-08-28]` **[D28](#7-open-decisions) closed: Company name is required at owner registration, public, and searchable by users — and the approval was kept narrow on purpose.**
  **Nothing in the backend changed, which is the useful part of this entry.** `companyName` has been `required` in the Register JOI schema since the endpoint was written, because `register.html` had marked the field required since `15acaa3`. The approval **confirms behaviour that already shipped** rather than asking for a change — the value of closing the row is that the behaviour is now backed by a decision instead of by an assumption inherited from a screen.
  **Public and searchable retroactively justifies where the wording came from.** The terminology was taken from the browse-contractors search placeholder `שם או חברה` / *Name or company*, which implied both that a company name is shown and that it can be searched — and D28 had flagged that **nothing had approved either**. Both are now approved.
  **The approval is scoped to `companyName` and was deliberately not widened.** It grants no visibility to `companies.officePhone`, which stays bound to the still-open [D15](#7-open-decisions) question of what a public profile shows. It does not make a company a browsable entity, and gives it no profile, route or screen. And it **does not make the name unique** — two unrelated businesses may legitimately share one, a unique index would fail a second signup for a reason the user could not fix, and searchability changes none of that.
  **No index was added, and that is the standing practice rather than an oversight.** Nothing reads or searches companies yet; an index lands with the query that needs it, the same rule the error-code table follows. One note recorded for whoever builds the search: it must pass a Hebrew collation (`{ locale: 'he' }`), for the same reason [§3.2](#32-data-model-entity-index--full-design-in-the-companion-html) already records against sorting user names — default byte order is not alphabetical in Hebrew.
  **What this row exposed and does not resolve**, because it belongs to a different piece of work: there is still **no company-invitation flow built**. [[companymemberships]] can express invitation → self-registration → owner approval in full, and no endpoint walks any of it.

- `[2026-08-28]` **Correction: the four owner permission codes were wrongly removed earlier the same day, and are restored (`feature/register-screen.backend`).**
  **What went wrong.** The rule *"do not pre-create a permission-code vocabulary"* was read as *"there are no approved permission codes"*, and all four were deleted, leaving `COMPANY_PERMISSIONS` empty and a newly registered owner holding `permissions: []`. That was an over-correction. **Create projects, create tasks, manage their own company, and add/invite employees were already approved as owner defaults** — the thing never approved was a *broader* vocabulary for capabilities that do not exist. Those are different questions, and conflating them stripped a signed-up owner of the authority the product rule gives them.
  **Restored, and the list is now closed at exactly four.** `project.create`, `task.create`, `company.manage`, `company.invite_employees` — one per approved capability, no fifth. Public Register grants an owner all four at signup; an **employee receives none of them by default**, which is unchanged and was never in question. The Mongoose `enum` is back for the same reason it was removed: with a populated list it is a real constraint, whereas an empty one was measured to be silently no constraint at all.
  **The no-pre-creation rule survives, and is now enforced rather than described.** `CompanyPermission` is derived from the closed list, so a fifth code is a **compile error** until the capability it names is implemented and approved. Verified both directions: the four approved codes compile, and `'project.delete'` fails with *Type '"project.delete"' is not assignable to type '"company.invite_employees" | "company.manage" | "project.create" | "task.create"'*.
  **Written now, though nothing reads them yet.** None of the four capabilities is built, so no endpoint checks a permission today. They are still granted at signup so the rule holds for every account created before the check exists — no backfill, and no owner who silently has no authority. Whatever authorization layer arrives **must implement these defaults as they stand and must not restate the product rule**, and neither set may be derived from `companyPosition`. The verification script now asserts both halves: the owner row carries all four, and the employee default set is empty.

- `[2026-08-28]` **[D29](#7-open-decisions) closed: two coined names approved, and the permission vocabulary emptied rather than ratified (`feature/register-screen.backend`).**
  **`pending_company_approval` and `standing: 'owner' | 'employee'` are approved as written.** Standing's meaning was pinned in the process: it is the person's **organizational standing within the company**, and it is neither their professional position nor their permissions. Three fields, three questions, none derived from another — which is the same separation the position field already carried.
  **The four permission codes were rejected, and the rule behind the rejection is the durable part.** `project.create`, `task.create`, `company.manage` and `company.invite_employees` were faithful transcriptions of the approved rule's own wording — and that was the problem: **a permission-code vocabulary may not be pre-created.** A code is added only when the capability it names is implemented and approved, never ahead of it, so the list can never describe a power the platform does not have. None of those four capabilities exists, so none of the codes should have.
  **All four were removed, and the emptiness is enforced rather than described.** `COMPANY_PERMISSIONS` is now `[] as const`, which makes `CompanyPermission` resolve to `never` — so the **compiler rejects any code that has not been approved**, and the first approved capability makes the type meaningful in the same edit. Verified, not assumed: a probe assigning `'project.create'` fails with *Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'never'*. The schema deliberately declares **no `enum`** on `permissions`, because Mongoose was measured treating an empty enum as *no constraint at all* — it would have read like a guard while being none. Both come back together with the first code.
  **What did not change.** The rules were never in question: permissions exist separately from `companyPosition`, an **employee receives none by default**, a position grants no authority, and owner and employee defaults stay **two separate decisions that may differ** — both are empty today only because there is nothing to grant, and an employee must never inherit an owner's set. Public Register now writes `permissions: []`. The transaction, the three-document commit, the request contract and every Login/Refresh behaviour are untouched; the verification script asserts the empty array in place of the four codes and still passes all sixteen checks.

- `[2026-08-28]` **Register became transactional, and the company relationship moved off the person onto its own document — approved product rules, applied the same day the first version shipped (`feature/register-screen.backend`).**
  **The compensating delete is gone, and it was never the target.** That morning's Register created a company, created a user, and deleted the company by hand if the user write lost the race — the honest best available on a standalone `mongod`. The approved requirement is stronger: **either the Company, the User and the owner relationship all commit, or none of them exists.** The local database is a single-member replica set, so `session.withTransaction` was available and is now what Register uses. `runInTransaction` lives in `db/mongoose.ts` beside the connection it needs, repositories accept a session, and the use case receives the runner as a dependency — **no second database-access layer was introduced**, which was an explicit instruction. Two things stay deliberately *outside* the transaction: the duplicate-email check, so the ordinary duplicate writes nothing at all, and the bcrypt hash, because holding a transaction open across a quarter-second of CPU widens the write-conflict window for nothing. Tokens are issued **after** the commit, so a session can never be handed out for a user that was rolled back.
  **The four `company*` fields came off [[users]], and the reason is a rule about people who do not have accounts yet.** Three days earlier this document argued for a foreign key on the person, and named its own upgrade path: *"this pair promotes into a `companymemberships` collection — a migration, not a redesign."* That is what happened, and the trigger was not multi-company membership. It was **invitations**: an owner will add employees with a full name and a company position **before those employees have accounts**, and a relationship with no user cannot be a field on a user. So [[companymemberships]] exists, `users` carries **no company fields at all**, and — by explicit instruction — there is no "contractor account type" and no "employee account type". A User is a person; how they stand to a business is a separate document.
  **Five concepts, five fields, and none of them derives from another.** `standing` (owner / employee) · `status` · `companyPosition` (the job) · `permissions` (what they may do) · and the trade, which stays on the person as `specialties`. **A Site Manager is granted nothing by being a Site Manager** — permissions are explicit, an owner from public Register receives every capability the platform names, and an **employee receives none by default**. That separation was the instruction; the four code strings that implement it are transcriptions and are flagged in **[D29](#7-open-decisions)**.
  **The invitation rule became structural rather than a filter, which is the part worth keeping.** The pending-activation list an owner approves from **is the invited row itself**, claimed by a registration that matched it. Somebody who merely searches for or types a company name has no row to claim, so they can never appear in that list — there is nothing to filter out. A remembered filter can be forgotten; this cannot. Two indexes carry the rest: `{ company, status }` serves the future pending list and the invitation lookup, and a **partial unique** index on `{ user }` where `status: 'active'` enforces one active relationship per person, using the same technique [[subscriptions]] already uses for one active row per user — verified, a second active membership is refused with `E11000`. **[Superseded 2026-08-29 — see the `user_current_unique` entry above.](#3-architecture--main-flow)** That index constrained `active` alone, so *active at company A + pending at company B* was still writable; it has been replaced by one covering both live states, and uniqueness no longer applies to `active` only.
  **None of the employee flow is built, on instruction, and that is why it is written down.** Owner invites staff (name + position only, no account created) → employee self-registers and must **match an existing invitation** → the row becomes `pending_company_approval` → the owner approves one, several, or all. It is documented in `Backend/README.md` and in `docs/database-design.html` so a future endpoint cannot contradict the model; **no endpoint walks any of it today.** Two further rules recorded, not implemented: an employee is not an independent professional profile and **must not appear in Browse** (Browse selects people holding an `owner` relationship, so no flag was denormalised onto [[users]]), and **employee availability is a separate future concept** whose vocabulary was deliberately **not invented** — `companies.availability` stays the organization's *work* availability and may not be reused for it.
  **What did not change, and was checked rather than assumed.** The Register **request contract is identical** — same fields, same 201, same `Set-Cookie`. `officePhone` stays on the company and `businessPhone` on the person, still on two different documents so no fallback is reachable; all four presence combinations re-verified. `users.phone` still not introduced. `EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` (409) and `REQUEST_VALIDATION_FAILED` (400) unchanged; the duplicate now aborts the transaction as well, and leaves **zero** orphan companies. bcrypt-only, `confirmPassword` never stored, `acceptedTerms` validated and still **not** stored pending [D25](#7-open-decisions). Login and Refresh untouched: rotation, replay, family revocation and token-type separation all re-run and still pass. **No frontend file was modified.**
  **A verification was added rather than claimed.** `npm run verify:register-txn` builds the real use case with the real repositories and the real transaction runner, injects a failure at the user write and again at the membership write, and asserts that no company, no user and no membership remains — then runs the success case and checks the owner row and its permissions. Eighteen checks, all passing. It is a script, not a test framework: the repository still has no runner, and **this proves the transaction, not the suite**.

- `[2026-08-28]` **Register got its backend, and the company name it had been collecting since 2026-08-27 became a real entity rather than a string (`feature/register-screen.backend`).**
  **The branch could not be based where it was asked to be, and that is the first thing this entry records.** The instruction was to base it on `develop` and reuse the existing Login foundation. `develop` had the *server* foundation and **not one line of the auth foundation** — `feature/login.backend` had never been merged, its sixteen `features/` files existed only on its own branch, and `develop`'s copy of this document contained zero occurrences of "Refresh Token" or `/api/auth`. Reusing Login from `develop` was therefore impossible, and rebuilding it was forbidden. On the owner's instruction `feature/login.backend` was merged into `develop` first (`624bf7d`); the only conflict was this log, where both branches had appended a `2026-08-27` entry and **both were kept**.
  **Register authenticates the new user immediately, and that is a decision rather than a convenience.** Neither this document nor the screen settled it — `register.html` submits to `action="#"` and has no handler. The owner's rule is that signup issues authentication rather than requiring a second Login, so `POST /api/auth/register` answers `201` with an Access Token, a safe user and the `Set-Cookie` Refresh Token, and the client continues into the profile wizard. **No second token mechanism was created.** `issueTokenPair` was lifted out of the login closure into `tokens/tokenPair.service.ts` and both use cases now share it, so the family, the SHA-256 storage, the rotation and both lifetimes are decided once. Measured, not assumed: a Register Access Token opens `/api/health-auth`, its Refresh Token rotates, replaying the spent one revokes the whole family, the two types are refused in each other's role, and the account then logs in normally.
  **A Company is an entity, because a String could not express what the owner described.** `company` was going to be a descriptive field on each person. It is not: a User is an individual and a Company is the business, and the model has to hold a Main Contractor operating through one, the `מנהל אתרים` / Construction Managers and `מנהל עבודה` / Site Managers under it, a specialty contractor with their own, and **a contractor employing another contractor of the same trade** — "independent" never meaning "works alone". So [[companies]] exists, the link is `users.company`, and **five concepts are kept apart on purpose**: `companyStanding` (owner/member), `companyPosition` (the job), `specialties` (the trade), `companyMembershipStatus` (invited/active/inactive) and permissions, which is **deliberately not modelled**. Trade can never imply ownership: two drillers, identical `specialties`, different standing. **Ownership is recorded exactly once** — [[companies]] has no owner field, so the two cannot disagree, which is the trap [D19](#7-open-decisions) and [D20](#7-open-decisions) are both stuck in. This closes the storage half of **[D28](#7-open-decisions)**.
  **The three phone concepts stopped depending on a rule and started depending on the schema.** `users.phone` is personal/login and Register still never asks for it; `users.businessPhone` is the individual's; **`companies.officePhone` is the organization's**, because several people under one contractor share one office line and storing it per person would be one fact copied N times. They now sit on **two different documents**, so a fallback between them is not merely forbidden — it is unreachable. Verified across all four combinations: office only, business only, both, neither. Still **no format rule**, only a length bound, because [D27](#7-open-decisions) records that no valid-number rule has ever been approved.
  **Availability became the business's, not the person's.** `companies.availability` is the single source of truth for whether an organization is accepting new work, with **no per-user copy and no per-user override** — a Main Contractor decides, and everyone under that company derives their status. Browse will choose its own read strategy when it is built rather than being handed a denormalised copy that starts drifting today. This is **not** delegation: [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) is one confidential hop about a specific task, and confusing the two would put a private mechanism behind a value printed on every browse card. Answers the availability half of **[D14](#7-open-decisions)**; the heavy-equipment half still has no schema.
  **Three frontend contract changes are owed, and none was made from this branch.** *(All three made 2026-08-28 on `feature/register-backend-fixes` as `7c1e7be` — see the entry at the top of this log.)* The name split is the load-bearing one: `edit-profile.html` **already** collects `firstName` and `lastName` as two required inputs, `docs/database-design.html` marks both required and sorts Hebrew surnames with `sort({ lastName: 1 })`, and its own Register snippet already wrote both — so the canonical split stays and **no parsing heuristic was added**, on instruction. Register therefore takes the two fields, and `register.html` owes the split. Location is the same fix the owner chose for the same reason: `city` plus a canonical `region` **code**, rejecting display text, which closes the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) region defect at the source instead of storing raw text the browse index can never use. And `trade-other` is renamed **`specialtyOther`**, the name `edit-profile.html` already used — two approved screens had two vocabularies for one concept, and one of them had to give.
  **One new error code, and it is an exception that had to be argued for.** `EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` (409) confirms that an address holds an account, which is exactly what Login's unified `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` and `forgot-password.html`'s "same message either way" copy exist to prevent. It was approved anyway because a signup form must tell the user which field to correct and is already an enumeration surface by nature. **Login and password reset are unchanged.** No other code was invented.
  **What was deliberately not built.** There is **no company-invitation flow**, so Register can only create an owner — the Construction Manager and Site Manager cases the model now expresses cannot yet be produced through signup, and that gap is recorded rather than papered over with company-name matching, which would let anyone join a business by guessing its name. `companyPosition` is stored by nothing and read by nothing. `acceptedTerms` is required and **never persisted**, because [D25](#7-open-decisions) has not settled whether the documents it names will exist. No transaction is used: a standalone `mongod` has none and the dev setup is one, so the email is checked first and **only the company this attempt created** is deleted if the user write loses the race — verified to leave zero orphans.

- `[2026-08-27]` **Register was rebuilt to fit one desktop screen, gained a required Company name, adopted the corrected bilingual mechanism, and was merged into `develop` — the first time the screen's markup and stylesheet have existed on any branch but its own (`15acaa3`, `d47b2ce`, `048d883`, `5a978e5`, `70d5aca`, merged as `3797fe0`).** *(Recorded 2026-08-28, a day after the merge — see the last paragraph.)*
  **The merge is the significant part, and the entry below understated it.** `register.html` and `register.css` were not "one branch behind": they had **never been in `develop`'s history at all**. `e77e9eb` ("splitting project to front and back") moved `register.js`, `register.ts` and `lang.*` into `Frontend/screens/`, but there were no HTML and CSS files on that branch to move — so from `e77e9eb` until this merge, `develop` carried a **compiled register script with no markup to run against**. The merge adds `Frontend/screens/register.html` (+640) and `register.css` (+1271) as **new files, not modifications**. Every Register entry in this log from `2026-06-15` onward describes work that was only ever readable on `feature/register-screen`; they were written here because this document lives on `develop`, and the code they describe was not beside them.
  **`70d5aca` re-did a move `develop` had already made, and that is the same stale-base symptom rather than a second mistake.** The branch forked from `83b9fb2` — the first commit in the repository — so it still kept `screens/` and the build configs at the repo root. Merging as-is would have added a second root-level `screens/` tree shadowing `Frontend/screens`, plus three duplicate config files, so the branch was relocated to match `develop`'s layout first. Pure renames, no content change, `tsc` passes from `Frontend/`. **`develop`'s tree after the merge holds one copy of each file** — verified, not assumed.
  **Company name is required, and the project has nowhere to put it — this opens [D28](#7-open-decisions).** The field was an owner decision and its terminology is not invented: it follows the only company wording the project had established, the browse-contractors search placeholder `שם או חברה` / *Name or company*. It is built as twin `he-field`/`en-field` inputs like Full name, which is also the shape the CSS submit gating already reads, so a required field gates the button with no new rule. **But the string `company` appears nowhere in the data model** — not in [[users]] in [§4](#4-data-model), not anywhere in `docs/database-design.html`. This is [D27](#7-open-decisions)'s problem one step worse: the phone fields are optional and a contractor can complete signup ignoring them, whereas **this field blocks account creation for a value the schema cannot store**. `docs/database-design.html` was deliberately not edited, for the reason D27 gives — a schema change is its own act, not a side effect of a screen.
  **Two [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) defects were closed by copying `c6d53c5`, which is exactly what the 2026-08-25 entry instructed.** The screen now loads the same shared `lang.js` every other bilingual screen loads, so the recorded defect where switching to English and navigating away silently returned to Hebrew is fixed; and the CSS moved from `#lang-en:checked ~ .auth-layout` to `html:has(#lang-en:checked)` with `direction` on the root, so the **whole page mirrors from one declaration** instead of two inner panels. A third defect surfaced only because the page finally mirrors: scoped to `.auth-layout`, the English font swap never reached `body`, so `--font-sans` resolved against `:root` and computed Heebo — **English copy had never rendered in Inter on this screen**. Two latent RTL bugs were fixed with it: the corner registration marks dropped physical border sides while positioning logically, and the language pill used a four-value physical padding shorthand, so its tight side did not follow the globe when mirrored.
  **The brand panel stopped advertising confidential delegation, making Register the third screen swept under [§3.3](#33-hard-product-constraints) constraint 5.** The middle bullet named the feature, its mechanics and its confidentiality on a **signed-out** page — the same string already removed from `login.html` (`aedca18`) and, in another form, from `landing.html`. It is replaced by the approved binary start/complete model from §1's non-goals: `דיווח בשתי פעולות — התחלה וסיום, בלי מעקב התקדמות` / *"Two actions, start and complete — no progress chasing"*. **Copy only: no permission logic is exposed and no authority behaviour changed**, and no shipped file, comment included, now names the feature.
  **Availability now uses the browse-contractors wording verbatim, so a contractor cannot set one label at signup and read a different one in the directory.** `מקבל עבודות חדשות` → `זמינות לעבודות חדשות` (which also drops the masculine-only Hebrew corrected on browse earlier), and `לא מקבל עבודות חדשות כעת` → `אין זמינות לקבלת עבודות חדשות כעת` / *"No availability for new work at the moment"*. Limited already matched. The legend becomes `זמינות` / *Availability* because the open state now carries the full wording. **No status was added or redefined.** The Hebrew optional marker on the phone fields also changed `לא חובה` → `אופציונלי`; English was already *Optional*.
  **The layout took three passes in one day, and the middle one is recorded as a wrong answer rather than a step.** The card was capped at 500px inside a ~778px panel while the page ran to 1.76 screens, so `d47b2ce` raised `--panel-max` to **620px** — not by eye, but because the widest paired label, OFFICE PHONE / BUSINESS PHONE with their markers, needs ~256px of column and 600px missed it by 4px, wrapping BUSINESS PHONE a line taller than its neighbour. `048d883` then rebuilt the form as a **6-column grid**, dropping the `.form-row` wrappers so a tall block could sit beside a stack of short ones — the phone pair and the availability block stopped costing 307px of separate rows and cost 220px together — and put Full name, Company name and Email three-across. **`5a978e5` undid the three-across row after measuring it**: at 1280px those three fields need 536px of box width against a row that has 502px, and the card cannot grow into it because the panel caps it at 603px there, so **no distribution of three columns could show its own placeholders**. The grid went to 12 columns split **5/7** rather than evenly, because handing the difference to the field with the longer example is what holds two columns down to a ~400px card instead of giving up at ~452px.
  **The breakpoint is a container query on the card, not a viewport media query, and the reason is structural.** The card's width does not track the window — the brand panel's share changes at 1024px, so the same viewport can leave the card anywhere from ~396px to its 620px cap, and a viewport breakpoint would fire in the wrong places. Asking the card its own width collapses the single-column fallback to two ~2px slivers where the card really is too narrow, instead of opening a tall single-column band mid-desktop. One root-cause fix came out of the same alignment checks: in the 861–1024px band the brand panel's 40% share squeezed the card until CONFIRM PASSWORD wrapped and PASSWORD did not, leaving the two inputs 15px apart — **that share is now 36%**.
  **Net effect, and what was deliberately not traded for it.** Document height at 1440×900 went **1585px → 1211px** in both languages; at 1920×1080 the whole form through the Create account button sits above the fold in both. Shorter viewports still scroll, and what remains below the button — the OAuth block, footer and secure note — **was not cut to achieve this**. Across all four commits: no field was added, removed, reordered or changed in meaning **except Company name**; DOM order is unchanged, so reading order is identical in both directions, and no product behaviour moved for layout reasons.
  **Checks, and what they do not prove.** All four commits were verified in **Edge headless over the DevTools protocol, reading computed style rather than assuming**: `direction` and body font read back per language; he/en text, input and select swaps confirmed by computed `display`; language persistence confirmed across a reload through `localStorage`, driving the real `עב` / `EN` labels rather than setting radio state directly; submit gating exercised on both language sides, including that clearing Company re-disables the button and that emptying both phones does not; **52 configurations (26 widths 1920→320px × 2 languages)** with zero clipped placeholders and no page-level horizontal scroll; row-level alignment measured at ten-plus widths. **What none of it proves: no human has looked at this screen.** Every result is a computed number from a headless engine — there is no visual review of the identity row, no check in a real browser at any zoom or with real font loading, and no manual submit of the form. The tightest placeholder margin is Company name in English at **20px spare at 1100px**, which is a measurement, not a comfort judgement. `048d883` also shipped a **known cosmetic trade-off** that `5a978e5` is the fix for, and it is recorded here because it was live on the branch in between.
  **This entry is a day late, and the omission is the process failure worth recording.** The standing rule is that every change is documented as it lands. These five commits were authored on a branch whose copy of this document ends at **D9 and `2026-06-07`**, so no entry could be written beside the code — the established workaround, which the entry below states outright, is to write it on `develop` against the live document. On 2026-08-27 the merge happened and that step did not. **The gap was found by the owner asking, not by the process**, and the correction is that a merge into `develop` is not finished until the entry exists.
- `[2026-08-27]` **Authentication moved from one JWT to an Access Token plus a stored, rotating Refresh Token, reversing a decision this project had written down twice (`feature/login.backend`).**
  **What was reversed, and it was not a gap.** [§3](#3-architecture--main-flow) described *"returns JWT → client stores token"*, and `docs/database-design.html` went further and gave the reasoning: *"JWT is stateless, so there is no sessions collection. Nothing is stored server-side per login."* Both are now wrong, on owner instruction, and both were rewritten rather than left to contradict the code. The old model's one revocation hook — `security.passwordChangedAt`, which let the middleware reject tokens issued before a password change — is untouched and still applies to Access Tokens.
  **Why two tokens rather than one.** A single 7-day JWT is a 7-day window for anything that copies it, and there is no way to end it early. Splitting the credential lets the thing sent on every request live 15 minutes, and confines the 7-day credential to one endpoint. **The 7 days did not change** — it is the same session length the single JWT already had, moved onto the token that now carries it.
  **They are kept apart by three mechanisms, not by expiry.** A different secret each, an explicit `typ` claim checked after signature verification, and separate verify functions with no shared generic entry point. Swapping them fails on the first mechanism alone; the other two mean it still fails if a later change ever reuses a secret. `config/env.ts` refuses to start the process if the two secrets match, or if the Refresh lifetime is not longer than the Access lifetime — an inverted pair is a broken token model, not a misconfiguration to serve traffic with.
  **The Refresh Token is an HttpOnly cookie, and that costs something.** JavaScript cannot read it, so an XSS bug cannot lift the long-lived credential. The price is `credentials: true` on CORS and `SameSite=None; Secure` in production, because Vercel and Heroku are different sites. The strict origin allowlist is what makes that safe and the two settings now have to be read together — noted in `config/cors.ts` so neither is loosened alone.
  **Rotation with reuse detection, which is the part that needed a collection.** Each Refresh Token is spent once and replaced. Replaying a spent one means a copy is in circulation, so the whole family descended from that login is revoked — the stolen copy and the legitimate one both die, which surfaces the theft instead of hiding it. Only SHA-256 hashes are stored, the same stance the schema document already took for password-reset tokens, so a database leak yields nothing replayable. SHA-256 rather than bcrypt because the input is a machine-entropy token with no dictionary to defend against, and this hash runs on every refresh.
  **What was deliberately not decided here.** Logout is not implemented, so no route revokes a family on purpose yet — ***(updated 2026-08-29: Logout is now a **closed product capability** by owner decision, and this is the route it rides. Still unbuilt; see [§6](#6-feature-registry).)*** — multi-device session listing, a per-user revoke-all, and reuse-detection alerting are all absent. `docs/database-design.html` gained the `refreshtokens` collection in the same sitting, per golden rule 3.

- `[2026-08-27]` **The Login backend was built, and the frontend Login screen turned out to need nothing from it yet (`feature/login.backend`).**
  **`POST /api/auth/login` is convention, not an approved route.** Neither document names it — the only API path either one contains is `/api/health-auth`. It follows the `/api/<feature>` shape the foundation established and the example already sitting in `routes/index.ts`. Flagged rather than presented as settled.
  **The screen is static and submits to `action="#"`, so "what the frontend expects" could not be read off it.** It collects `email` and `password`, marks both `required`, and applies an email `pattern` and no password rule at all. Its one error state is already a unified *"Invalid email or password"* with a comment explaining the anti-enumeration intent — so the screen and `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` agreed before either was wired. **Where Login navigates on success is still undecided**, and no backend behaviour assumes an answer. **Nothing in `Frontend/` was modified.**
  **Two frontend affordances were deliberately not served:** the *Continue with Google* button and the password-visibility toggle are both marked in the markup as Stage-2 stubs, and Google Login stays out of this branch.
  **The unified error absorbed a case the documents never covered.** Nothing says whether a `deactivated`, `banned` or `deleted` account may log in — [D8](#7-open-decisions) is open. Non-`active` accounts are refused, with the same `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`: a distinct code would confirm the address holds an account. The password is compared *before* the status is inspected, so the suspended path costs the same as the wrong-password one.
  **Timing was treated as part of the same promise.** An unknown email would otherwise answer measurably faster than a known one, because it skips bcrypt entirely — an enumeration oracle behind an error message built to prevent exactly that. The no-such-account path now runs a throwaway comparison; measured, the two paths are indistinguishable (0.99s vs 1.00s over five requests).
  **`profileComplete` and `language` are on the wire for documented reasons, not because the fields exist.** [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) makes `users.language` the account-level preference and demotes `localStorage` to a pre-login default, so the client has to be told it at login; `profileComplete` drives the nag the schema document describes. **`isAdmin` is deliberately absent** — it is authorization, and this branch keeps that separate.
  **The `users` model holds authentication identity only** — seven fields, not the full designed schema. Register, the profile wizard and the browse-and-filter fields are left to the branches that need them.

- `[2026-08-27]` **The backend server foundation was documented, three sittings late (`f287fb9`, `28a4481`, `cd151e6`, merged as `b55b4b6`).**
  **Golden rule 1 was missed and this entry is the repair.** The foundation was built and merged with **no Decision Log entry, no ticked checkbox and no structural update** — a search of this file for `Backend/` returned nothing, every Stage 1 box was still `[ ]` including four the work had completed, and [§3.1](#31-repo-structure-skeleton--grows-over-time) still described a `client/` + `server/` layout that had not existed since `e77e9eb`. The rule exists because a source of truth that trails the code is worse than none, and it failed on the largest structural change the project has made.
