  **What the foundation actually established**, recorded now so the next branch does not rebuild it: `config/env.ts` is the only reader of `process.env` and validates it with JOI; `app.ts` builds the Express app without listening, which is what makes it testable; `middleware/errorHandler.ts` is the single place a failure becomes a response, always `{ code, message }`, with anything unintentional flattened to `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` and detailed only in the log; `middleware/validateRequest.ts` writes validated values to `res.locals` because `req.query` is a getter in Express 5 and cannot be reassigned; and `routes/index.ts` is a composition root where a feature mounts in one line.
  **The layout is by domain, not by layer**, which is a real departure from the `models/controllers/routes` skeleton [§5](#5-current-focus--to-do) had asked for. A branch named after a screen adds code to the module owning the responsibility, so Login and Register share one `auth` module instead of each growing a copy. §3.1 and §5 both now say so.

- `[2026-08-26]` **Register gained two independently optional business phone fields, and the same screen went through a bilingual QA pass (`c402429`, `42b8a27` on `feature/register-screen`).**
  **The work was requested as "the approved D15 changes", and D15 is not this.** [D15](#7-open-decisions) is *phone visibility on the public profile* — whether `users.phone` is public or withheld until a connection is accepted — and its status is **Open**, having been written up as a decided rule on 2026-08-20 and reopened by the owner the same day. Nothing in this document approves adding phone fields to Register. **The change itself was approved directly by the owner and built to a detailed written spec; only the number was wrong**, and it is recorded here rather than adopted for the same reason the 2026-08-20 renumbering entry gives: §6, §7, §11 and `docs/database-design.html` all cross-reference these IDs, so a future reader following *"D15"* into this entry would land on an unrelated and still-undecided question. The spec's own content is now **[D27](#7-open-decisions)**.
  **Register had no phone field at all, which changes what "do not repurpose the personal phone" could mean.** The instruction was that the existing personal/login phone must not be turned into a business-facing number. On inspection there was nothing to repurpose: the form collected name, email, trade, location, availability, password and consent, and `docs/database-design.html` records the single `users.phone` as *collected in onboarding step 3* — the profile wizard. So the requirement is satisfied by construction rather than by a safeguard, and **no personal phone was added to this screen to satisfy it**, because adding one would have been the scope the instruction was written to prevent.
  **Two fields, and the independence is enforced by three separate absences rather than by a rule.** `טלפון משרד` / *Office phone* and `טלפון עסקי` / *Business phone* are two concepts and stay two inputs with two names. Neither carries `required`; neither appears in the `:has()` submit-gating list in `register.css`; and neither uses the `.he-field` / `.en-field` classes, which matters more than it looks — the generic gating rule matches `.form-group > .he-field:placeholder-shown`, so **a language-twinned optional field would have silently disabled the submit button forever.** Digits read the same in both languages, so the twins were never needed. An empty `type="tel"` with no `pattern` is never `:invalid`, so the `.touched` red-border rule cannot fire on either. All four combinations submit.
  **No validation rule was invented, and that is a decision rather than an omission.** The obvious move is an Israeli phone `pattern`, and it was not made: what counts as a valid number here has never been approved, and [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) already keeps error *messages* out of the markup as codes. `dir="ltr"` is set on both, per rule 3's explicit *"do not mirror phone numbers."*
  **The QA pass corrected six things and deliberately left more than it fixed.** Corrected: the Full name placeholder rendering Hebrew in the English form (see [§10](#10-bug--issue-log)); English-only `aria-label`s on both password reveal buttons and on the feature list; a `<title>` reading `הרשמה` where the heading and button both say `יצירת חשבון`; `על אותה פלטפורמה` (*the same platform*) against *one platform*; and `הפעלה מוצפנת` in the TLS note, where `הפעלה` means *activation* and the English says *session*. **Left alone as approved terminology, on owner instruction:** the availability wording, and the brand-panel bullets — where `תזמון מדורג` means *tiered*, not *cascade*, and `האצלת סמכות חשאית` means *delegation of authority, covertly*, against a document that says *delegating work, confidentially*. Those two are the [§1](#1-product-overview) concepts and their Hebrew renderings are fixed nowhere in this document, so they are recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) rather than retranslated by whoever happened to be editing the screen. **Also left, and listed there:** `אזור פעילות` (*area of activity*) against *Location*, which is a genuine meaning mismatch whose repair is a product choice between two defensible labels, and four trade-list pairs including `ריצוף וחיפוי` (*flooring and cladding*) against *Tiling and flooring*.
  **The branch this landed on is not one commit behind — it forked from the first commit in the repository.** `feature/register-screen` branches from `83b9fb2` and has never been merged. *(Superseded 2026-08-27: merged into `develop` as `3797fe0`. The entry above also corrects “exist **only there**” to the stronger fact — `register.html` and `register.css` had never been in `develop`'s history at all, so the merge added them as new files.)* `register.html` and `register.css` exist **only there**, on no other branch; `lang.ts` and `validation.ts` exist on every other branch and **not** on that one, so the screen still carries its own local `register.ts`, and the copy of this document sitting beside it ends at **D9** and `2026-06-07`. That is why this entry is committed on `develop` against the live document rather than the stale one in the working tree. It also means the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) items already recorded against `register.html` — the unmirrored `.auth-layout` grid, the hard-coded `<html lang="he">`, the missing `lang.js` — are all still open and were **not** touched here; the 2026-08-25 entry below is explicit that the repair should copy `c6d53c5`, and that remains outstanding.
  **Checks, and what they do not prove — which here is nearly everything.** What was actually run: an HTML well-formedness parse of the edited file, a duplicate-`id` check across the document, and greps confirming that `required` and `pattern` appear on neither phone input and that no phone selector appears in any `register.css` rule. **No browser was opened at all.** So: **(a)** the two-column phone row was **never rendered**, at any width, in either language — that it collapses to one column below 560px is read from the existing `.form-row` media query, not seen; **(b)** the language toggle was **not exercised**, so the claim that the new labels and the *Optional* marker swap correctly rests on the `.he-text` / `.en-text` classes being written correctly and nothing else; **(c)** **no RTL check** — the marker's pill sits inside a `.form-label` and uses no physical properties, which is an argument, not a measurement; **(d)** the *"submit button is never blocked by an empty phone"* claim is the load-bearing one for D27 and it was verified **by reading selectors, not by clicking the button**; **(e)** the new Hebrew strings have **not been read by a native speaker**, which is the same gap the 2026-08-25 entries record and matters more here because half this change is a Hebrew copy pass; **(f)** the corrected 14px marker was never measured for contrast against `--text-mute` on the cream surface, only assumed from the tier's recorded ≈4.6:1.
- `[2026-08-25]` **The three auth screens now mirror the whole page with the language, and the entry below is superseded on two counts (`c6d53c5` on `feature/login-screen`).**
  **This is the second attempt at one request, and it is recorded that way because the first attempt did not do what was asked.** The owner's requirement was the *screen*: Hebrew RTL, English LTR, *"the page structure, alignment, and directional positioning must reflect the selected language"*, with an explicit *"do not only translate the text while leaving the Hebrew layout direction in place."* The entry below (`4e1cf33`) read that as a language-pill placement bug, fixed the pill, and stopped. The pill fix was correct and still stands — but `.auth-layout`'s `grid-template-columns: 44% 1fr` never mirrored, so the navy brand panel stayed on the **physical left in both languages** and Hebrew went on rendering translated strings inside a left-to-right layout. The owner reported it as still wrong, and re-measuring `4e1cf33` at 1440×900 confirmed it: the pill moved between corners, and it was the only element on the screen that did. **A grid container lays its columns along its own inline axis**, so `direction` declared on `.brand-panel__inner` and `.form-panel` — both *inside* the grid — could never reorder the two columns; it mirrored the text in the cells and nothing about the cells. See [§10](#10-bug--issue-log).
  **The repair is [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) rule 1, not a fourth workaround.** `direction` moved to the root: `html { direction: rtl }`, flipped by `html:has(#lang-en:checked)`. **Six per-container declarations became two**, and the four sibling text-swap rules and the separate font-variable rule folded into the same root selector — the pill now picks up Inter in English, which it never did before. Two consequences were only *visible* once the page could mirror, and both were fixed in the same pass: `.form-panel`'s `border-left` accent became `border-inline-start` so it stays on the edge shared with the brand panel, and the `.reg-mark` corner brackets went logical, **closing a [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) entry open since 2026-08-20**. `lang.ts` additionally syncs `<html lang>` and `<html dir>`, which patches the screen-reader half of the same §8 debt on these three screens.
  **The pill's corner is reversed from the decision taken hours earlier, by the owner, for a reason that only existed after the page mirrored.** The entry below chose *right in Hebrew, left in English* — the mirror of the project convention — and recorded the divergence in §8 to be settled later. Once the whole page mirrors, the reading-start corner is the **navy brand panel in both languages**, and the cream pill lands on top of `.reg-mark--tl` and hides it. `inset-inline-start` became **`inset-inline-end`**, which puts the pill over the cream form panel in both languages. Both options were rendered and compared before the choice. Two things fall out of it: the pill is back to **left in Hebrew, right in English**, so the twelve-screen divergence the entry below opened is **closed the same day rather than carried into React**, and the unmeasured navy-on-navy contrast question it flagged is now moot rather than answered.
  **`register.html` is now a different mechanism, not one commit behind.** *(Resolved 2026-08-27, `15acaa3`: Register copied `c6d53c5`, including the unmirrored-grid fix this paragraph names.)* It still carries the original defect, and its repair should copy **this** commit rather than the one below — which also fixes its unmirrored grid, a defect nobody had named because the pill was the only symptom being looked at.
  **One copy change rode along, on owner instruction:** the forgot-password bullet `איננו חושפים אם קיים חשבון לכתובת — הגנה מפני מיפוי משתמשים` became `מטעמי אבטחה, תוצג אותה הודעה בין אם קיים חשבון לכתובת האימייל ובין אם לא.`, with the English sibling reworded to match rather than left as *"anti-enumeration by design"*. **The security behaviour is unchanged** — this is wording only, and the screen's `.brand-footer-note` still makes the same point a second time, which is worth a look.
  **Checks, and what they do not prove.** Rendered in headless Chromium across **3 screens × 2 languages** at 1440×900: brand panel and form panel swap sides with the language, the pill lands top-left in Hebrew and top-right in English over the cream panel in both, the corner brackets open inward in both, and the back-link chevron points to the reading start on `forgot-password.html` and `reset-password.html`. `tsc --strict --noEmitOnError` compiles `lang.ts` clean. **What none of it proves: (a)** these are static first-paint renders, so `:hover`, `:focus`, the `:user-invalid` red borders, the eye toggle and the forgot-password step-2 view were never exercised; **(b)** **Chromium only** — `:has()` on the root element and the logical border sides carry this entire fix, and Safari and Firefox were not opened, which is the largest gap here; **(c)** the sub-860px check proves only that `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` at a **492px** viewport with zero out-of-bounds elements, identical before and after — Edge clamps `--window-size` and crops the PNG, so no true 390px composition was ever seen (the same trap [§10](#10-bug--issue-log) recorded on 2026-08-20, hit again); **(d)** Google Fonts did not load, so all of it used fallback metrics; **(e)** no screen reader was run, so the `lang`/`dir` sync is verified as code and not as behaviour.
- `[2026-08-25]` **The auth screens' language toggle now moves with the language, and the corner it moves to is deliberately the mirror of every other screen's (`4e1cf33` on `feature/login-screen`).** *(Superseded the same day by `c6d53c5` above: the pill's corner was reversed back to the project convention, and its per-element `direction` declarations were removed in favour of one on `html`. The diagnosis below stands; the placement decision and the implementation do not.)*
  **The pill had never moved, and the cause is that a logical inset with nothing to resolve against is just a physical one.** `.lang-switch` was positioned with `inset-inline-end: 22px` — correct by [§3.4](#34-bilingual--bidirectional-ui-hebrew-rtl-default--english-ltr) rule 2, which bans `left` and `right` outright — but it is a **sibling of `.auth-layout`**, and `direction: rtl` was only ever declared on `.brand-panel__inner` and `.form-panel`, both *inside* that container. A logical inset on a fixed-position element resolves against **that element's own** direction, so the pill inherited the root `ltr` in both languages and `inset-inline-end` meant `right` every time. **Measured before touching anything, because the page direction is not the thing in question:** at 1440×900 the pill sat at `right: 22px` in Hebrew *and* in English, with computed `direction: ltr` in both — the readback that names the bug, since reading `dir` on the page would have shown the panels flipping correctly and hidden it. The repair gives the pill its own direction, `rtl` by default and `ltr` under `#lang-en:checked ~ .lang-switch`. **A second, quieter physical declaration went with it:** `padding: 5px 8px 5px 10px` is a four-value shorthand, which is physical and survives every logical-property audit this project runs, so the 10px side would have stayed left while the globe moved right; it is now `padding-block` / `padding-inline-start` / `padding-inline-end`, matching what `landing.css` and the dashboard screens already do for the same reason.
  **The placement chosen is the opposite of the project's own convention, and it is an owner decision rather than an oversight.** The two screens that already do this correctly were measured first: `404.html` — the closest analogue, a fixed pill on a screen with no navbar — puts it at `left: 46px` in Hebrew and `right: 46px` in English, and `landing.html`'s navbar pill lands at `left: 102px` and `right: 102px` respectively. So the project convention is **left in Hebrew, right in English**. Both mappings were put to the owner with those numbers, and the owner chose **right in Hebrew, left in English**, which keeps the Hebrew default where it has always been and moves only English. The implementation follows from the choice rather than the other way round: **`inset-inline-start`** rather than `inset-inline-end` places it on the **leading** corner, so the rule still reads as a direction-aware statement instead of a hard-coded side. **The divergence is written into [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) to be settled project-wide before React collapses these into one component**, and the tally is exact: **twelve screens outside this flow carry the pill**, eleven of them placing it left in Hebrew, `register.html` placing it nowhere at all because it carries the same unfixed defect, and **three now doing the opposite**. A shared component cannot hold both. *(Two of the eleven — `404.html` and `landing.html` — were measured in the browser; the other nine were read from source, where the pill sits in-flow inside the direction-flipping `.app` and cannot do anything else.)*
  **The fix reaches three screens because the stylesheet serves three screens, and that had to be asked rather than assumed.** `login.css` is linked by `forgot-password.html` and `reset-password.html` under an explicit `<!-- Reuses the login stylesheet -->` comment, and it carries their `.step-radio` and `.auth-step` rules outright — it is the **auth stylesheet under a login-era filename**, a fact this document had never recorded and which [§6](#6-feature-registry) now states on both rows. The alternative, scoping the rule to Login with a new body hook, was rejected by the owner: it would have left two screens in the same flow with a pill that does not move, to fix an inconsistency.
  **Checks, and the five things they do not prove.** Measured from the live DOM in headless Chromium, driving the **real `עב` / `EN` labels** and clearing `localStorage` between runs rather than setting the radio state directly, so `lang.js` persistence sits in the path being tested. **3 screens × 2 languages × 3 viewports (1440, 900, 390) = 18 configurations, all passing:** Hebrew on the **right** at exactly `right: 22px` with computed `direction: rtl`, English on the **left** at `left: 22px` with `direction: ltr`, `top: 18px` unchanged throughout, the globe mirroring to the correct side of the pill in each, and **zero** cases off-screen or overlapping `.form-card`. The pre-change baseline was measured the same way, so the *"never moved"* claim is a reading rather than an inference. Screenshots were read at 1440 in both languages and at 390 in English. **What none of it proves: (a)** Google Fonts did not load in the headless run, so every measurement used fallback metrics — real Heebo and Inter will change the pill's *width*, though not the corner it is anchored to; **(b)** only Chromium was exercised, and while logical-inset resolution on positioned elements is specified behaviour, Safari and Firefox were not opened; **(c)** in English the pill now sits **over the navy brand panel**, which is unavoidable for any side-switching on this split layout — its active label is a `--primary` navy chip inside a cream pill, so it reads clearly, but that contrast was never measured and it is the nearest thing in this change to the instructor's no-navy-on-navy rule; **(d)** its `box-shadow` uses `--primary-20` and is now invisible against that panel in English — cosmetic, seen and left alone; **(e)** no screen reader was run, and the toggle's `role="group"` and label association were not re-checked, so nothing here says the corner change is perceivable to anyone not looking at the screen.
- `[2026-08-25]` **The public delegation disclosure came out of the Landing page, and the Login screen was brought up to the corrected Register type scale (`3a9fd52` on `feature/landing-page`, `e33d935` on `feature/login-screen`).**
  **The Landing block is gone and nothing replaced it, which is the decision rather than an omission.** `landing.html` carried `האצלה חסויה` / *Confidential delegation* as a titled value block stating the mechanic in full, on the one screen written for strangers — the larger of the two instances found on 2026-08-25 and the one [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) constraint 5 was written for. The owner agreed it violated the boundary. **No softer wording, no hint and no second explanation was written in its place**, because a faithful description of the mechanism *is* the disclosure however gently it is phrased, and **no replacement value proposition was invented** — the owner reserved that. The section now shows two blocks instead of three and `.values__grid` goes from three columns to two so no slot sits empty. **The cost is now total and is written down rather than left implicit:** with both surfaces cleaned, **no screen in the project tells an eligible contractor the capability exists.** [D26](#7-open-decisions) stays open and is the only place that gets to answer it.
  **The Login readability pass found fourteen mismatches, not one.** Comparing every `font-size` in `login.css` against its `register.css` counterpart — Register having already been through this correction — the brand panel was furthest off: `.brand-sub` **14.5px** against **19px** and `.brand-feature` **13.5px** against **18px**, with `.secure-note` at **10.5px** and the beta badge at **9.5px**, both well under anything a reader should be asked to work at. Also corrected: form title 28→31, subtitle 14→17.5, alert 13.5→15.5, inputs and buttons 14.5→**17px**, field error 13.5→14.5, divider label 11→13, links 13→15.5 and 12.5→14, footer 13.5→16, eyebrow 11→13, body 15→16. **Hierarchy was preserved rather than flattened** — every value is its Register counterpart, so the relationships between title, body, label and note are the ones already reviewed on that screen, and the 14.5px field-error tier stays *below* the 15px supporting-text floor because it is a different role, exactly as [§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen) rule 6 states. **Containers grew with the type**, following what `register.css` did for its own pass: `--panel-max` 440→500px, brand column 390→460px, inputs and buttons 48→54px.
  **The pass caused one regression and measurement caught it, not judgement.** The widened text column reached the two decorative crosshairs, positioned at a *percentage* of the brand panel: `.crosshair--b` overlapped the footer note in English by 12×12px at 1280px, and `.crosshair--a` overlapped the eyebrow in Hebrew at 1024px. **The pre-change baseline was re-measured to confirm attribution** — zero overlaps before, so this was introduced here rather than uncovered. Both marks moved from a percentage offset to the panel's padding band; re-checked across 11 width/language configurations, zero overlaps, nearest gap 14px.
  **Verified in a real browser this time, which the earlier entry could not claim.** Edge headless over the DevTools protocol, using `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` so the layout viewport is genuinely resized, `document.fonts.ready` awaited before every measurement, and the language switched by setting the CSS-only radio rather than assumed. **13 configurations from 1440×900 down to 320×568, both languages:** zero elements crossing either viewport edge and **zero page-level horizontal scroll in all 13**, with direction read back from computed style (`rtl` in Hebrew, `ltr` in English) and the `.he-text` / `.en-text` swap confirmed by computed `display` rather than by eye. Screenshots were read at 1440 and 1280 in both languages and at 390 and 320. **What it still does not prove: (a)** whether the new scale is *comfortable* for the older users it is aimed at is a judgement no measurement makes — the geometry is clean and the readability claim is the owner's to confirm; **(b)** the two sibling screens sharing `login.css` were **not opened**, and `.back-link` (11px) and `.form-hint` (12px) are under the rule 6 floor there — recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks), not fixed; **(c)** the muted *Sign in* button in every screenshot is the existing `:has(.form-input:invalid)` dim-until-valid rule, **not** a regression from this pass, and its contrast at `opacity: 0.45` was never measured; **(d)** no screen reader was run, and the new Hebrew string from `aedca18` still has not been read by a native speaker.
- `[2026-08-25]` **The Login brand panel stopped advertising confidential delegation, and its footer note was raised to the project's 15px minimum (`feature/login-screen`, `aedca18`, `c528201`, `40f1979`).** Two unrelated corrections on one screen, committed separately so the privacy fix can be read, reverted or cited on its own.
  **The copy that was removed named the feature, its mechanics and its confidentiality in a single line.** The middle brand-panel bullet read `האצלת סמכות חשאית, בלתי נראית לצד שמעל` / *Confidential delegation, invisible to the party above* — on a **signed-out** page, which means it has been telling every GC who ever reached it that the mechanism exists, that the sub they contracted with can use it, and that it is designed to be invisible to them in particular. [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) protected the guarantee in the serializer and nothing protected it in the copy, so the rule is now written down as **constraint 5** rather than left as something everyone was assumed to know. ~~**The replacement describes product behaviour only** — `הודעות נפתחות רק לאחר אישור בקשת חיבור` / *Messaging opens only after a connection is accepted* — which is the connection-gated messaging [§1](#1-product-overview) already lists, exposes no permission logic, and does not overlap the two bullets beside it.~~ ***(Superseded — the gate was reversed by the findings of 2026-08-28.)*** The reasoning above was sound when written: the line was chosen precisely *because* it restated §1's gate. §1 no longer says that. **So this string now states a reversed rule as product behaviour, on a shipped, signed-out screen** — it is the only place in the project where the old gate is still user-visible, and it is owed a replacement. **The replacement wording is not written here and must not be invented:** it needs owner approval, and a faithful one-line statement of the request-gate model is the natural candidate rather than the decision. The [D26](#7-open-decisions) constraint is untouched and still binds — whatever replaces it may not name delegation. Tracked in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks). **No second explanation of delegation was written to replace the first**: that was the instruction, and it is the only safe version, because any faithful description of the mechanism is the same disclosure in softer words.
  **Removing the copy exposed the question the copy was answering, and it is now [D26](#7-open-decisions).** A capability that may never be named is also a capability nobody can discover, and the people who may legitimately use it are the same people the platform must keep it hidden from on their other projects — a GC on one job is a sub on the next. So the boundary and the discoverability problem are two halves of one design question, opened rather than resolved. **`landing.html` keeps its `האצלה חסויה` block until it closes** — owner-agreed as a violation, owner-instructed not to be repaired by deletion alone, and tracked in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks). No replacement Landing copy was invented.

Measured rather than assumed: every `font-size` in `register.css` was listed, and everything below 14.5px there is a mono chip or control label (`.brand-logo__badge` 12px, `.lang-switch__btn` 12.5px, `.divider__label` and `.secure-note` 13px), not reading text. So **15px is the base rule now**, the modifier is deleted, and `forgot-password.html` and `reset-password.html` rise from 12px with it since they share the stylesheet. **13.5px is not an approved size** and is written into [§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen) as rule 6 so it cannot be cited later as a precedent.

A grep of `login.html` for `delegat|invisible|hidden|proxy|behalf|חשאי|סמכות|בלתי נראי|מוסתר|במקומו|בשם` returns only `aria-hidden` attributes. The remote was verified after pushing: `origin/feature/login-screen` and local HEAD both at `c528201` at that point, with no commit rewritten. **What none of it establishes: (a)** nothing was rendered — no browser check at any width or in either language, so whether 15px reads comfortably in place, and whether the longer English note still fits the panel at its `38ch` measure, are unverified; **(b)** the two sibling screens were re-sized by a shared rule and **neither was opened**, so their layouts at 15px are unseen; **(c)** a grep finds vocabulary, not implication — the file was read in full and nothing else gestures at delegation, but that is a reading, not a measurement; **(d)** the new Hebrew string has not been read by a native speaker and is **new, unapproved copy**.
- `[2026-08-24]` **Landing page built as the project's first public screen, on `feature/landing-page` (`d110e6f`)** — approved screen **#1**, on `landing.html`, a filename nobody had reserved.
  **It does not carry the app navbar, and that is the whole shape of the screen.** Every previous screen with a navbar was reached signed in, so it copied `.app-nav` with its notifications bell and profile chip. This one is reached signed **out**, where both controls are meaningless — so it carries a `.site-nav` of its own: brand, Plans, Sign in, Create account, language pill. That is the **404 screen's reasoning applied a second time** (*"a standalone screen cannot know whether there is a session"*), and it is why this is **not an eighth copy** of the drifting navbar [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) tracks. It also sidesteps that navbar's recorded 620–860px overflow rather than inheriting it: five items, wrapping to their own row from **760px**, measured clean at every width. The rejected alternative was reusing `.app-nav` with the bell and chip removed, which would have produced an eighth divergent copy of a component already three rules apart from itself.
  **The dependency visual is a placeholder, and it is built from CSS grid specifically so it can be honest in two directions.** Approved screen **#13** does not exist, so there is no real screenshot; inventing a fake dashboard and presenting it as product was ruled out. What ships is a four-row working-day chart whose caption states, in both languages, that it is example data rather than a product screenshot, that it is **one** example project, and that a real dashboard screenshot replaces it. **Grid rather than SVG was the load-bearing choice:** grid columns follow the inline direction, so the entire chart mirrors itself for Hebrew, where a single SVG would have been an English picture flipped — or, if flipped wholesale, would have mirrored its own text. **The chart also had to avoid two domain errors at once:** it must not imply tasks are independent, so it shows a gate task with two tasks in sequence behind it **and a second trade depending on the same predecessor**; and it must not imply a universal sequence, which the caption denies in words. Original-versus-shifted is carried by a dashed bar, a solid bar, a legend **and** a written sentence on every row — four signals, none of them colour.
  **The ruler counts working days, never weeks.** The first draft labelled it *Week 1 / Week 2 / Week 3*, which would have put a **fixed four-day working week** on the public face of a product whose approved domain rule is that **no universal working week may ever be hard-coded** — the [2026-08-23] entry below, arrived at from a direction nobody anticipated. Changed to `יום 1 / יום 5 / יום 9 / יום 13` before it was measured. Worth recording because the rule was obeyed on the screen that introduced it and nearly broken on the next one that had no obvious reason to touch it.
  **Copy was reused wherever it already existed, which is most of it.** The three value blocks restate `login.html`'s brand-panel strings and §1's own wording; `יצירת חשבון` / *Create account* and `כניסה` / *Sign in* come from the two auth screens; the five How-It-Works labels are the owner's build wording. **Nine strings are genuinely new and are flagged as unapproved** in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks), the hero headline first — it is the first sentence the project has written to describe itself to a stranger. **No price, plan name or quota appears anywhere on the screen**: Plans is a link, and `subscriptions.html` remains the only place that states what a plan contains.
  **Nothing unresolved was closed to make the page read well.** No cascade arithmetic, no notification behaviour, no approval authority, no trust-score formula, no dependency type, and no claim about what happens when a party rejects — [D2](#7-open-decisions), [D3](#7-open-decisions), [D6](#7-open-decisions) and [D23](#7-open-decisions) are all still open and the copy stays on the conceptual side of every one of them. *"One round"* is used because approved screen **#18** already uses it; what one round **means** is still D2 and the page says nothing about it. The delegation block states the approved [§3.3](#33-delegation-visibility-model-depth--1) model and **introduces no label of any kind for a delegate**, which is the confidentiality rule stated as a design constraint rather than trusted to good intentions.
  **Checks, and the five things they do not prove.** Measured from the live DOM in Edge headless over the DevTools protocol, using `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` rather than the iframe harness — it resizes the real layout viewport, so the ~492px headless window clamp that [§11](#11-decision-log) records never applies and no measurement is a cropped larger layout. **24 widths from 1440px to 320px × both languages = 48 configurations:** zero elements crossing either viewport edge and zero page-level horizontal scroll in all 48, with `.sr-only` and the language radios excluded from the geometry walk per the §10 `margin: -1px` lesson. **All five webfonts confirmed loaded before measuring** — and the English pass had to be re-run to get this right, because Inter and Fraunces are only downloaded once English is *showing*, so the first run measured Latin text on fallback metrics. **The language swap was read back from computed style, not assumed:** `.app` direction `rtl` → `ltr`, the `.he-text` / `.en-text` pair flipping, both pill labels inverting, and the hero serif going Frank Ruhl Libre → Fraunces. **The mirroring was verified by geometry rather than by eye:** in Hebrew the chart's later bars sit at *lower* x than earlier ones and in English at higher x, and the navbar keeps Sign in before Create account before the pill in both directions. **The flow chevrons were checked in three states** — `scaleX(-1)` in Hebrew, identity in English, and `rotate(90deg)` in **both** once the sequence stacks below 900px, where a down arrow is direction-neutral. **Statically:** 50/50 `he-text`/`en-text`, all ids unique, all 7 id references resolving, 62 `dir="auto"` attributes and **zero** on a mixed-language element per §3.4 rule 7, **zero** physical inline-axis properties, **zero** four-value padding or margin shorthands, zero `!important`, **zero inline `style` attributes** (the bar geometry was moved into CSS for this), zero `href="#"`, braces balanced at 156, every hex palette or the `#4A5D6E` gradient mid-stop, every class in the markup has a rule and no rule is unused. Heading order is h1 → h2 → h2 → h3×3 → h2 → h2 with no skipped level, and every action is a real `<a>`, so the screen contains **zero `<button>` elements** and therefore no control that could be dead. **What none of it proves: (a)** geometry is not legibility, and whether the chart communicates the cascade *at a glance* is an owner judgement no measurement can make; **(b)** the Hebrew and English copy was never read by a native speaker of either, and the hero headline in particular is a writing judgement, not a technical one; **(c)** no screen reader was run — the four `.sr-only` schedule descriptions and the `aria-hidden` on the bars were verified structurally and never heard, and English text is still announced with Hebrew pronunciation because `<html lang="he">` is hard-coded, which is the §8 debt this screen inherits and does not fix; **(d)** the three destination screens (`subscriptions.html`, `login.html`, `register.html`) live on other branches, so **every link on this page is dead until those branches reach `develop`** and no check here exercised one; **(e)** the first full-page screenshot appeared to show washed-out text at the bottom of the board, which turned out to be a `captureBeyondViewport` artifact stretching the `background-attachment: fixed` gradient — re-measured at a real 1280×860 viewport scrolled to the bottom, contrast holds, and the artifact is recorded here so the next person capturing this project does not chase it.
- `[2026-08-24]` **[D25](#7-open-decisions) opened: whether FieldSync should have Terms and Privacy screens at all.** Neither is in the approved 36, neither has a filename, and no legal text exists anywhere in the project — so the public footer had nothing legitimate to point at, and **none was invented**, because writing a privacy policy for a system whose data handling is undecided would be fabricating a commitment. **One half is decided by owner instruction and is recorded now so no later screen breaks it: if they are added, they are two separate screens, `Terms` and `Privacy`, never one combined `Legal` screen.** The footer ships without them rather than with dead links. The second-order question the decision surfaces: **`register.html` already collects an email and a password and links to no terms of any kind**, so "add them" is not only two new screens but a consent affordance on a screen that has already been reviewed without one.
- `[2026-08-24]` **Three further gaps flagged rather than decided while building the Landing page**, all in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks). (a) **The Subscriptions screen is an authenticated account screen** — bell, profile chip, weekly usage strip, *Your plan* flag, Billing history — and approved screen #1 requires public plan access; the link ships and the concrete repair `feature/subscriptions-screen` owes is written down, including the trap that the public variant must not be built by deleting from the reviewed authenticated one. (b) **`help.html` and `contact.html` were reserved for two screens outside the approved 36**, by owner instruction, widening scope rather than filling it in. (c) **The dependency placeholder is the only visual in the project standing in for another screen's output**, so the swap was tied to #13's own branch rather than left to be noticed — and the note records that a real screenshot is a raster image in one language, which re-opens the mirroring question this CSS chart answers for free.
- `[2026-08-24]` **A pre-existing bilingual defect found by the Landing page: English body copy has been rendering in Heebo, the Hebrew sans, on every screen using the `.app` shell.** `body` resolves `var(--font-sans)` against `:root` before `#lang-en:checked ~ .app` can redefine it, so only elements that re-declare `font-family` themselves ever pick up Inter. **Measured on `my-projects.html`, not inferred:** switched to English it renders `.projects__sub` in Heebo with `.app` correctly at `direction: ltr`. Fixed on `landing.css` with one line on `.app`; **eight other stylesheets still carry it** and are listed in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks). It survived this long because Heebo has Latin glyphs, so the English screens were legible and merely wrong — the §3.4 *"correct in one direction, wrong in the other"* tax in a form no `left`/`right` grep can find. **Not fixed elsewhere here**, per the standing instruction not to modify unrelated screens from another screen's branch.
- `[2026-08-23]` **The working-calendar domain model is approved, and it is a product rule rather than a screen detail.** Owner-stated with the Create / edit project instruction, and validated against how the trade actually works in Israel. **Five parts, all decided.** (1) The project is scheduled in **working days, not calendar days**. (2) The **Main Contractor / Project Owner keeps one reusable default working calendar** of their own. (3) **A new project inherits that default**, so a contractor never rebuilds their normal week when they open a project. (4) **Everyone taking part in a project is scheduled by that project's calendar**, not by their own. (5) **A project may carry project-specific changes** to the calendar it inherited. **And one prohibition, which is the part most likely to be broken by accident: no universal working week may ever be hard-coded** — not Sunday–Thursday, not Sunday–Friday, not Monday–Friday. The field evidence behind it: Jewish-sector contractors do not normally work Saturday; Arab-sector contractors may work Saturday in exceptional cases but commonly do not on Jewish-run projects; **some contractors work Friday and some do not**; nobody works on a holiday eve or the holiday itself; and Chol HaMoed and similar periods **vary by contractor**. So a working week is a property of a *person*, never of the platform. The prototype honours this visibly rather than merely avoiding the mistake — the calendar panel says in both languages that FieldSync has no fixed working week and that the days shown are *the default you set for yourself* — and the Sun–Thu example on the screen is **mock data for that one contractor**, labelled as such. **Where any of this is stored is [D22](#7-open-decisions), and none of it is decided.**
- `[2026-08-23]` **Schedule-exception approval authority is approved: a delegate's approval inside their granted authority is final.** Owner-stated. An exception may be approved by the **Main Contractor / CEO**, or by a person **explicitly delegated the authority to approve schedule exceptions**; when such a delegate approves within the authority they were granted, that approval **ends the matter** and the request must **not** then be forwarded to the Main Contractor / CEO for a second approval. The rule is recorded now, before any exception screen exists, precisely so no later screen introduces a redundant confirmation step by default. **Two things worth stating alongside it.** First, this is a **different sense of "delegation" from [D7](#7-open-decisions)** — D7 caps *who does the work* at one hop and says nothing about *who may approve* — so the two must not be collapsed, and whether the approval chain has its own depth limit is open. Second, **the model cannot express it today**: [[memberships]] carries a per-project `role` and nothing that records granted approval authority, who granted it, or its bounds. Workflow, scope and notification are [D23](#7-open-decisions). **No exception UI was built on Create / edit project**, on instruction.
- `[2026-08-23]` **Create / edit project built as one screen with two states, on a new `feature/create-project` branch (`36c3d82`)** — approved screen **#12**, on the filename the 404 screen reserved and the Personal dashboard linked, and the first screen in the project that has to be **two things at once**.
  **Both states live in one file, and the reason is a limit rather than a preference.** Approved #12 is *"Create / edit project"* — one screen — and My projects already points its *New project* control and its owner cards' *Edit* control at the same `create-project.html`. A file with no JavaScript cannot change an input `value`, so an empty Create form and a populated Edit form cannot be the same markup; they are **two `<form>` elements** swapped by a hidden radio pair, exactly the mechanism the language toggle uses for strings. The switch is rendered **on the screen, openly labelled `מצב אב־טיפוס` / *Prototype state***, with a line saying React turns these into two routes and the switch disappears. **The rejected alternative was a second `edit-project.html`** — cleaner markup, no switch — which would have invented a filename nobody reserved and left My projects' *Edit* control landing on the Create state. The cost of the chosen path is written down in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) rather than hidden: this is the first control on any screen in this project that is not a product control.
  **Every field is one [[projects]] already defines, and location is the one that had a trap in it.** `name`, `description`, `location`, `startDate`, `targetEndDate` and `photos` — nothing invented. Location is collected as **city + region code + address**, matching the embedded `{ city, region, address }` sub-document, rather than as one free-text line: [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) already records that free text cannot populate a `region` index, found the hard way on `register.html`, and repeating that mistake on a new screen would have been the same bug with a different owner. The region list is the one the profile screens already use, duplicated per language because option text cannot use the `.he-text` span trick. **Two disagreements with the schema were found and neither was smoothed over:** the instruction makes both dates **required** where the schema marks neither, and **`projects.status` is collected nowhere** even though My projects renders it on every card. Both are in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks); neither was resolved by quietly changing the other side.
  **The working calendar is shown as an inheritance, not as a setting.** The default state says *Using your default working calendar*, shows the inherited week as a summary sentence **and** as a chip strip, and offers *Customize for this project*; the customised state shows seven real day toggles and offers *Reset to default*. Both actions are **two labels on one checkbox**, so the relationship contractor-default → project-calendar is demonstrated in both directions rather than described in one. **The example week is labelled as one contractor's own default in the copy itself**, so the screen cannot be read as declaring a FieldSync working week — the approved prohibition above is stated on the screen, not merely obeyed by it. A second note says the panel sets **the weekly pattern only**, so holidays, holiday eves and one-off dates are not silently implied by a control that cannot express them. **Working days are never carried by colour alone:** a working chip is filled *and* carries a check, a non-working chip is dashed *and* carries a dash, and the sentence above the strip says the same thing in words. **Nothing is stored** — [D22](#7-open-decisions).
  **Delete Project is present, separated, and says nothing.** It exists **only in the Edit state** — absent in Create rather than disabled there, which is the standing rule applied a fifth time — and it sits **after** the save bar, outside the form, in a light panel with its own edge, so it never shares a row or a treatment with *Save changes*. Its `:target` dialog is the screen's single `:target` component and it is **nested inside the delete section**, so it cannot be reached by hash while the Create state is showing. The wording states, in both languages, that it has not been decided whether the project is deleted, archived or kept, what happens to its tasks, files, messages, history and members, or whether the action can be undone — and that the button does nothing in the prototype. **[D24](#7-open-decisions) was opened rather than answered**, and the dialog was written so that whichever way it closes, no sentence on this screen has to be retracted.
  **Validation is the existing touched model, plus a message.** `novalidate` keeps the instructor's no-native-popups rule; the tracked `validation.js` marks a field touched on blur; an invalid required field then shows the login screen's hard-hat icon inside the field **and a written message below it**, which is the one addition — an icon alone is not an error message, and three of these fields are the only required ones on the screen. **No control is disabled anywhere** (measured: zero), and no cross-field rule was faked: nothing checks that the target end follows the start, and [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) says so.
  **Checks, and the five things they do not prove.** Measured from the live DOM in Edge headless through an iframe, because the headless window clamps at roughly 492px and a direct screenshot at 420px is a 492px layout cropped. **21 widths from 1440px to 320px × both languages × both screen states = 84 configurations:** zero elements crossing either viewport edge and zero page-level horizontal scroll in all 84. *(The first run reported 84 failures, all of them the same false positive — the visually-hidden radios sit at `L-1` because `margin: -1px` is how the clip technique works. That is the [§10 2026-08-23](#10-bug--issue-log) `.sr-only` lesson in a second costume: a geometry check that walks `*` will find every visually-hidden element unless it is told not to.)* **Every state swap was proved by reading computed style back:** create form `block` / edit form `none` and the reverse; the delete section `none` in Create and `block` in Edit; the inherited and customised calendar blocks swapping correctly in **both** forms and in **both** directions of the toggle; and the language swap verified on `.he-select`/`.en-select` and `.he-field`/`.en-field` specifically, since those need a block rule the generic inline one would otherwise take from them, with `.app` direction `rtl` then `ltr`. **Census from the DOM:** 4 required inputs in the edit form, 7 day toggles per form with 6 ticked in Edit and 5 in Create, 5 working and 2 non-working chips per inherited strip, 3 photos with 3 remove controls in Edit and 0 in Create, 2 destructive controls, **0 disabled controls**, **0 `href="#"` dead links**, 1 `:target` dialog. **Statically:** 34 ids all unique, 34 `aria-labelledby` / `aria-controls` / `for` references all resolving, `he-text` and `en-text` balanced at 131 each, 16 `dir="auto"` attributes **none** of them on a mixed-language element per §3.4 rule 7, **zero** physical inline-axis properties, **zero** four-value padding or margin shorthands, zero `!important`, zero inline `style` attributes, braces balanced at 222, every hex palette or the `#4A5D6E` gradient mid-stop or the approved `#A63232`, every class used in the markup has a rule and no rule is unused. Rendered and read at 1280px in all four language × state combinations, at 420px in both languages, with the delete dialog open in both, and with the validation state forced on an empty required field. **What none of it proves: (a)** Google Fonts did not load in headless, so every measurement used fallback metrics and real Heebo and Inter will shift heights slightly; **(b)** the native `<input type="date">` widget is **browser-and-locale-dependent** and was only ever seen in Edge on macOS — its picker, its segment order and its behaviour in RTL are the one thing on this screen that must be checked by hand in a real browser; **(c)** geometry is not legibility, and whether four stacked panels read as a hierarchy is an owner judgement; **(d)** no screen reader was run, so the `aria-label` on the calendar toggle and the missing English `<label for>` association were verified structurally and never heard; **(e)** the prototype state switch was exercised from the DOM, and nothing here shows whether a reader understands at a glance that it is not part of the product.
- `[2026-08-23]` **Three decisions opened by Create / edit project, and none of them closed.** **[D22](#7-open-decisions)** — where the contractor's default working calendar lives, how it and a project override are stored, how one-off exception dates are represented, where holiday data comes from, whether changing the default reaches existing projects, and what a calendar change does to scheduled tasks. The domain model above is decided; **not one field exists for any of it**, and *"two working days later"* is currently uncomputable. **[D23](#7-open-decisions)** — the exception-request workflow, whose authority half is decided above and whose *"within their granted authority"* clause the model cannot evaluate, because [[memberships]] records a role and no granted authority. **[D24](#7-open-decisions)** — what Delete Project does, with the `archived` status named explicitly as the trap to avoid reusing. All three name collections, so all three were added to `OPEN_DECISIONS` in `docs/database-design.html` in the same sitting, per golden rule 3 and the exclusion criterion that page now states. **No field, index, enum or relationship was added or changed anywhere in the schema.**
- `[2026-08-23]` **My Tasks built as one work list on a new `feature/my-tasks` branch (`9fec69d`)** — approved screen **#15**, on the filename the Personal dashboard reserved, and the first screen where a project task and a standalone job have to stand beside each other in the same list.
  **The two kinds of work share one list and are told apart by a tag, not by a section.** Splitting them into *Project tasks* and *Solo jobs* was the obvious layout and was rejected: #15's own wording is *"every task across all projects **plus** all standalone jobs"*, and the whole value of the screen is that a contractor's day is one queue regardless of where the work came from. A row instead carries a **source tag** — a solid tag naming the project, or a dashed tag reading `עבודה עצמאית` / *Solo job* — so the distinction is solid-versus-dashed, an icon and the wording together, never colour. **The screen deliberately encodes no answer to [D5](#7-open-decisions).** That decision is still open in §7, and the tag renders *"is there a project"*, which is one nullable field under the polymorphic shape and one list membership under the split shape. Nothing on the screen, in its markup or in its class names, prefers either. *(While checking this, the schema page turned out to already answer D5 in the affirmative while §7 keeps it open — recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks), not resolved here.)*
  **Overdue is a group, an edge and a sentence — and never a status.** The owner's instruction was explicit that no stored `Overdue` status may be created through the UI, and the screen honours it in a way that is visible rather than merely claimed: an overdue row **keeps the status chip it actually stores**, so the three rows in that group read `בביצוע`, `טרם התחילה`, `בביצוע` and the lateness is carried by three other signals at once — the group it sits in, a leading edge on the row, and the lateness written out in words (*"באיחור של 4 ימים · יעד 19 באוגוסט"*). Three non-colour signals, which is also what satisfies *do not rely on colour alone*. The group lede restates the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) rule that this list sends no reminders, so the *"no progress nagging"* non-goal is stated where somebody adding a `task_overdue` notification would read it.
  **The action set is a pure function of the stored status, exactly as My projects made it a pure function of role.** `not_started` gets *Start*, `in_progress` gets *Complete*, `completed` gets **neither** — the control is absent, not disabled, per the standing rule this project has now applied four times. Every row also carries a quiet *Open* into `task-detail.html`, so a completed row is not left with nothing. That is the approved binary model from §1 put on the row itself, which is the point of the screen: the two progress actions should not require opening another screen.
  **The pending-proposal marker is a second fact about the row, not a state of it.** It sits below the date and **beside** the status chip, never in place of it — the one row carrying it shows `טרם התחילה` *and* the marker. That is the honest rendering: `tasks.publicStatus` and an open [[rescheduleproposals]] item are different documents answering different questions, and collapsing them into one chip would have invented a status the model does not have. The marker links to `reschedule-proposal-review.html`, which the dashboard already points at for the same object, so the two screens send the same decision to one place.
  **Four filter controls, none of them wired, and that is deliberately not the My-projects answer.** #15 names four axes — project, status, due date, type — and all four are present as labelled native `<select>` elements. Status and type *could* have been made live with the hidden-radio pattern; project is a list the markup would have to enumerate by hand and a due-date sort is the hand-written `order` My projects already refused to fake. A bar where half the controls work reads worse than one that is uniformly not yet wired, so all four are inert and [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) says so. This follows **Browse**, whose whole rail is unimplemented, rather than **My projects**, which omitted sort — the distinction being that an unimplemented control is honest and a fake sort would have been wrong. Each language carries its own `<select>`, because option text cannot use the `.he-text`/`.en-text` span trick — the same cost §3.4 already records against the register screen's trade list.
  **Three group empty states plus a global one, and the global one has no CTA.** All four are driven by `:has()`, the mechanism My Network and My projects already use, so the markup is right when React supplies real data. The global state omits any call to action because **approved screen #15 contains no create control** and none has been approved for this screen; the cost — a viewer with an empty list is told what would appear and given no way to start anything from here — is written down in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) rather than solved by inventing a button.
  **Mock data was reconciled against the two screens that already describe this work, and that surfaced a contradiction on one of them.** The eleven rows reuse the dashboard's three overdue items, its five solo jobs and its deadline list, and every project named is one the viewer really stands in per My projects — so Herzl 8, which is an invitation, carries **no task**. Two dashboard rows could not be reused: *Pour ground-floor slab* and *Roof waterproofing* are each shown as **completed** and simultaneously as the subject of an **open** date negotiation. The proposal marker therefore went onto *Plaster finish, floor 2*, an open task in the same project inside the same response window, so the viewer still has exactly one proposal awaiting an answer. The dashboard was **not edited** — different branch, and the instruction was not to touch unrelated screens — and the finding is in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks).
  **Checks, and the five things they do not prove.** Measured from the live DOM in Edge headless through an iframe, because the headless window clamps at roughly 492px and a direct screenshot at 420px is a 492px layout cropped — which is exactly how a false overflow would have been reported. **20 widths from 1440px to 320px × both languages = 40 configurations:** zero elements crossing either viewport edge, zero page-level horizontal scroll, zero overlapping row rectangles. The English pass was then **re-run after `document.fonts.ready`** with Inter and Fraunces confirmed loaded, and all five webfonts (Heebo, Frank Ruhl Libre, Inter, Fraunces, IBM Plex Mono) were verified loaded — so unlike the dashboard's measurement, these numbers are from real font metrics, not fallbacks. **Every empty state was proved** by emptying its list and reading the computed style back: each group's line went `none → block` while its panel went `block → none`, and with all three empty the global state appeared and the filter bar and group wrapper both went to `none`; all four restored. **Census from the DOM:** 11 rows (3 / 6 / 2), 5 project-source and 6 solo, 6 `not_started` · 3 `in_progress` · 2 `completed`, 1 proposal marker, 6 *Start* and 3 *Complete*, **zero** progress buttons inside the Completed group, 6 rows with a counterparty and 5 stating none. **Zero elements carry any `--late` or `--overdue` status class**, which is the check that the derived state never became a stored one. The proposal row keeps its own chip and the marker does not overlap it, in both directions. The language toggle was verified on the duplicated filter pairs specifically, since they need a specificity override the other bilingual elements do not: `flex / none` in Hebrew, `none / flex` in English, with `.app` direction `rtl` then `ltr`. **Statically:** 50 ids all unique, 47 `aria-labelledby` references all resolving, `he-text` and `en-text` balanced at 112 each, 44 `dir="auto"` attributes all on single-language spans per §3.4 rule 7, **zero** physical inline-axis properties, **zero** four-value padding or margin shorthands, braces balanced at 148, zero `!important`, the only `<script>` is `lang.js`, every hex is palette or the `#4A5D6E` gradient mid-stop, and every class used in the markup has a rule. **What none of it proves: (a)** geometry is not legibility — whether the four tiers inside a row (title, source, counterparty, date) actually read as a hierarchy is an owner judgement; **(b)** the filter controls were never exercised because there is nothing to exercise, and no automated check can show that a control a user expects to work does not; **(c)** eleven rows is a demonstration, and nothing here shows the panel stays scannable at sixty; **(d)** the all-empty new-account screen was proved by computed style, never read as a rendered screen; **(e)** no screen reader was run — the `aria-labelledby` composition was verified structurally, not by listening to it.
- `[2026-08-23]` **[D21](#7-open-decisions) opened: what a task row's counterparty is, and what a delegate sees in its place.** Approved screen #15 never mentions a counterparty and the owner's instruction requires one on every row, so the screen had to pick a field and picked `tasks.createdBy`. Two gaps follow and neither is a rendering detail. **Five of the eleven rows have no counterparty at all**, because the viewer opened the work themselves; they say *"Created by you"*, which is wording chosen to avoid an empty slot, not a decision. And **`createdBy` is the one field the schema explicitly forbids showing a delegate** — its own note says this field alone would expose the party above — so a delegate's My Tasks has to resolve the same line to the **delegator** instead, which makes the counterparty viewer-relative in a model that records no such thing. Recorded rather than solved, because the layer that would implement it is the Stage-5 serializer.
- `[2026-08-23]` **Four further gaps flagged rather than decided while building My Tasks**, all in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks). (a) `docs/database-design.html` answers the largest sub-question of **D5** — one collection with a nullable `project` — while §7 still records D5 as open and states both options; the same drift class the GridFS and 404 corrections cleaned up earlier the same day, left for its own review. (b) Two Personal-dashboard tasks are **completed and under an open date negotiation at once**; found here, not repaired here. (c) **#15 says "filter/sort" and none of the four controls is wired**, with the reasoning for uniform inertness written down. (d) **No create control was placed and the empty state carries no CTA**, because #15 approves neither — with the cost to the empty-list viewer stated instead of designed around. Also recorded: **seven labels are new, unapproved copy** — two group titles, the two counterparty strings, *Start* and *Complete*, and the proposal marker sentence — all gender-neutral by the same rule [D14](#7-open-decisions) set, with *Start* and *Complete* the pair worth settling first, since §1 names the whole tracking model after them and this is the first screen to render them as controls; and `my-tasks.html` is the **sixth copy of the app navbar**, carrying the corrected 800px wrap and adding no new divergence beyond pointing at its own page, which My projects already does.
- `[2026-08-23]` **My projects built as a role-organised screen (approved #11), on `feature/my-projects` (`a791231`).** Owner / Member / Invited is **derived, not stored** — `projects.owner` for Owner, a [[memberships]] document with `status: "accepted"` for Member, the same document with `status: "invited"` for Invited — because the approved "(owner/member/invited)" wording is a *projection* over two fields the schema already has, and inventing a fourth enum value would have contradicted the single most important modelling idea in the design, that role lives on the relationship. The per-project `role` code is rendered beside the badge so `sub` / `supplier` / `viewer` do not vanish behind a three-state label. **Role decides which controls exist, and status decides none of them:** the only documented permission in the model is `role !== 'gc' → 403` on task creation, so Owner gets *New task* and Member does not; Invited gets Accept / Decline and **no Open**, because the authorisation query requires `accepted` and a control that can never succeed is one this project has removed twice before. Keeping the action set a pure function of role is what stops status from quietly becoming the axis. Filtering is four hidden radios rather than `:target`, which stays free for a future dialog — and it hides whole groups, so *"this filter matches nothing"* and *"this role is empty"* are one state served by one empty line.
- `[2026-08-23]` **Sort deliberately not built on My projects, though approved screen #11 says "filter/sort".** A CSS-only sort is a hand-written `order` per card, which is a second ordering to maintain rather than a sort, and static data would invalidate it silently the first time a card is added. Recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) as a limit of the no-JavaScript prototype rather than shipped as a control that does not mean what it says.
- `[2026-08-23]` **[D20](#7-open-decisions) opened: what a project card's pending-action count actually counts.** `projects.stats.openProposalCount` was written with a field note nominating it as the source for exactly this badge, and it answers a different question — open proposals *on the project* rather than items awaiting *this viewer*. The schema's own Rothschild 42 sample proves the gap: `openProposalCount: 2`, of which one names the viewer. The screen renders the viewer-scoped number because that is what the Personal dashboard already means by a pending action, and the index that serves it (`{ 'items.respondent': 1, status: 1 }`) already exists. **No schema was changed** — the row records the gap.
- `[2026-08-23]` **Two further gaps flagged rather than decided while building My projects.** (a) *"Owner"* has two candidate definitions — `projects.owner` and a `gc` [[memberships]] row — which are duplicated on purpose but not constrained to agree; the screen uses `projects.owner` and the consequence is written down. (b) **Nothing defines what a project invitation may disclose before acceptance**, even though an invited membership fails the `accepted` authorisation check; the card shows name, city, dates, inviter and offered role and withholds member and task counts. Both in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks); neither converted into a silent product decision.
- `[2026-08-23]` **Herzl 8 mock-data contradiction on the Personal dashboard found and repaired (`13c26dd`).** Four rows on one screen put the same viewer in incompatible relationships to one project. Herzl 8 stays the invitation so the *Respond* control keeps a destination; the sent invitation moved to *Private villa — Kfar Saba* and both `Railing installation` rows to *Logistics warehouse — Modi'in*. **No widget definition or count changed, and no product rule was invented to justify the old data.** Full write-up in [§10](#10-bug--issue-log).
- `[2026-08-23]` **`OPEN_DECISIONS` reconstructed from the repository history, four decisions backfilled, and two unapproved assumptions removed from `docs/database-design.html`.** A documentation-synchronization repair only: **no product or database decision was closed, opened, narrowed or renumbered.**
  **(a) The audit came first, and it changed the diagnosis.** Seven IDs were absent from the array — `D4`, `D12`, `D13`, `D14`, `D15`, `D16`, `D17`. Reconstructing the array at every one of the 17 commits that touched the file showed it has **only ever grown**: `D1–D3, D5–D10` at creation (`e8b173f`, 2026-08-01), `+D11` (`c025b5f`), `+D18` (`1c949b4`), `+D19` (`1d64530`). A `git log -S` on the quoted entry form returns **zero commits** for all seven. So nothing was ever removed, nothing was lost to the 2026-08-20 renumbering — which touched only COLLECTIONS lines 648 and 681, never the array at 2674 — and closure is not a removal trigger either, since `D7` still sits there with `closed: true`. The cause is plain **failure-to-add**, and §7 itself is intact: D1–D19, no gaps, no duplicates, ascending.
  **(b) Backfilled `D13`, `D14`, `D15` and `D17`; confirmed `D19` already present.** Each row states only what §7 already records, with `affects` drawn from the collections those §7 entries actually name — `D13` → [[users]] · [[fileassets]] · [[tasks]] · [[projects]], `D14` → [[users]], `D15` → [[users]] · [[connections]], `D17` → [[connections]]. `D13` names a hypothetical `workentries` collection that does not exist, so it is rendered as plain code rather than a `[[…]]` link that would resolve to nothing. Ascending order preserved.
  **(c) `D4` and `D12` deliberately excluded, and the rule is now written on the page.** `D4` is repo layout and `D12` is Git/build tracking policy (closed 2026-08-02); neither is database design and neither names a collection. `D16` is excluded too — it is an API/privacy-behaviour decision that changes no collection — but it is now referenced where it bears on behaviour. The exclusion rule is stated in the "Still open" lede, because the absence of any stated criterion is exactly what made these look like oversights and forced this audit.
  **(d) The previous gap finding was itself incomplete.** The 2026-08-23 §8 entry named `D13`, `D14` and `D17` but **missed `D15`**, which meets its own criterion and which the schema page was *already* citing as open in two places while carrying no row for it. Corrected by appending to that finding, not by rewriting it.
  **(e) Unapproved GridFS assumption removed — `D1` stays open.** The page had drifted from "here are two options" into treating GridFS as decided: the array said *"The schema above assumes GridFS"* and called the Buffer alternative **impossible**, the `fileassets` tag read *"GridFS metadata"*, its purpose said the bytes *"live in GridFS"*, the `storage` field was typed `driver: 'gridfs'` with a Mongoose `default: 'gridfs'`, and the 16 MB modelling rule asserted bytes *"live in **GridFS**"*. **No owner approval for GridFS exists anywhere in this document.** All of it is rewritten to present GridFS and the capped Buffer as the two open options with their trade-offs. The "impossible" claim was the load-bearing distortion: §7 offers a cap at *~15 MB*, which is **under** the 16 MB limit, so the Buffer option is a product trade-off about capping upload size — not a technical impossibility. `storage` is now `driver: String` with the value explicitly pending D1, which is what the sub-object was always for.
  **(f) Unapproved settled-404 assumption removed — `D16` stays open.** The Privacy tab had asserted *"Returns **404, not 403**"* as a rule, and its response pane rendered a literal `HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found`. Both were written on **2026-08-01**, nineteen days *before* `D16` was opened on 2026-08-20 as an explicitly parked question — so the page and §7 have been contradicting each other since, unnoticed by either. Neither answer is chosen now: both arguments are stated, the pane renders `4xx` pointing at D16, and what stays settled is the only part that was ever settled — **the outsider receives no task payload either way.** The three `AppError(…, 403)` code samples are left untouched on purpose: they refuse *actions* to viewers who already know the resource exists, which is not the disclosure question D16 asks.
  **(g) `D11` title synchronized** to §7's *"Subscription data model & quota enforcement"*; wording only, no change to the decision or its status.
- `[2026-08-23]` **Third Browse review (`a911626`) — one state removed, one axis of meaning removed, and one overlay turned back into layout.** Four corrections, and the first two both narrow the screen rather than adding to it.
  **A blocked contractor is not discoverable in Browse, so the state does not exist there.** The previous review had rendered `--blocked` as a fifth card state and recorded, honestly, that nobody had decided whether such a contractor belongs in the results at all. The owner has now decided: they do not. Card 9 is gone, the badge is gone, and `.contractor-card--blocked` and `.avatar--muted` went with it as dead CSS. The point worth keeping is *where the rule lives* — this is a **query rule, not a rendering rule**, so the fix is that the directory never returns the row, not that the card hides it. The prototype can only record that; the clause is written against the `pair`-indexed [[connections]] lookup at Stage 2. Recorded against the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) finding that raised it. **And writing it down opened [D19](#7-open-decisions):** the rule excludes contractors blocked *by the viewer*, and [[connections]] has no field recording **who** did the blocking — `requester` records who sent the connection request, which may be the other party. So the model can say two people are blocked and cannot say which way round, which is the one thing this rule needs. Flagged rather than resolved in passing, and added to `docs/database-design.html` in the same commit.
  **Connect renders only where there is no relationship — now actually true.** The rule was stated in the previous review and the markup did not fully honour it: the outgoing-request card offered *View profile* alone, so a viewer who had already asked saw a card with less to do than every other state. It now carries *My network*, matching the incoming card. Both request states therefore send the decision to the one screen that owns it, and **Browse carries no Accept/Decline** — accepting a request is a My Network action, and duplicating it here would give the same edge two places to be changed from.
  **Color stopped being a way to say which relationship this is.** Four badge tints became one, and the state is now carried by wording and by icon (a check for connected, the same clock for both request states — deliberately the same, because both mean *waiting*, and the wording is what separates them). The **per-state card top edges went with them**, which the instruction did not name but which were doing the identical job one layer out: four tints of `border-block-start-color` keyed to the same four states. Leaving them would have kept color as a discriminator while claiming to have removed it. **Availability is untouched** and keeps its approved traffic-light exception — that is a different system, it is on the dot rather than the text, and §3.5 already licenses it.
  **The advanced-filter panel is a grid column, because a panel that covers the results is not a filter.** It had been solving a genuine problem — a rail taller than the viewport cannot stay pinned — by leaving the layout altogether, which is exactly why it covered the cards: an out-of-flow element makes no room for itself. It is now a real column, revealed by `.browse__body:has(.adv-toggle:checked)` switching the grid to `268px 268px minmax(0, 1fr)`, pinned and height-capped on its own, with its fields scrolling internally and the rail's Apply still pinned beside it. `:target` was not an option — the profile panel already spends this screen's only one — and the sheet-with-scrim treatment below 860px is unchanged. Full mechanism in [§10](#10-bug--issue-log).
  **The judgment call worth flagging: four columns do not fit.** Rail + panel + results + a 380px profile exceeds the page's 1200px cap, so with both panels open the **profile is what gives way** and drops beneath the results. That is not a new rule — it is the one the responsive ladder has followed since the panel was built — but it is now reachable at full desktop width, which it was not before.

- `[2026-08-23]` **Personal dashboard built as a static screen on a new `feature/personal-dashboard` branch (`cc700ee`)** — approved screen **#5**, the Home route, and the first screen whose whole job is to answer *"what needs me today?"* without opening a project.
  **Four concepts, four definitions, and the definitions are what keep them apart.** The owner's instruction was that *Pending actions*, *Waiting on others*, *Overdue* and *Things to clean up* must never merge or be renamed into one another, so each is defined by a query rather than by wording, and each says its own definition in its lede. **Pending actions** = something another person needs *from* the viewer, and every row carries exactly one control, which is the visible test. **Waiting on others** = something the viewer is owed, and **no row carries an action the viewer can complete** — only a quiet *Track* link. **Overdue** = a task past `endDate` and still not `completed`. **Things to clean up** = an [[messages]] document with an `agreementForm` whose `createdTask` is still `null`. Three of the four map onto indexes that already exist for other screens; the fourth is the sparse `{ 'agreementForm.status': 1 }` index that was written for exactly this.
  **Waiting on others and Overdue are owner additions, flagged rather than absorbed.** Neither appears in the approved wording of #5, which names pending actions, solo jobs, deadlines, notifications, cleanup and rating prompts. They are recorded as additions in [§6](#6-feature-registry) so a later reader does not mistake them for something the lecturer's list already contained — the same discipline the Blocked group on My Network was given.
  **Overdue is visibility and never a reminder.** Approved on the day: showing an overdue row on Home must not by itself create repeated reminders or notification pressure. The model complies today only *by accident* — [[notifications]] are written at write time and nothing schedules anything — so the rule is now in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) as a trap, because a `task_overdue` type or a nightly sweep would be easy to add and would look helpful. It is the §1 **"no progress nagging"** non-goal wearing a different hat.
  **The dismissal is permanent, says so, and has nowhere to be stored — new [D18](#7-open-decisions).** `agreementForm.status` is `draft | sent | accepted | declined` and none of those means *"I have decided not to open a task for this."* Reusing `declined` is rejected for the same reason [D17](#7-open-decisions) rejects it: it already states that the other party turned the agreement down. The control **ships anyway**, per the standing rule that a gap in the model is not a reason to drop a control from a prototype, and the permanence is stated at dismiss time in a `:target` dialog that also says there is no reminder and no recovery. **One question D18 surfaces that nobody has asked:** whether either party can dismiss independently, since both see the same exchange — which is the whole argument between a per-viewer array and a shared status.
  **Availability inside a chip needed a second label length, and that is new copy.** The approved semantics were reused exactly — three states, hue on the **dot only**, text always beside it — but `אין זמינות לקבלת עבודות חדשות כעת` does not fit a chip.
