The chip shows a shortened noun-based label and carries the **full approved wording in `.sr-only`**, so colour is never the only carrier and assistive tech hears the approved sentence. The short forms stay gender-neutral, but **they are not approved copy** and are flagged against [D14](#7-open-decisions), which now also records that the enum needs two label lengths per state.
  **Pending actions and Notifications are told apart by shape, not by a disclaimer.** Pending actions is a work queue where every row has a control; the notifications widget is **counts by type and nothing else**, with no per-item row and no action. Its footer is a **line of copy, not a control** — approved screen #20 is a *popup*, so a *View all* link would have had nowhere to point, and a dead button is the pattern this project has already rejected twice. The connection-request row is the same reasoning applied once more: it **links to My Network rather than repeating accept and decline**, exactly as the Browse `--incoming` card does.
  **Two lanes of unequal weight, and the small-screen order is not the desktop order.** The wide lane carries Overdue, Pending actions, Urgent solo jobs and Waiting on others; the narrow one carries Upcoming deadlines, Things to clean up, Waiting for your rating and Notifications. Below 1080px the lanes become **`display: contents`** and each widget takes an explicit `order`, so the single column reads Overdue → Pending → Deadlines → Solo jobs → Waiting → Cleanup → Rating → Notifications. That is the point of the trick: without it, one column would have had to be either the desktop lane order or a duplicated DOM, and the priority order is not the same as the lane order. Verified at every measured width rather than assumed.
  **No KPI cards, no charts, no statistics.** The only aggregate numbers on the screen are the per-widget counters and the notification counts by type, both of which are the widget's own subject rather than decoration. The greeting is one line and sits above the first widget, not in place of it.
  **Every widget has its own empty state**, one neutral line each, hidden by **`.wlist:has(> li) ~ .wempty`** — the same mechanism and the same restraint as My Network, so the markup is already correct when React supplies real data. The *view all* footer hides with it, because a link to a full list is pointless when the widget is empty. The solo-jobs filter has a **second, separate** empty line for *"jobs exist but none match this filter"*, which is a different sentence and a different state. **All eight ship populated**, which is the opposite trade from My Network's deliberately-empty Blocked group: this screen exists to be read as a whole, and an empty widget on it would look like a bug rather than a demonstration. The cost is that no empty state is visible without editing the file, and the recipe for checking them is in the handover notes.
  **Urgency is ordering, not a score.** The five solo jobs are sorted by how close each is to its due date, and **no numeric urgency value appears anywhere**, because the product has no such field and inventing one would have created a trust signal nobody defined. The filter is four hidden radios — All, Not started, In progress, Due this week — mapping to `publicStatus` and `endDate` and to nothing new.
  **`:target` was spent deliberately.** [§10](#10-bug--issue-log) records that a screen gets at most one `:target` component, so it went to the **dismiss confirmation**, which is the one place the owner required something to be communicated at action time. The job filter uses radios, as My Network's tabs do. **Zero new JavaScript**, and `lang.js` is loaded — this screen does not repeat the defect §8 records against `register.html` and `browse-contractors.html`.
  **Six filenames reserved and linked, and the cost stated.** `my-tasks.html`, `task-detail.html`, `my-projects.html`, `create-task.html`, `create-project.html` and `reschedule-proposal-review.html` are reserved under their approved screen names, following the practice the 404 screen set — which is why this screen already had a name waiting for it. All six are **dead until those screens are built**, recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) rather than discovered later.
  **Checks, and the four things they do not prove.** Measured from the live DOM in Edge headless across **23 widths from 1440px to 320px × both languages = 46 configurations**, loading the page into an iframe because the window clamps at ~492px: **zero** elements crossing either edge, **zero** intersections between any two widget rectangles, no widget collapsed, and the single-column stack order identical to the intended priority order in all 26 configurations below 1080px. The filter was exercised from the DOM: All 5, Not started 3, In progress 2, Due this week 3, with the filter-empty line correctly hidden in all four. **Every one of the eight empty states was proved** by removing its rows and reading the computed style back — all eight went `none → block`, and all three *view all* links went to `none` with them. Statically: HTML nesting parses clean, 25 ids all unique, **90** `dir="auto"` attributes all on single-language spans per §3.4 rule 7, `he-text` and `en-text` balanced at 189 each, braces balanced, **zero** physical inline-axis properties, **zero** four-value padding shorthands, zero `!important`, and every hex in the file is palette, the `#4A5D6E` gradient mid-stop, or one of the three approved availability dots. A full text-node walk in each language found **one** cross-language string, the `עב` toggle label, which is the existing convention. **What none of it proves: (a)** Google Fonts did not load in headless, so every measurement used fallback metrics and real Heebo and Inter will shift heights slightly; **(b)** geometry is not legibility — contrast, and whether the two-lane hierarchy actually reads as a hierarchy, are owner checks; **(c)** the mock data is representative, not real, so nothing here proves the widgets stay readable at ten rows instead of three; **(d)** the empty states were proved one widget at a time, and **the all-empty new-account screen has never been rendered whole**. **No data-model change** — every widget reads fields and indexes that already exist; the only schema question raised is D18, which is recorded, not implemented.

- `[2026-08-22]` **Second owner review of Browse — connection states, advanced filters and English avatars (`6a50657` on `feature/browse-contractors-screen`)** — the review of the review: it reverses one mechanism chosen deliberately two days earlier, closes the §8 gap that entry left open, and fixes a pinning bug that had been mislabelled as working.
  **(1) The status badge left the corner, because a corner is not a place.** It was `position: absolute; top: 14px; inset-inline-end: 14px`, floating over the identity row, and absolute positioning cannot see the name it is sitting on — at a 285px card the badge box and the name box simply occupied the same coordinates. It is now an ordinary flow item with `align-self: flex-start`, taking **its own row above the avatar and name**, which is the only fix that scales with text length in two languages. `aria-hidden="true"` came off in the same change: the badge used to be decoration beside a button that said the same thing, and it is now the *only* thing on the card that states the relationship, so it has to reach assistive tech. The attribute moved onto the inner `<svg>`.
  **(2) The inbound arrow is gone; both pending states share the clock.** The incoming badge had a bespoke down-arrow-into-a-tray glyph that appears nowhere else in the project. It now uses `<circle r="9"/><path d="M12 7v5l3 2"/>` — the same clock `my-network.css` gives `.wait-note` and the same one the *request sent* badge already carried. **The two pending states therefore share an icon**, which is deliberate: they are the same kind of state, and the distinction is carried by the words (`בקשה נשלחה` vs `ממתין לאישור`) and by the badge tint, not by asking the reader to tell two small glyphs apart. English was retuned to match — *Awaiting your approval* rather than *Awaiting your response*, so it mirrors the Hebrew and stays clearly distinct from *Request sent*.
  **(3) A relationship is a status, never a disabled button.** The *request sent* card carried `<button class="btn--muted" disabled>`, which is precisely the pattern the 2026-08-20 public-profile review rejected on the other screens: a greyed control invites a click that can never succeed. It is deleted, along with the now-dead `.btn--muted` and `.pp__actions-main .btn--muted` rules. The rule is now stated once and holds for all five states — **Connect renders only where there is no relationship at all.** The outgoing card is left with *View profile* alone, which is correct and looks intentional; **no Accept/Decline was added anywhere**, on instruction, because that workflow belongs to My Network and duplicating it would give the same request two homes.
  **(4) `--blocked` completes the five states and closes the §8 finding.** The gap the 2026-08-20 entry recorded against itself is now shut. **One judgement call, recorded rather than smoothed over:** the sample set contained no blocked contractor, so card 9 (Khaled Mansour) was converted into one — otherwise the newly specified rule would have had nothing on the screen to apply to. That is the same trade My Network made when it shipped Blocked empty so the empty state would be genuinely visible, run in the opposite direction, and it is one `<li>` to revert. It also surfaces a question nobody has answered: **whether a blocked contractor should appear in browse results at all** is a product decision, not a CSS one.
  **(5) Advanced filters open beside the rail — `<details>` is gone, and this reverses two days of reasoning.** The 2026-08-20 entry chose `<details>` and argued it well; the argument was about the *mechanism* and never about the *placement*, and placement was the actual complaint. Collapsing the group shortened the rail but expanding it still made the rail taller, so reaching the refining controls still meant scrolling the page — the original problem, moved rather than solved. The four fields now live in an `.adv-panel` that is a **sibling of `.filters__card`, absolutely positioned at `inset-inline-start: calc(100% + 18px)`**, so it opens *next to* the rail and contributes nothing to its height; its own body scrolls internally, and below 860px it becomes a centred `position: fixed` sheet with a tap-to-close scrim. Because the fields now sit outside the `<form>`, each carries **`form="filters-form"`** — standard HTML5 form association, no JavaScript. **Still zero new JavaScript, and still no second `:target`:** the opener is a hidden checkbox, so the [§10](#10-bug--issue-log) rule that a screen gets at most one `:target` component is intact, and the profile panel keeps it.
  **The accessibility cost of (5), stated plainly.** The 2026-08-20 entry praised `<details>` for being *"keyboard-operable and correctly announced without a single ARIA attribute, which a radio-driven disclosure is not"* — and that advantage is genuinely lost. The checkbox is focusable and labelled, so it is operable and it has an accessible name, but it announces as **a checkbox, not as an expandable region**, and `aria-expanded` cannot be kept in sync without JavaScript. `aria-controls` is set as a partial mitigation. Keeping `<details>` was investigated and does not survive the new layout: its body must be a descendant of the element, so it would be clipped by the scrolling field list it now has to escape. **This is a real regression, accepted knowingly for the layout, and it disappears in React** where the trigger becomes a real `<button aria-expanded>`. Recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks).
  **The pinned rail was genuinely broken, and it had the same root cause.** `.filters` has carried `position: sticky` since `97f3331` and the owner was right that it scrolls away: **an element taller than the viewport has nothing left to stick with**, and the expanded group made the rail ~1,400px tall. So the two complaints — *"I still have to scroll for advanced filters"* and *"the rail scrolls away"* — were one bug, and moving the fields out fixed both. The rail is now capped at `calc(100vh - var(--nav-h) - 44px)` and the card is a flex column with a fixed head and a new **`.filters__foot`** holding Apply, so only the field list scrolls and the submit button is always in view. The corner registration ticks moved from `.filters__card` to `.filters` in the same change, or they would have scrolled away with the content they exist to frame. Both traps are in [§10](#10-bug--issue-log).
  **(6) English avatars — the same defect the file already knew how to fix.** All nine cards and the profile panel hard-coded Hebrew initials as bare text, so they stayed Hebrew in English mode. They now use the `he-text` / `en-text` pair exactly as `my-network.html` does, and the three people who appear on both screens (Dana Levi, Musa Khoury, Tamar Shapira) keep the initials My Network already gives them, so nothing contradicts across the pair.
  **One defect found by the render that was not on the review list.** With avatars fixed, an English screenshot still showed a Hebrew search placeholder — `שם או חברה` — because that field had never been language-duplicated. Split into the `he-field` / `en-field` pair the city filter already uses. Then every visible text node, `placeholder`, `value`, `title` and `aria-label` was walked in English mode: what remains is the `עב` language-switch label and three deliberately bilingual `aria-label`s, both existing conventions. **This is the same class of defect as `ede1c52`'s missing `.he-field` rules** — language duplication keeps being added correctly in one place and forgotten in the one beside it.
  **Checks, and the four things they do not prove.** A browser exists on the machine now, so unlike 2026-08-20 this review has real rendering. Sixteen configurations were measured — Hebrew and English × panel open and closed × 1440/1280/900/700/640/620px × 900/800/680px heights, plus scrolled states — asserting from the live DOM rather than by eye: **zero** badge–name, badge–avatar or badge–metadata rect intersections at any width down to 285px cards, including the longest English string; every card with a state modifier carries no Connect and every card without one carries exactly one; the rail pins at exactly `top: 88px` after scrolling; the page's scroll height is identical whether the panel is open or closed. Tag nesting parses clean on the HTML and every `id` is unique. **What none of it proves: (a)** Google Fonts did not load in headless, so every measurement used fallback metrics and real Heebo/Inter will shift heights slightly — which decides whether the field list shows an inner scrollbar at a given window size, and nothing else; **(b)** `:target` does not fire under `--dump-dom`, confirmed identical on the pre-review file, so **the advanced panel and the profile panel have never been seen open at the same time**; **(c)** the ≤860px sheet has measurements but no screenshot — the capture timed out repeatedly — so its scrim contrast is unverified; **(d)** geometry is not legibility, and contrast and the focus ring against the dark active trigger remain owner checks. The pre-existing navbar overflow between ~630px and 700px was re-measured, confirmed byte-identical on the pre-review file, and left alone. **No data-model change**, so `docs/database-design.html` is untouched: a blocked card is a `connections` row with `status: 'blocked'`, which the schema has always carried.

- `[2026-08-20]` **Owner review of Browse and My Network — five approved requirements applied (`ede1c52` on `feature/browse-contractors-screen`, `df04f9f` on `feature/my-network`)** — two screens, two branches, one review; the changes are recorded together because three of the five only make sense as a pair.
  **(1) The filter rail is now Basic + Advanced.** The owner's complaint was concrete: even on a wide desktop the rail was long enough to scroll. Search, specialty, region and availability stay visible — they answer *who does this trade, roughly where, and can they take work on*, which is the whole question a directory exists to answer — and city, travel distance, minimum rating and minimum flexibility move into a collapsed group, because every one of them refines an answer the basic four already gave. **No filter was added, removed or redefined**, on instruction. The mechanism is a **native `<details>`**, not the hidden-radio trick the tabs on My Network use and not `:target`: `:target` is already claimed by the public-profile panel on this very screen, and [§10](#10-bug--issue-log) records the rule that a screen gets at most one `:target` component. `<details>` also costs nothing in accessibility — it is keyboard-operable and correctly announced without a single ARIA attribute, which a radio-driven disclosure is not. **Zero new JavaScript**; the flexibility slider's existing script still binds by id and is unaffected by being inside a collapsed group.
  **One thing this split does cross.** The 2026-08-19 entry grouped city and travel *with* region deliberately, so there would be one geographic block. That block now spans the fold: region above, its two refinements below. They are kept **first inside the advanced group**, immediately under the divider, so the progression region → city → travel still reads in order — but the tension is real and is recorded rather than smoothed over.
  **(2) A fourth card state, `--incoming`, for a request the contractor sent *the viewer*.** This closes half of the [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) finding raised on the day My Network was built: the card rendered *Connect*, which invited a request the unique `pair` index must reject. Approved wording is **`ממתין לאישור`**. It is deliberately **a status, not a workflow** — accept and decline live on My Network and are not duplicated here, so the action slot carries a link to that screen instead of a second pair of buttons. The existing *request sent* state was left alone on instruction. **The blocked card state is still missing** and §8 now says so specifically.
  **The mock person was changed to make the two screens agree.** Card 5 was Ronit Segev, who exists nowhere else; she is now **Tamar Shapira**, the first row of My Network's *requests received*, with the same trade, city, rating and flexibility she already had there. This is the same discipline the My Network entry describes — anyone appearing on both screens must appear in the same state — applied in the other direction. The navbar's *Network* link changed from `#` to `my-network.html` in the same commit, because a card that navigates somewhere the navbar will not is a defect. **Both links are dead until the two branches reach `develop`**, which is stated here so it is a known cost rather than a repeat of the `public-profile.html` mistake.
  **(3) Availability wording is gender-neutral in Hebrew.** `מקבל עבודות חדשות` and `לא מקבל עבודות חדשות כעת` are masculine verbs, so the interface required different copy depending on the contractor's gender — a defect the English strings hid completely, since English does not inflect here. The three states now read `זמינות לעבודות חדשות` · `זמינות מוגבלת לעבודות חדשות` · `אין זמינות לקבלת עבודות חדשות כעת`, a noun ladder rather than a verb, and the middle one needed no change because it was already built that way. **The filter legend had to change too** — it read `זמינות לעבודות חדשות`, which is now the open state's own label, so it became simply `זמינות`; leaving both would have stacked the same string twice inside one fieldset. English closed became *No availability for new work at the moment* so the English ladder mirrors the Hebrew. The unavailable wording is **approved for the prototype and explicitly not final**. Recorded against [D14](#7-open-decisions), since whatever shape that decision picks must render neutral labels.
  **(4) Static empty states on all four My Network groups.** One neutral line each, and nothing else: no illustration, no icon, no explanatory paragraph, no CTA — the owner's constraint, and the right one, because an empty group is not an opportunity to sell anything. Each line is scoped to its own group and hidden by **`.net-list:has(.net-row) + .net-empty`**, so the markup is already correct when React supplies real data instead of being a mock that has to be deleted. **Blocked ships with no rows** so that one empty state is genuinely visible on the screen rather than only reachable by editing the file; the cost, accepted knowingly, is that the screen no longer demonstrates Unblock — the control the My Network entry above calls a deliberate new placement.
  **(5) Card metadata typography aligned to My Network, not the reverse.** The two screens disagreed on every equivalent value: name 16 vs 17px, location row 13 vs 14px, flexibility label 12.5 vs 14px. My Network's numbers won because it was built *after* [§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen) was written and its CSS cites rule 3 directly, while 13px and 12.5px are exactly the micro-text that rule exists to stop — the browse stylesheet had already been corrected once for the same defect on 2026-08-19 and these three values were missed. The connection badge went 11 → 12.5px for the same reason. **`text-overflow: ellipsis` was dropped from the company name**, which now wraps as it does on My Network: a clipped company name is unreadable, and rule 5 forbids a width limit tighter than the content needs.
  **Checks, and one that could not run.** Tag-nesting parsed on both HTML files (zero unclosed, zero mismatched), brace balance on both stylesheets, the basic/advanced split verified by parsing the form rather than by eye, one empty line confirmed per panel, and grep counts on all twelve replaced strings. **No screenshots were taken — no browser is installed on the machine — so there is no visual verification at any width in either language**, and that is the honest limit of everything above: it is static analysis, and it proves structure, not that the screen reads well. A re-indent pass deleted a closing `</div>` on the flexibility filter group during the work; the nesting parser caught it, which is the argument for running it. **No data-model change**, so `docs/database-design.html` is untouched: an incoming request is a `connections` row with the viewer as `recipient`, which the schema has always carried.
- `[2026-08-20]` **My network built as a static screen on a new `feature/my-network` branch (`1f35f21`, `1660f4c`)** — approved screen **#10**, the last `Planned` row in the Stage-2 identity block, and the screen that makes the §1 connection gate visible instead of merely asserted.
  **One collection, four groups.** Everything on the screen is `connections` read four ways: `status: 'pending'` where the viewer is `recipient` (requests received), `status: 'accepted'` (connections), `status: 'pending'` where the viewer is `requester` (requests sent), and `status: 'blocked'`. That is exactly the split the schema already anticipated — the two indexes `{ recipient: 1, status: 1 }` and `{ requester: 1, status: 1 }` name *"My network — pending requests received / sent"* as the queries they exist to serve. The screen adds no field and asks for none.
  ~~**The gate is shown, not described.** Message exists **only** on accepted rows. On a pending or blocked row it is **absent, not disabled** — the standing rule from the 2026-08-20 public-profile review, where a greyed control was rejected because it invites a click that can never succeed. Each group also carries a one-line lede saying what the gate does in that state, so *request → accept → then message* is legible without reading the marketing copy in §1.~~ ***(Superseded 2026-08-29 — the gate was reversed by [D9](#7-open-decisions), findings 2026-08-28.)*** The *absent, not disabled* rule and the four groups stand; what does not is the framing. **There is no connection gate to show**, so `request → accept → then message` is no longer the sequence the screen exists to make legible, and the group ledes may speak only about the **network edge**. This is documentation catching up with a reversal, not a screen redesign — and see the following paragraph, which is wrong for a second reason.
  **Tabs are hidden radios; the dialog keeps `:target`.** Both were going to use `:target` and they cannot: a document has one target at a time, so opening the removal dialog would have collapsed the page back to the default tab behind the overlay. Radios suit a small one-of-N selection — the same reasoning the public-profile note used when it chose `:target` over radios for a one-of-nine — and `:target` stays with the modal, which needs no input per row. Logged in [§10](#10-bug--issue-log) with the rule: **at most one `:target` component per screen.**
  **Removal is confirmed by one generic dialog, not one per row.** This follows the correction the owner already made once: static HTML/CSS cannot put the clicked person's name into the dialog, and nine copies of it would only pretend otherwise. Unlike the public-profile panel, a confirmation needs no identity to be a valid confirmation, so this one carries **no name at all** rather than representative mock data that could contradict the row that opened it. Under React it becomes `<ConfirmDialog name={…} />`.
  **What the dialog deliberately does not say.** ~~It states that the person leaves your network and that direct messaging closes — the gate, which *is* defined.~~ ***(Superseded 2026-08-29.)*** **The "messaging closes" half is now wrong twice over.** Once because [D9](#7-open-decisions) reversed the gate, so removing a connection closes nothing — and again because what a disconnect does to an **existing thread** is precisely D9's **still-open** half, which is the very thing this paragraph already knew it could not claim. **The dialog should therefore say nothing at all about messaging** and speak only to the network edge: the person leaves your network. Its own caution below was already half-right; it simply did not go far enough. It still says nothing about the existing conversation, because [D9](#7-open-decisions) has not decided what happens to a thread on disconnect, and nothing about reconnecting later, because that depends on [D17](#7-open-decisions). An earlier draft promised *"you can send a new connection request later"* and it was cut: whether that is true depends entirely on whether removal deletes the row or occupies the unique `pair` index with a tombstone.
  **New open decision [D17](#7-open-decisions) — how a connection edge is torn down.** Remove, cancel and decline are three controls with no state behind them: `connections.status` has no `removed` and no `withdrawn`. Flagged rather than decided, and **the controls ship anyway**, per the standing rule that a gap in the model is not a reason to drop a control from a prototype. The decision also surfaced something previously unstated: because `declined` is kept *"so the same person cannot re-request in a loop"*, **declining is already irreversible** and no screen warns about it.
  **Blocked added as a fourth group — a placement, not a new capability.** `connections.status` has carried `'blocked'` since the collection was designed, and the public profile has been able to set it since 2026-08-20, but **no approved screen could undo it**. My Network is the connections screen, so Unblock lives here. Flagged and approved rather than assumed — the same shape as the Block finding itself, which was missing from the requirements rather than from the model. Blocked rows deliberately show **no trust metrics and no profile link**: what a blocked pair may see of each other is undefined, so the row shows identity, trade and the block date, and nothing that would need a rule to justify.
  **Cancel request added to sent rows**, also undocumented — approved screen #10 names *"pending requests sent"* with no verb — and folded into D17 rather than given a quiet answer of its own.
  **Deleting a chat is not on this screen, and that is the finding.** It was raised as an existing capability; the repository does not contain it. Screen #19 lists *mute-conversation* only, [[threads]] has no delete or hide field, and D9 never mentions a user-initiated delete. Recorded in [§8](#8-known-issues--risks) with the questions it has to answer; **nothing was invented on My Network**, since the instruction was explicitly not to place it without a definition.
  **Mock data is consistent with Browse rather than merely plausible.** Only two people appear on both screens, and both agree: Dana Levi is *Connected* on her browse card and sits in Connections here; Musa Khoury is *Pending* there and in Requests sent here, with the same city, rating and flexibility. Everyone else is a new person, because a browse result is a filtered directory and need not contain the viewer's whole network — whereas anyone on both screens in two different states would be the contradiction the 2026-08-20 review already rejected once. Checking this produced the §8 finding that **a browse card has no state for "this person sent you a request"**, which would render as *Connect* and invite a request the unique `pair` index must reject.
  **Reserved filename:** `inbox.html` = Inbox / messages (#19). Message was first built as a dead `<button>` and became a real link (`1660f4c`), matching the practice the 404 screen set — reserve the approved screen's own name so the branch that builds it cannot pick a different one. `View profile` points at `browse-contractors.html#profile`, because the public profile is a **feature inside Browse** and has no route of its own.
  **Checks.** No new colors — every hex and `rgba()` base extracted from both files: six hexes, all palette (`#4A5D6E` is the mid-stop of the approved `--brand-gradient`), five rgba bases, all palette-derived. **Zero** physical inline-axis properties and **zero** four-value padding shorthands, both by grep — the second check is the one that caught the copied navbar paddings. `dir="auto"` appears 36 times, all on single-language spans, per §3.4 rule 7. `lang.js` is loaded, so this screen does not repeat the defect §8 records against `register.html` and `browse-contractors.html`. Rendered in Edge headless across **18 widths from 1440px to 320px × both languages × all four tabs**: no element crosses either edge. Two things that check does **not** prove: it measures geometry, not whether the layout reads well, and it says nothing about contrast — both are owner checks.
- `[2026-08-20]` **Duplicate decision number corrected: two different decisions were both called `D13`** — a documentation defect, but a load-bearing one, since §6, §11 and `docs/database-design.html` all cross-reference these numbers and a reader following `D13` had no way to know which of the two rows was meant. *Completed-work storage shape* (opened 2026-08-18) **keeps D13**: it claimed the number first, and its own decision-log entry literally reads "new **D13**", which is dated history and should not be rewritten. *Phone visibility on the public profile* (opened 2026-08-20) **becomes D15** — D14 was already taken by the availability / heavy-equipment row. **Every reference was repointed in the same commit, not just the row heading:** three in this document (the §6 Public profile note, and two inside the 2026-08-20 decision-log entries) and **three in `docs/database-design.html`** (twice in the `users.phone` field note, once in the public-profile projection rule). That last file is the reason this mattered more than tidiness — the schema doc was pointing readers at the completed-work decision when it meant phone visibility. Each renumbered location carries a short inline note saying it was opened as `D13`, so the history stays readable rather than looking as though D15 existed all along. **§7 was also sorted into ascending order** (it ran D11 → D14 → D13 → D12 → D13), which is a pure row move: no wording in any decision changed.

- `[2026-08-20]` **404 Not Found built as a standalone static screen on a new `feature/error-screens` branch (`afff980`)** — approved screen **#28**, and the first row in §6 that had been carrying a completely empty Notes cell.
  **Standalone, with no `.app-nav`.** An unmatched route is reachable both signed-in and signed-out, and static HTML/CSS cannot branch on session. Reusing the app navbar would show a logged-out visitor a notifications bell and a profile chip reading someone's name; a second reduced navbar variant would mean two navbars to keep in step. So the screen takes the login/register treatment instead — blueprint board, one centred cream card, the language pill pinned clear of both frames — and the branch is named for error screens generally because 403 / 500 are the obvious siblings.
  **The copy is neutral because [D16](#7-open-decisions) is open.** *This page isn't available* — never "you don't have permission", never "this page never existed", and never anything that separates *missing* from *forbidden*. The three possible reasons are phrased as possibilities and none of them claims to be the true one. This is not stylistic: if the server ever answers 404 for permission failures, the response code is the only thing keeping a GC from detecting a delegate's task by probing URLs, and copy that says "this doesn't exist" would undo it in the one place the user actually reads. Building the copy neutral now means the screen needs no rewrite whichever way D16 closes.
  **No technical routing information is displayed** — no failed URL, no pathname, no route or debug data. An earlier draft showed the requested address in a mono chip as a demonstration of §3.4 rule 5 bidi isolation; it was dropped on the owner's instruction, because an error screen is not a debugging surface.
  **One recovery action.** *Back to Home* → `personal-dashboard.html`, the Personal dashboard (approved screen #5). **Corrected on the day (`901c3e1`) from `dashboard.html`, at the owner's instruction** — the approved list names screen #5 *Personal dashboard*, and `dashboard.html` was a shortening on the reasoning that it is also the home route. That was the wrong call: #13 is *Project dashboard*, so a bare `dashboard.html` leaves a reader guessing which of the two it is, and the reserved name should match the approved screen name rather than its role. No secondary CTA and no link row added to fill the layout, and **the FieldSync logo is deliberately not a link** so there is exactly one recovery path rather than two that happen to agree. There is **no "go back" control** because `history.back()` is JavaScript; flagged in §8 rather than faked.
  **Filenames reserved for screens not yet built:** `personal-dashboard.html` (#5 Personal dashboard) and `project-dashboard.html` (#13 Project dashboard), recorded so the branch that builds them does not pick a different name and silently break this link. Both reserved names now match their approved screen names exactly, which is the rule to follow for every remaining screen. The stylesheet is **`error.css`, not `404.css`**, so a later 403 or 500 reuses it instead of forking a seventh copy of the palette.
  **No new colors** — verified by extracting every hex and `rgba()` triplet from both files: six hexes, all palette (`#4A5D6E` is the mid-stop of the approved `--brand-gradient`), and five rgba bases, all palette-derived. Logical properties only, verified by grep: **zero** physical `left`/`right`. `dir="auto"` sits on single-language spans only, per §3.4 rule 7.
  **One visual fix worth recording.** The large `404` was first set in the display serif, which is what the headline uses. It renders solidly in Hebrew (Frank Ruhl Libre) but badly in English: Fraunces is a high-contrast face whose `4` is drawn with strokes that deliberately do not meet, so at ~112px it reads as a damaged glyph rather than a numeral, and raising the weight to 700 only made the break more obvious. The numeral moved to `--font-sans`, where Heebo and Inter draw near-identical digits — so the most prominent element on the screen now looks the same in both languages, which for a bilingual product matters more than matching the headline's serif. Checked by rendering both languages at 1280px and at true 390px / 360px viewports.
- `[2026-08-20]` **Owner review of the public profile — four corrections applied (`97f3331`); supersedes parts of the two entries below** — the panel shipped that morning was reviewed the same day and four things came back. **(1) Nine duplicated panels replaced by one generic panel.** Building a panel per card was the wrong trade: it made every state clickable, but at the cost of nine copies of the same markup, and it quietly implied the prototype could do something it cannot. The owner's instruction is the honest framing — static HTML/CSS **cannot** inject the clicked contractor's data, so the prototype should carry **one generic profile of "the currently selected contractor"** with representative mock data, and the per-contractor binding is deferred to React, where this becomes `<PublicProfile />` taking the selection as props. All nine `View profile` controls open that one panel. The file drops from 2,388 lines back to 1,243. **A separate `public-profile.html` was considered and rejected by the owner**: pure HTML/CSS has no include mechanism, so a second file could only be embedded through an `<iframe>` — and the CSS-only language toggle cannot cross a document boundary, which would leave the profile stuck in one language and break §3.4. The side-panel UX stays inside Browse until the React refactor. **(2) The filter rail now stays on the side at every width.** The previous ladder moved it to a full-width row below 1180px; that was never asked for and it made opening a profile rearrange the whole page. What gives way instead is the profile, which drops **beneath** the results column below 1080px — still taking real layout space, still never overlaying the cards. The 1080px figure is measured rather than guessed: 268px rail + 22px gap + a 280px card + 22px gap + a 380px panel + 44px of page padding needs about 996px of viewport. **(3) Cards become a single column when the profile is open**, one under another, instead of the 2-up grid the first version produced — a single column keeps each card wide enough to stay readable in the narrowed results column. The intended desktop layout is now exactly **rail | one-column results | profile**. **(4) Cold start made consistent rather than demonstrated.** The first version gave Yossi Mizrahi a `—` on his card so the empty state would be visible somewhere; with one generic panel a card can no longer have a matching per-contractor profile, so that card goes back to its real score and **no mock data contradicts itself**. The `—` presentation is now implemented once — the same `.state-empty` mark and the same mechanism on a card and in the panel, differing only in type size — so real data cannot render the empty state two ways. **Also reopened: phone visibility**, recorded here as **[D15](#7-open-decisions)** rather than left as an approved rule; see the correction inside the entry below. (Opened on the day as `D13`; **renumbered to D15 on 2026-08-20** because that number was already taken.)

- `[2026-08-20]` **Public profile built as a side panel inside Browse Contractors (`b07fd30`, on `feature/browse-contractors-screen`)** — **(a) Classified as a feature, not a screen or a route.** §6 had drifted into calling it "a screen with its own route, not a feature of My profile"; that wording is corrected and the original registry note — *public profile = feature, not standalone route* — stands. §6's own preamble already allows for this: the approved list of 36 is a list of screens, but "many are popups/components, not routes," and item 7 is one of them. It gets no file, no route and no navigation of its own. The owner's layout requirement is what settles it: the profile opens *within* Browse, does not replace it, and does not cover the contractor cards — because the value of a directory is comparing a candidate against the list you found them in, and a full-page navigation destroys exactly that. So the panel is the **third column of `.browse__body`** (`268px minmax(0, 1fr) 380px`), and opening it narrows the card grid from **3-up to 2-up** rather than overlaying it. `minmax(0, 1fr)` on the results column is load-bearing — a plain `1fr` refuses to shrink below its content, so the grid would overflow instead of reflowing. **(b) Selection is CSS-only via `:target`, not the usual hidden-radio trick.** The panel represents *whichever* contractor was clicked, so this is a one-of-nine selection: radios would need ten inputs and about twenty selectors, while `:target` needs two rules — `.profile-panel:target` to reveal, `.browse__body:has(.profile-panel:target)` to switch the grid. `View profile` becomes a real `<a href="#pp-…">`, keyboard-accessible with no ARIA scaffolding, and `:has()` was already in use here. **Zero new JavaScript**; the existing flexibility-slider script is untouched. **(c) Nine panels, one per card**, because a single shared panel would show the same person no matter which card was clicked. The cost is ~1,330 lines of duplicated bilingual markup — the exact debt §8 already records, and the exact thing the React migration collapses into one component plus props. It buys something real in the meantime: **every state is genuinely reachable instead of faked** — Connect / Pending / Message follow each card's connection state, and Rate appears only where the viewer is eligible. **(d) Presentation is copied from the profile screens, not reinvented.** Same metric tiles, `work-item` cards, `facts` lists and anonymous `review-list`, so a contractor sees the same profile whether it is theirs or someone else's; only the copy moves from second person to third. **(e) Nine dead links fixed on the way** — every `View profile` pointed at `public-profile.html`, a file that has never existed. **(f) Rate is gated by shared completed work, not by connection** — `ratings` is unique on `{ rater, ratee, context.task }`, so eligibility comes from a shared completed task and nothing else. The panel proves the two are independent: Dana Levi is connected *and* eligible, Samir Abdallah is eligible *without* being connected. Where the viewer is not eligible the control is **absent, not disabled** — a greyed button invites a click that can never succeed. The interactive task picker is Stage 2. **(g) Flexibility cold start: a neutral `—`, no explanation** (D6 presentation, settled here). Yossi Mizrahi has no score, and **his browse card was changed to match** — a card showing `55` beside a panel showing `—` would be a worse bug than either state alone. **(h) Block added to the action row.** It was missing from the item-7 requirements, but `connections.status` already carries `'blocked'` as a first-class state, so the absence was a documentation gap rather than a product decision — flagged and approved rather than assumed either way. Report and Mute qualify under the standing 2026-08-13 rule that a `Planned` feature may appear on a screen while a `Stretch` one may not. (**Phone visibility was recorded here as `D13` and renumbered to [D15](#7-open-decisions) on 2026-08-20**, because `D13` was already in use.) **(i) Responsive ladder, three stages:** rail + cards + panel above 1180px; below it the filter rail moves to a full-width row so cards and panel stay side by side; below 860px everything stacks in the order filters → profile → results, so the page keeps its identity. The panel's rules sit **last in the stylesheet on purpose** — `:has()` outranks the plain `.browse__body` rules in the earlier Responsive section, so the panel's own media queries have to come after them to win. **No new colors**; logical properties throughout, re-checked against the 2026-08-03 four-value-shorthand trap.
- `[2026-08-20]` **Three public-profile visibility rules decided and written into `docs/database-design.html`** (golden rule 3) — all three are read-side serializer rules; no field changed shape. **(1) Public work shows trade, city, month/year, role, completed status and permitted images — never a project name, a client name, other members, or any mark distinguishing delegated work.** §3.3 makes this load-bearing rather than cosmetic: a named project plus a date is often enough to identify the GC to anyone in the trade, and the delegation guarantee dies the moment a public profile lets an outsider reconstruct who worked for whom. Building the constraint into what is *rendered* means nothing identifying can leak, because nothing identifying is sent. **(2) Rater anonymity extends to every non-admin viewer, not only the ratee.** The 2026-08-18 decision withheld `rater` and `context` from the person being rated; it said nothing about a third party. That gap is self-defeating — a contractor could simply open their own profile from the browse list and read the names — so the rule now belongs to the public-profile projection as a whole. **(3) `phone` is withheld until a connection is accepted.** ⚠️ **Superseded the same day — see the correction entry above. This is now [D15](#7-open-decisions), open, not a rule** (opened as `D13`, renumbered to D15 on 2026-08-20 because that number was already taken). The reasoning below is kept because it is the argument *for* gating, and D15 still has to weigh it: §1's connection gate exists to stop the platform becoming a sales-spam channel, and a phone number printed on every public profile makes that gate decorative. The panel still shows the field, so a viewer can see what a connection would give them; messaging stays the channel of record.
- `[2026-08-19]` **Faint secondary text darkened project-wide, and the "before joining" label dropped from work cards** (2 commits on `feature/my-profile-screen`, 1 each on `feature/browse-contractors-screen`, `feature/register-screen`, `feature/login-screen`) — readability only; no hierarchy, layout, spacing or content changed beyond it, and the hexagon completion badge is untouched.
  **Measured rather than eyeballed.** The two faint text tokens were **`rgba(31,36,40,0.40)` at 2.40:1** and **`0.55` at 3.62:1** against the cream surfaces — both under the 4.5:1 AA floor for normal text, which is why dates like *July 2026* were uncomfortable to read. Raised to **0.64 (≈4.6:1)** and **0.75 (≈6.8:1)**, verified against both `--surface` and the slightly darker `--background`. Renamed to **`--text-mute`** and **`--text-soft`** because the old names stated opacities that no longer applied. Applied to **every** stylesheet carrying them — profile (×3), browse-contractors, register, login — since the tokens are duplicated per screen and a fix in one would have left the others faint.
  **One tier was lost, deliberately.** The old scale had four steps; the bottom two both failed contrast. Lifting them over the floor left no room for a third secondary step, so the former 0.55 and 0.75 tiers merged into `--text-soft`. Metadata stays visibly lighter than secondary body copy, which is the distinction that carries meaning; the finer 0.55-vs-0.75 split did not survive and could not. Recorded as **[§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen) rule 4** so the next screen inherits the two-tier scale instead of reinventing a faint one.
  **Label removed.** *Completed before joining / עבודה מלפני ההצטרפות* is gone from the work cards — it labelled an absence rather than telling the reader anything about the work. Location and date stay as their own `.work-item__meta` line, so the context survives without the label. **Left in place:** the same phrase inside the Edit Profile section lede, where it is prose telling a contractor that pre-platform work may be uploaded, not a card label. Flagged rather than removed silently.

- `[2026-08-19]` **Badge finalised as a hexagon mark, the metrics pair moved into the Edit Profile header, and the divider tightened** (3 commits on `feature/my-profile-screen`) — presentation only; no eligibility, privacy or badge **rule** changed, and the badge still asserts recorded completion rather than quality.
  **Badge final design.** The tick became a **hexagon enclosing a check**, drawn in `--secondary` — the same accent as the rating stars — on a `--secondary-15` field with a full-strength tan border. The solid tan fill and its inset shadow are gone, so the mark reads as the accent and the label stays on `--primary`. **Location and date are now separate metadata**, not folded into the title: the title carries the work (`מגדלי הצפון` / Northern Towers), and a new mono `.work-item__meta` line carries `city · month year`. Previously the location was buried inside the title string, which left the tile with no date at all.
  **Edit Profile's metrics moved to its header**, beside the identity, matching the view screen. This also **settles a mismatch flagged earlier the same day**: the two screens had drifted apart, with the view header holding the pair and the edit header not. Because the shared rule was written as `.trust:has(.trust__metrics)` and `.trust > .trust__metrics` rather than hard-coded, the edit header picked up the two-column arrangement automatically with no CSS change — which is the payoff for scoping it structurally instead of duplicating it. Confirmed by locating each file's trust-section byte range and checking the metrics element falls inside it: **both true**. The ratings heading went back to *Ratings you received* on both, since the score no longer sits in that section.
  **Divider tightened and darkened.** `.trust__metrics` had `padding-block-start: 22px` above a `--secondary-45` dash, which read as a large empty band. Now **12px** above a **`--primary-20`** dash — a derived palette value, no new hue — so it is more visible while staying subtle, with the inline variant at ≥1000px matched (`22px`, same dash). Card design and content untouched.

- `[2026-08-19]` **My Profile view: the rating and flexibility pair returned to the header, and the section rhythm evened out** (`38c4549` on `feature/my-profile-screen`) — view screen only; `edit-profile.html` was not modified, and its metrics stay in its ratings section.
  **Metrics back beside the identity.** The pair moved back inside `.trust`, together with the flexibility disclosure, so both computed signals read as part of the profile header. Styling, labels, content and logic are untouched — only the position.
  **The two-column header rule went into the shared `profile.css`, but scoped structurally** rather than duplicated into the view stylesheet: `.trust:has(.trust__metrics)` and `.trust > .trust__metrics`. That matters because the two screens currently differ — the view header holds the pair, the edit header does not — and an unscoped rule would have turned Edit Profile's header into a two-column grid with an empty second column, squeezing its identity block to nine-tenths width. Scoped this way the shared sheet carries one rule that applies wherever a header actually holds the metrics, and Edit Profile renders identically to before. Verified by locating the trust section's byte range in each file and checking whether the metrics element falls inside it.
  **One spacing rhythm.** The gap between About me and Completed Work was not a margin at all — the screen had **two stacked grids**, and row two could not begin until the taller column of row one finished, so a short About panel left a gap the height of the Details panel beneath it. Merged back to **one** grid with two flex columns: About me and Completed Work in the wide column, details and scheduling preferences in the narrow one. Every gap between major sections is now the same **22px** — `.trust` bottom margin, the column `gap`, the grid gap and the grid's bottom margin all agree — and Completed Work still sits directly beneath About me with scheduling alongside.
  **Ratings heading restored** to *Ratings you received*; with flexibility no longer in that section, *Ratings and flexibility* would have described content that is not there.

- `[2026-08-19]` **My Profile view: Completed Work paired with Scheduling preferences, Ratings moved last** (`09fde8f` on `feature/my-profile-screen`) — order only, on the **view screen alone**; `edit-profile.html` was not touched. New reading order: **About me → Completed Work + Scheduling preferences side by side → Ratings last.**
  **How, without inventing a layout.** The screen already had one two-column `.profile-grid`; it now has **two**, stacked. Row one is About me beside the structured details, row two is Completed Work beside Scheduling preferences. Because Completed Work occupies the same (wider, `1.4fr`) column as About me, it sits **directly beneath it** as required, and because each row is its own grid the two panels in row two genuinely align rather than drifting apart at differing column heights. Ratings and flexibility follow as a full-width section, so it is the last content on the page.
  **Nothing else changed** — verified by diffing the file line-multiset against the previous commit: the only additions are the second grid's three wrapper elements, and the only removals are two source comments that described the old column split and would now have been wrong. No styling, sizing, content or logic was altered, and the Ratings/Flexibility pair keeps the composition restored earlier today.
  **Mobile is unaffected by the change:** `.profile-grid` already collapses to a single column at 960px, so the stack reads About me → details → Completed Work → Scheduling → Ratings, with Ratings still last.

- `[2026-08-19]` **Profile refinement round two: three project-wide UI rules established, Completed work finished, availability and heavy-equipment pickers added, and Browse gained precise geography** (12 commits on `feature/my-profile-screen`, 5 on `feature/browse-contractors-screen`, 2 on `feature/register-screen`, 1 on `feature/login-screen`) — commits were kept **branch-specific and split by action** at the owner's instruction; no change from one screen was bundled into another screen's branch.
  **Three regressions were found on more than one screen, so they became rules rather than fixes** — new **[§3.5](#35-shared-ui-rules-apply-to-every-screen)**. **(1)** The "placeholder jumps on focus" report was **not** a floating label — there is no floating-label pattern in the project. It was `transform: translateY(-1px)` on `:hover`/`:focus` lifting the whole field. **(2)** Labels had drifted back into micro-text on three screens because each screen sets its own sizes, so the Register correction from 2026-06-28 never propagated; **Register at 14px is now named as the reference number** so the next screen copies a value instead of re-making a judgement. **(3)** Short copy was held to a reading measure it did not need, so ledes and hints wrapped early; `--prose-max` now applies to genuine prose only. Both regressions are logged in [§10](#10-bug--issue-log). The common root cause of (2) is duplicated per-screen CSS, which dissolves when the shared layer lands.

Checked: the only FAQ in the project is on the subscriptions screen, and it explains neither — it lists them as plan capabilities. The `זה מה שמסנן אתכם בחיפוש` clauses are gone: telling a contractor how the search index works is not their concern. **Every sidenote that named confidential delegation was deleted** — the platform must not disclose that the feature exists, so an explanatory note about it was itself the leak.
  **Empty states unified.** Flexibility with too little history and ratings with none now show the **same neutral `—`**, with no explanatory paragraph beside either. Previously the cold start carried its own sentence, which both duplicated the explanation and drew attention to an absence.
  **Completed work finished.** It is now the **last content section** on both screens, so profile information is never interrupted by images, and on Edit Profile the Save / Cancel bar follows it as the page's final action. The removal **X** stays on editable images even though it does nothing yet, deliberately: no temporary JavaScript was invented to make it work.

That choice was made specifically to avoid the contrast problem an amber *text* colour would have had on the cream card.

Added a **city / town** filter backed by a datalist of Israeli settlements and a **minimum travel distance** filter, both grouped with the region control so the rail has one geographic block rather than three competing ones. A regional search must still surface nationwide contractors.
